- Tags: ui, quickslots, targeting, combat-feel, source-fidelity, tests
- Added a player-first quickslot targeting pass. Quickslot wand use now goes through a new `EventBus.enter_targeting_auto` signal: `GameScene` routes it into `TargetingCoordinator.enter(..., auto_resolve=true)`, which immediately fires at the last valid target if still visible/in range, otherwise the nearest visible hostile, and falls back to manual targeting when no target is clear. Manual inventory targeting is unchanged. `TargetingCoordinator.resolve` now records `_last_target_pos` so repeat quickslot use keeps pressure on the same enemy, matching the fast SPD quickslot feel without changing item-window behavior. Added coverage in `test_targeting_input.gd` for nearest-target auto-resolve, last-target preference, manual fallback, and last-target recording; pinned the new EventBus signal in `test_event_bus.gd`. Verification: Godot import passed, `git diff --check` clean, full headless suite passed (4036 checks, 0 failures). `[ENGINE]` ask: window/mobile playtest a quickslotted wand near two visible sewer mobs — first tap should hit nearest, follow-up taps should keep hitting that mob while it remains valid.

- Tags: talents, warrior, thrown-items, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Warrior Improvised Projectiles (T2, 2 points), which matters in the early-player path because Warrior is the default melee/tutorial-friendly class and this talent unlocks before the first boss stretch if the player survives into tier 2. Source: upstream `Item.cast` / `Talent.IMPROVISED_PROJECTILES`: throwing a non-missile weapon item at an enemy blinds it for `1 + points` turns (2/3) and starts a 50-turn `ImprovisedProjectileCooldown`. Port: `Hero._do_throw_item` now probes the projectile collision cell before the item-specific throw branch, excludes `MissileWeapon`/`SpiritBow`, ignores allies/NPCs/null cells, applies `Blindness`, and attaches the new cooldown buff. Added `src/actors/buffs/improvised_projectile_cooldown.gd` and `test_improvised_projectiles.gd` coverage for registry, durations, cooldown gating, missile exclusion, untalented heroes, allies, and null targets. Also resolved the local toolbar/mobile-HUD merge conflict by keeping the SPD icon-only compact toolbar behavior, landscape Rest threshold, and expanded touch targets. Verification: `git diff --check` clean, Godot import passed, full headless suite passed (4031 checks, 0 failures).

- Tags: talents, mage, wands, buffs, source-fidelity, tests
- Fixed Mage meal talents to the upstream split (Talent.onFoodEaten). The port's `mage_empowering_meal` (T1) wrongly granted Recharging for 8/12 turns — that is upstream's T2 ENERGIZING_MEAL effect at wrong numbers. Now: Empowering Meal attaches a new `WandEmpower` buff (`src/actors/buffs/wand_empower.gd`, upstream buffs/WandEmpower.java) — 1+points (2/3) bonus damage on the next 3 damage-wand zaps; upstream `set()` semantics on re-eat (boost overwritten, left = max(left, shots)) via a `merge()` hook for Char.add_buff. Consumption mirrors DamageWand.damageRoll: `Wand.roll_zap_damage(hero: Char = null)` adds dmg_boost, decrements zaps_left, detaches at 0; hero is passed at the 8 DamageWand-equivalent wands (magic missile, fire bolt, frost, lightning, disintegration, living earth, blast wave, prismatic light) and deliberately NOT at battlemage on-hit, transfusion, or other non-DamageWand rolls. New real `mage_energizing_meal` (T2) grants 2+3*points (5/8) turns of Recharging. Buff persists via serialize/deserialize extras (dmg_boost/zaps_left) — no save-contract change (script-path buff persistence, talent ids additive). Tests: `test_mage_meal_talents.gd` (16 checks: attach/boost/left per point, no-talent no-op, boost+deplete+detach over 3 zaps, hero-less roll neither boosts nor consumes, re-eat overwrite/max semantics, Energizing durations 5/8, serialize round-trip); `test_soul_eater_kill.gd` sentinel updated Recharging→WandEmpower; `test_wand_of_lightning/disintegration` overrides re-signed. Suite 4014 checks 0 failures. `[ENGINE]` ask: Mage playtest — eat with Empowering Meal, next 3 damage zaps should visibly hit harder, buff icon clears after the third; Energizing Meal now needs T2 points and gives shorter recharging than before. Roster gaps recorded in backlog: Mage T1 LINGERING_MAGIC + scholars-at-T1, T2 INSCRIBED_POWER/WAND_PRESERVATION/ARCANE_VISION, T3 DESPERATE_POWER/ALLY_WARP.

- Tags: talents, warrior, strength, buffs, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Warrior Strongman (completes upstream Warrior T3: HOLD_FAST + STRONGMAN). Source: upstream `Hero.STR()` adds `floor(STR * (0.03 + 0.05*points))` bonus strength — 8%/13%/18% of the base `STR` field (excludes Ring of Might / AdrenalineSurge bonuses). Port: the port has no dynamic `STR()` accessor — consumers read `str_val` directly — so the bonus is baked into `str_val` via a new live non-persistent `StrongmanBuff` mirroring the `RingOfMight.MightBuff` contract: `get_str_contribution()` lets `Hero.serialize` persist clean base stats; `update_bonus()` recomputes base str by subtracting every live buff's str contribution (own included), making it idempotent and order-independent vs ring rebuild. `Hero.update_strongman_bonus()` attaches/detaches/recomputes and is called from `upgrade_talent` (on the strongman id), Potion of Strength `drink` (after `str_val += 1`), and end of `Hero.deserialize` (after ring `resolve_post_load`). Registered as Warrior T3 `_make(..., 3, 3)`. Tests: `test_strongman.gd` (12 checks, registered): per-point bonus at base 18 (+1/+2/+3), floor-to-zero at base 10 +1 (buff stays attached), zero points no buff, idempotent recompute, potion recompute uses new base, ring-style fake str buff excluded from base, serialize saves clean base 18, deserialize rebuilds to 21 with contribution 3. Buff-description registry test forced a `description()` — good gate. `[ENGINE]` ask: Warrior 13+ playtest — buying Strongman with 18+ str should immediately show +2/+3 str on the stat sheet, enabling heavier gear; save/load must not drift str up or down. `git diff --check` clean; gdparse/gdlint unavailable — Godot `--import` + full headless suite is the parse gate (3949 checks, 0 failures). Fragile `hero.gd` touched via three small hand-edits (upgrade hook, new method, deserialize rebuild). New `.gd` files need `godot --headless --import` before the suite can resolve their `class_name` (fresh `.uid` files committed). Warrior class roster now missing only: Veteran's Intuition, Provoked Anger (T1), Iron Stomach, Liquid Willpower, Improvised Projectiles (T2).

- Tags: talents, warrior, defense, buffs, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Warrior Hold Fast (first class-tier-3 talent; upstream Warrior T3 is HOLD_FAST + STRONGMAN, both were missing — the port previously had subclass T3s only). Source: upstream `Hero.rest` attaches `HoldFast` pinned to `pos`; `Hero.drRoll` adds `HoldFast.armorBonus()` = `NormalIntRange(points, 2*points)` (1-2/2-4/3-6); `HoldFast.buffDecayFactor(target)` returns 0.5/0.25/0.0 and slows combo + shielding buff decay by 50/75/100% while braced; buff detaches on any position change (act + armorBonus both check). Port: new `HoldFastBuff` (`hold_pos` serialized, static `decay_factor(ch)`), attach in `Hero._do_wait` (both single-wait and continuous rest route through the wait action), armor added in `Hero.dr_roll` before the armor branch (applies on both equipped-armor and fallback paths), `Barrier.on_turn` decay multiplied by the factor, `GladiatorCombo.on_turn` gains a fractional `_decay_accum` (serialized, default-safe on old saves — not a SAVE_VERSION change) since the port combo window is integer turns. WarriorShield intentionally untouched (its shield does not time-decay in the port, matching its own contract). Talent registered as Warrior T3 `_make(..., 3, 3)` with `required_subclass NONE` — first exercise of class-tier-3 through the tier gating (level 13) and per-tier point buckets, which handled it generically. Tests: `test_hold_fast.gd` (22 checks, registered): attach/re-pin-no-stack/zero-points, armor 3..6 bounds + dr_roll inclusion, off-tile detach via armor query and on_turn, decay factor 0.5/0.25/0.0/1.0/null, barrier frozen at +3 then resumes after moving, combo frozen at +3 then resumes, hold_pos serialize round-trip. `[ENGINE]` ask: Warrior 13+ playtest — after waiting, standing your ground should visibly tank harder and keep Gladiator combo/seal-barrier alive; moving should drop the buff. `git diff --check` clean; gdparse/gdlint unavailable — Godot import + full headless suite is the parse gate (3934 checks, 0 failures), fragile `hero.gd` touched via two small hand-edits. Warrior class roster still missing: Veteran's Intuition, Provoked Anger (T1), Iron Stomach, Liquid Willpower, Improvised Projectiles (T2), Strongman (T3).

- Tags: talents, warrior, combat, turn-economy, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Warrior Lethal Momentum (T2, 2 points) — first missing-upstream-talent add to the Warrior roster (previous slices only flipped inert slots; the port's class lists are still a subset of upstream's T1/T2). Source: upstream `Mob.die` (master, verified 2026-07-26): killing cause `hero || Weapon || Weapon.Enchantment` rolls `Random.Float() < 0.34 + 0.33*points` (67%/100% at +1/+2) → `Buff.affect(hero, LethalMomentumTracker, 0f)`; `Hero.attackDelay` head detaches the tracker and returns 0, so the killing blow itself costs no time (the tracker attaches mid-attack, before the delay is spent). Port: new `LethalMomentumTracker` buff (hidden, duration 1.0 ≈ upstream's 0f FlavourBuff one-action window since consumption happens inside the same attack action), `Mob._lethal_momentum_on_kill(source, forced_roll)` called from `_on_death` (gates on the killing source being a hero with points — covers melee; port thrown kills also pass the hero as source), and a consume-first head in `Hero._get_attack_delay` (no talent recheck, matching upstream). Talent registered as Warrior T2 `_make(...)`; not a save-contract change (buff persistence is script-path-generic). Tests: new `test_lethal_momentum.gd` (12 checks, registered): 0.66/0.67 threshold at +1, 0.99 success at +2, zero-points/null-source/mob-source gating, delay 0 + tracker consumed + next attack normal, live-randf determinism at +2 (chance 1.0). `LethalMomentumTracker` added to `test_buff_descriptions.gd` internal_ids (hidden tracker, same as ProtectiveShadowsTracker). `[ENGINE]` ask: Warrior playtest — at +2 every killing blow should be free (kill then immediately act again). `git diff --check` clean; gdparse/gdlint unavailable — Godot import + full headless suite is the parse gate (3912 checks, 0 failures), incl. fragile `hero.gd`/`mob.gd` (small hand-edits).

## 2026-07-25

- Tags: combat, wands, weapons, rng, source-fidelity, tests
- Closed backlog S14: shared `Balance.normal_int_range(min,max)` matching upstream watabou `Random.NormalIntRange` exactly — `min + int((Float()+Float()) * (max-min+1) / 2)`, a triangular distribution peaked mid-range (Godot `randf()` is 1.0-inclusive so the max+1 edge is clamped). Routed through it: `Wand.roll_zap_damage` (was flat uniform — the actual parity gap; upstream `DamageWand.damageRoll` is NormalIntRange), `Char.damage_roll`, `Char.dr_roll` (armor + barkskin components), and `Weapon._roll_from_range` (base range + excess-STR bonus; melee + missile paths). The old hand-rolled int-average `(randi_range+randi_range)/2` truncated low and had a different pmf than upstream. Cursed-zap backfire and blast-wave wall-slam left uniform (not upstream damageRoll sites). New `test_normal_int_range.gd` (14 checks, registered): degenerate/inverted range, bounds + endpoint coverage, mean + peak shape, exact 3-value pmf (2/9, 5/9, 2/9), wand/char roll bounds. Fragile files touched with small hand-edits only: `balance.gd` (helper prepended as section 0), `wand.gd` (1 line), `char.gd` (2 roll bodies), `weapon.gd` (roll body). `git diff --check` clean; gdparse/gdlint unavailable — headless suite is the parse gate (3441 checks, 0 failures). Talent queue note: all remaining `_make_inert` talents (Duelist×2, Assassin's Reach, Sniper Shared Upgrades, Champion×2, Monk×2) are blocked on unported systems (weapon abilities, blink UI, sniper's-mark free shot, dual wield, monk energy) — no unblocked talent item remains.

- Tags: talents, warrior, armor, glyphs, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Warrior Runic Transference (T2, 2 points) — last inert Warrior talent. Source: upstream `BrokenSeal.canTransferGlyph()` + `Armor.detachSeal()/affixSeal()` (master, verified 2026-07-25): on seal transfer the old armor's glyph rides the seal onto the new armor; +1 only common/uncommon glyphs, +2 any glyph (incl. curses). Port adaptation: the seal already auto-transfers at equip time (`Belongings.equip_armor`), so new `Belongings._transfer_seal_glyph()` moves the glyph only when the new armor is unglyphed (no overwrite-with-prompt UI exists; if both armors are glyphed, both keep theirs). Added upstream rarity buckets to `ArmorGlyph` (`COMMON_IDS`/`UNCOMMON_IDS` + `is_common_or_uncommon()`; camouflage unported, curses/rare excluded at +1). Flipped talent from `_make_inert` to implemented with real description; `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` re-anchored on `duelist_aggressive_barrier`. Tests: new `test_runic_transference.gd` (21 checks, registered): registry, rarity helper, 0-point no-transfer, +1 common transfers / rare+curse blocked, +2 rare+curse transfer, pre-glyphed new armor untouched. `[ENGINE]` ask: Warrior playtest — with +1, swapping from glyphed sealed armor to unglyphed armor should carry a common/uncommon glyph across. `git diff --check` clean; gdparse/gdlint unavailable — headless suite is the parse gate (2810 checks, 0 failures), incl. fragile `belongings.gd`/`armor_glyph.gd` (small hand-edits).

- Tags: talents, subclass, warden, missiles, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Warden Durable Tips (all three Warden T3s now live). Source: upstream `TippedDart.durabilityPerUse` (master, verified 2026-07-25): `use /= (1 + pointsInTalent(DURABLE_TIPS))`, so each tipped dart lasts (1+points) uses. The stale blocker "tipped-dart durability not ported yet" was half-wrong — the port has tipped-dart analogues (`curare_dart`/`paralytic_dart`) but its throw path consumes 1 quantity per throw with no durability at all (`MissileWeapon.use_once` has zero call sites). Port adaptation: deterministic counter instead of fractional durability — new `MissileWeapon.durable_tips_uses` (serialized, default 0) + `is_tipped_dart()`; `Hero._durable_tips_preserves()` gates `_consume_thrown_stack_item` in the throw path so each tipped dart survives exactly (1+points) throws before the stack loses one, matching upstream expected durability exactly. Non-tipped missiles and 0-point heroes are untouched; upstream's lotus preservation and dart-cleaning have no port counterpart. Additive save field with default — not a save-contract change. Tests: new `test_durable_tips.gd` (15 checks, registered): registry/implemented, tipped-dart flag coverage, no-points consume, +2 preserve/preserve/consume cycle with counter reset and repeat, non-tipped bypass, serialize round-trip. `[ENGINE]` ask: Warden playtest — with +2 Durable Tips a stack of curare darts should visibly deplete at 1/3 rate. `git diff --check` clean; gdparse/gdlint unavailable this run — Godot import + full headless suite is the parse gate (2789 checks, 0 failures), incl. fragile `hero.gd` (two small hand-edits).

- Tags: talents, subclass, battlemage, artifacts, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Battlemage Mystical Charge (all three Battlemage T3s now live). Source: upstream `MagesStaff.proc` head (master, verified 2026-07-25): any hero with the talent hitting with the staff calls `ArtifactRecharge.chargeArtifacts(hero, points/2f)` — instant charging, not a buff; the static helper charges all equipped non-cursed artifacts by that many turns' worth. The stale blocker "artifact recharging is not ported yet" was wrong — the port's `Artifact` base already has a `charge_rate`/`_recharge` passive model. Port: new `Artifact.charge_turns(turns)` (`_recharge(charge_rate * turns)`, cursed-gated) + `Hero._charge_artifacts(turns)` over the artifact and misc slots, hooked in `Hero.attack_proc` before the Empowered Strike block (upstream order: mystical charge first) gated on equipped `MagesStaff`. Divergence noted: upstream `charge(hero, amount)` is overridden per artifact (e.g. Horn of Plenty); the port applies the uniform charge_rate model, so rate-0 artifacts (Cape of Thorns, Chalice) correctly gain nothing. Not a save-contract change. Tests: `test_mystical_charge.gd` (9 checks, registered): registry/implemented, +2 hit grants 1 charge, +1 banks 0.5 partial then completes on second hit, no-talent/non-staff/cursed gating, misc-slot artifact charges, charge_max cap. `[ENGINE]` ask: Battlemage playtest — with an artifact equipped, staff melee hits should visibly speed up its charging at +1..+3. `git diff --check` clean; gdtoolkit parse OK on all touched files incl. fragile `hero.gd` + `artifact.gd`; gdlint: no new issues; full headless suite 2774 checks, 0 failures.
- Implemented Sniper Shared Enchantment (second live Sniper T3). Source: upstream `MissileWeapon.proc` (master, verified 2026-07-25): hero-thrown missile hits roll `Random.Int(3) < points(SHARED_ENCHANTMENT)`; on success `belongings.getItem(SpiritBow)` with a non-null enchantment and no MagicImmune procs `bow.enchantment.proc(this, attacker, defender, damage)` (the missile is passed as the weapon), applied before the missile's own enchant in `super.proc`. The stale backlog blocker "spirit bow not ported yet" was wrong — `SpiritBow` + a `belongings.spirit_bow` slot already exist. Port: `Hero._shared_enchantment_proc(missile, target, damage, forced_roll)` called in the throw-hit path right before the missile's own `proc_enchantment` (upstream order preserved); finds the bow via `belongings.find_item_by_id("spirit_bow")`, gates on points-in-3 roll + `MagicImmune`. Divergence noted: upstream also shares the bow's Projecting +4 throw reach in `MissileWeapon.throwPos`; the port's Projecting is a damage-proc stub with reach "handled externally", and thrown targeting has no reach clamp to extend, so that half is not ported. `sniper_shared_upgrades` stays inert with a corrected blocker note (needs the sniper's-mark free-bonus-shot system, not the bow). Not a save-contract change (slot was inert-gated). Tests: new `test_shared_enchantment.gd` (10 checks, registered): registry/implemented flags, forced-roll proc adds stub +5 and passes the missile as weapon, roll >= points fails, 0-points/no-bow/unenchanted-bow/MagicImmune gating. `[ENGINE]` ask: Sniper playtest — with an enchanted spirit bow, thrown darts should visibly trigger the bow's enchant at +3. `git diff --check` clean; gdtoolkit parse OK on all touched files incl. fragile `hero.gd` (two small hand-edits); gdlint: no new issues; full headless suite 2764 checks, 0 failures.

- Tags: talents, subclass, battlemage, shielding, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented Battlemage Excess Charge (second live Battlemage T3) and reconciled the T3 roster to upstream. Source: upstream `Wand.wandUsed` staff-wand branch (master, verified 2026-07-25): inside-staff wand + `hasTalent(EXCESS_CHARGE) && curCharges >= maxCharges` (checked before the charge decrement) -> `Buff.affect(hero, Barrier).setShield(round(buffedLvl()*0.67*points))`; `ShieldBuff.setShield` only raises (`if shielding <= shield`), never lowers/stacks. Port: `MagesStaff._apply_excess_charge()` called from `zap()` after the charge-spent check — `charges_before >= charges_max` reproduces the pre-decrement full-charge condition; shield = `roundi(imbued_wand.buffed_lvl()*0.67*points)` applied via merge-then-raise (`add_buff(Barrier)` + `set_shield` only when existing shielding is lower). Cursed backfires that spend a charge also qualify, matching wandUsed. Divergence noted: upstream keeps the staff wand's level synced to the staff level; the port's imbued wand levels independently, so the shield keys off the wand's own buffed level (level 0 -> 0 shield, same as an upstream +0 staff). Roster: non-upstream inert `battlemage_arcane_renewal` replaced by implemented `battlemage_excess_charge` + inert `battlemage_mystical_charge` groundwork slot (upstream: staff hits grant 0.5/1/1.5 turns of artifact recharging — blocked on the artifact-recharging system, not yet ported). Not a save-contract change (arcane_renewal was inert-gated, no live save holds points). Tests: new `test_excess_charge.gd` (9 checks, registered): full-charge shield 4 at +3/lvl2, +1/lvl3 scaling, no-talent/partial-charge/fizzled-zap/level-0 gating, stronger barrier untouched, weaker barrier raised-not-stacked. `[ENGINE]` ask: quick Battlemage playtest that zapping an upgraded staff at full charges shows the shield message/barrier icon. `git diff --check` clean; gdtoolkit parse OK on all touched files (none fragile); gdlint: no new issues (talent_data long-desc lines are pre-existing file style); full headless suite 2754 checks, 0 failures.

- Implemented Battlemage Empowered Strike (first live Battlemage T3). Source: upstream `Wand.wandUsed` staff-wand branch (`hasTalent(EMPOWERED_STRIKE) && charger.target == hero && wand not in belongings` -> `Buff.prolong(EmpoweredStrikeTracker, 10f)`) and `MagesStaff.proc` (`damage = Math.round(damage * (1 + points/6))`, tracker detached after the hit; master, verified 2026-07-25). Port: `EmpoweredStrikeTracker` FlavourBuff (created last slice, now wired + registered via Godot import); `MagesStaff.zap` grants it when a charge was actually spent (incl. cursed backfires, matching upstream wandUsed; fizzled/blocked zaps don't); `Hero.attack_proc` applies x(1 + points/6) round-half-up and consumes the tracker only when the equipped weapon is the staff — non-staff hits leave it alive like upstream. - Tags: talents, subclass, warden, shielding, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented the Warden's Shielding Dew talent (second live Warden T3). Source: upstream `Dewdrop.consumeDew` (master, fetched 2026-07-25): `effect = min(HT - HP, heal)`, `shield = heal - effect`, capped at `round(HT * 0.2 * points)` minus existing `Barrier.shielding()`, then `Barrier.incShield(shield)`. Port: `Dewdrop._shielding_dew_amount()` computes the overheal shield with the same formula/cap and `on_pickup` applies it via the existing `Barrier` buff (merge-then-`inc_shield` through `add_buff`). Adaptation notes: the port has no waterskin/dew vial, so the vial-collection branch and the upstream +25%-vial-charge half of the talent don't apply; the port's flat 1-3 heal (vs upstream HT/20-scaled drops) is preserved — only the overheal-conversion behavior is new. Talent metadata flipped from `_make_inert` to real with upstream-style description; not a save-contract change (slot id unchanged, was inert-gated). Tests: new `test_shielding_dew.gd` (9 checks, registered): registry, no-points zero, full-HP full conversion, partial-overheal split, no-overheal zero, cap vs existing barrier (partial + saturated), full-HP `on_pickup` integration granting 1-3 barrier shield. `[ENGINE]` ask: quick Warden playtest that walking over dew at full HP shows the barrier icon and the shield message. `git diff --check` clean; gdtoolkit parse OK on all touched files (via `python3 -m gdtoolkit.parser`; the `gdparse` shim isn't on PATH); full headless suite 2718 checks, 0 failures.

- Tags: talents, subclass, warden, armor, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented the Warden's Barkskin talent (first live Warden T3). Source: upstream `Hero.act()` tail — `if hasTalent(BARKSKIN) && map[pos] == FURROWED_GRASS: Barkskin.conditionallyAppend(this, lvl*points/2, 1)` (master, verified 2026-07-25; the only talent hook — the Warden reaches furrowed grass because her trample furrows it). Port: `Hero._apply_barkskin_talent()` called at the end of `execute_action()` (the port's end-of-act site): standing in furrowed grass appends/refreshes the existing upstream-parity `Barkskin` buff at `hero_level*points/2`, interval 1 (decays 1/turn; DR already wired via `Barkskin.current_level` in `Hero.dr_roll`). Adaptation notes: the dead/paralysed early-return paths skip the hook (upstream applies it on every act); the port furrows trampled high grass for all heroes, so non-Warden Huntresses with the talent via level access would also trigger it — subclass gating comes from the talent itself. Roster: non-upstream inert `warden_barkskin_mastery`/`warden_overgrowth` replaced by real `warden_barkskin` + inert upstream slots `warden_durable_tips` (tipped-dart durability not ported) and `warden_shielding_dew` (dew shielding not ported) — old slots were inert-gated, so not a save-contract change. Tests: new `test_warden_barkskin_talent.gd` (14 checks, registered): registry + old-slot removal, furrow trigger with +1/+2/+3 scaling incl. integer division, interval 1, no-talent and plain-grass gating, per-turn decay, conditionally-append reuse (single instance) with level refresh. `[ENGINE]` ask: worth a quick Warden playtest that the barkskin icon appears/refreshes while fighting inside furrowed grass. `git diff --check` clean; gdtoolkit parse OK on all touched files incl. fragile `hero.gd` (two small hand-edits); gdlint: one net new long-line hit in `talent_data.gd` (file's single-line roster style, matching pre-existing hits); full headless suite 2709 checks, 0 failures.

Source: upstream `Level.updateFieldOfView` multiplies the hero's shadowcast `viewDistance` by `1f + 0.25f*pointsInTalent(FARSIGHT)` after the level's own vision value is set, then `Math.round`s (master, verified 2026-07-25; `Dungeon.observe` applies the same factor to its recompute radius). - Tags: talents, subclass, freerunner, armor, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented the Freerunner's Evasive Armor talent (all three Freerunner T3s now live). Source: upstream `Momentum.evasionBonus(heroLvl, excessArmorStr)` returns `heroLvl/2 + excessArmorStr * pointsInTalent(EVASIVE_ARMOR)` while freerunning, called from `Armor.evasionFactor` with `excess = max(0, STR - STRReq)` (master, verified 2026-07-25). Port: `FreerunnerMomentum.evasion_bonus(excess_armor_str)` returns `excess * points` while at max momentum (the port's freerun state), and `Armor.evasion_factor` now looks up the momentum buff and adds the bonus after the encumbrance division, mirroring upstream's call site — heroes below the armor's STR requirement get no bonus since excess clamps to 0. The upstream `heroLvl/2` freerun base is intentionally not added: the port's existing x1.5 max-momentum `modify_evasion` multiplier already stands in for it (documented in the buff), though that multiplier does also scale the talent term slightly above upstream. Talent metadata flipped from `_make_inert` to real with upstream-style description. Tests: new `test_evasive_armor.gd` (11 checks, registered): registry, no bonus below max momentum, per-point scaling, zero-excess/untalented zero, `Armor.evasion_factor` integration delta, encumbered no-bonus; `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` flipped its Evasive Armor assertion to upgradable. `git diff --check` clean; gdtoolkit parse OK on all touched files; full headless suite 2688 checks, 0 failures.

- Tags: talents, subclass, assassin, combat, source-fidelity, tests
- Assassin Preparation rewritten to upstream parity + Enhanced Lethality talent. Bug found: the whole break-invisibility-on-attack path was dead — `Hero._do_attack` calls `Invisibility.dispel()` but no `dispel()` existed anywhere (runtime crash whenever the hero attacked while invisible); now implemented (removes the invis buff and, once `invisible <= 0`, consumes Preparation, matching upstream `Invisibility.dispel`). Source: upstream `Preparation.java` + `Char.attack` block (master, verified 2026-07-25): Preparation attaches via `Invisibility.attachTo` for Assassins only, builds one turn per invisible turn and detaches when visibility returns; attack levels at 1/3/5/9 turns give base damage bonus 10/20/35/50% with best-of-1/1/2/3 damage rolls, and a KO threshold (3/10/20/50% of max HP, ENHANCED_LETHALITY tables up to 6/20/40/100%, bosses/minibosses ÷5) that executes surviving enemies outright. Old port behavior (permanent buff building while merely unseen, flat 1.5-3.0x multiplier via `modify_damage`) removed. Port: `assassin_preparation.gd` rewritten (turns_invis + upstream tables, `prep_damage_roll(Callable)`, `can_ko`, staged desc, serialize); `Invisibility.on_attach` attaches it for Assassin heroes; `Char.attack` swaps the base roll for the prepared roll and adds the post-damage assassination block (skips hero/ally targets, kill credit to attacker via `die(self)`); `_apply_assassin` no longer grants a permanent buff (old saves shed the restored buff on their first non-invisible turn — no save bump); talents: non-upstream `assassin_deep_preparation`/`assassin_shadow_step` replaced by live `assassin_enhanced_lethality` + inert upstream slots `assassin_assassins_reach` (blink-attack UI not implemented) + `assassin_bounty_hunter` (loot-chance hook open). Documented divergences: no blink attack/ActionIndicator, prepared roll+KO applies to `Char.attack` melee only (hero thrown-weapon path in `hero.gd` still uses the plain roll), no prep buff-icon tint stages. Tests: new `test_assassin_preparation.gd` (22 checks, registered): attach gating, tier thresholds, dispel consumption, best-of-N roll math per level, KO boundaries incl. exclusive threshold, Enhanced Lethality 67%/100% scaling, boss ÷5, full `Char.attack` execution through 1000 armor, serialize round-trip. `[ENGINE]` ask: real-game feel of prep building/detaching with potions/cloak-free invisibility needs a Rogue playtest. `git diff --check` clean; `--check-only` on autoload-using files fails identically on main (pre-existing; suite is the parse gate); full headless suite 2640 checks, 0 failures.

- Tags: talents, subclass, warlock, combat, source-fidelity, tests
- Warlock Soul Mark upstream parity + Soul Siphon talent. Bugs found: the entire Warlock kit was dead code — wands never called `SoulMarkPassive.apply_soul_mark` (no mark was ever applied), and `Mob._on_death` passed the mob itself as the killer so the old death-heal's `source.is_hero` check always failed. Source: upstream `Wand.wandProc` marks on zap with chance `Random.Float() > 0.92^(wandLevel*chargesUsed+1) - 0.07` (15% at lvl 0) and duration `10 + wandLevel` (prolong); `Mob.defenseProc` restores on physical damage to a marked mob: `restoration = min(damage, HP+shielding)`, non-hero attackers scaled by `0.4*SOUL_SIPHON/3` (13/27/40%), heal `ceil(0.4*restoration)` only if HP<HT, satiety `restoration*SOUL_EATER/3`; wand/magic damage does NOT trigger restoration (verified against `00-Evan/shattered-pixel-dungeon` master 2026-07-25). Port: `SoulMark` rewritten (death-heal removed) with `process_restoration(damage, attacker)` + `prolong()`; `Mob.defense_proc` override routes marked pre-armor damage into it; `Wand._warlock_wand_proc` called from shared `zap()` applies the upstream chance/duration to the char at the bolt's stop cell (adaptation: chained/piercing secondary targets are not marked); `SoulMarkPassive` is now a pure indicator buff with the upstream subclass desc. - Gladiator Lethal Defense talent. Source: upstream `Combo.doAttack` — on a finisher move that kills (or a Crush AOE kill), `Buff.affect(hero, BrokenSeal.WarriorShield).reduceCooldown(points/3f)` where `reduceCooldown(pct)` subtracts `round(COOLDOWN_START*pct)` and floors at `-COOLDOWN_START` (-150); talent desc: cooldown reduced by 50/100/150 turns, can bank to -150 so the next activation is instantly available again (verified against `00-Evan/shattered-pixel-dungeon` master 2026-07-25). Port: `WarriorShield.reduce_cooldown(turns)` (floor -150; removal check loosened to `cooldown <= 0` so a negative-cooldown buff with no seal still detaches; `activate()`'s existing `maxi(0, cooldown+150)` already gives the instant-reuse behavior); `GladiatorCombo` gained a `_finisher_this_hit` flag set when `modify_damage` consumes the combo, and `on_damage_dealt` on a finisher kill attaches-or-reuses WarriorShield and reduces its cooldown by `50*points`. Adaptation: the port's combo is an automatic >=3-hit finisher (no move picker), so "kills with a finisher move" maps to "the killing hit consumed the combo multiplier"; ranged attacks never fire `on_damage_dealt`, matching upstream's melee-only combo. Roster: inert `gladiator_combo_mastery` (not an upstream talent) replaced by implemented `gladiator_lethal_defense` + inert `gladiator_enhanced_combo` groundwork slot (upstream's real T3 trio is Cleave/Lethal Defense/Enhanced Combo; Enhanced Combo needs the combo-move picker). Not a save-contract change: combo_mastery was inert-gated so no live save can hold points in it. Tests: new `test_lethal_defense.gd` (11 checks, registered): reduce_cooldown math + floor, +1/+3 kill reductions, buff auto-attach with -100 credit at +2, no-talent no-attach, non-kill finisher and non-finisher kill no-ops, flag reset, instant reactivation from -150; `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` now sweeps GLADIATOR too with cleave/lethal_defense in the implemented list and the inert probe moved to `gladiator_enhanced_combo`. `git diff --check` clean; parser + gdlint clean on touched files (new test lint-clean; warrior_shield/gladiator_combo hits are pre-existing style); full headless suite 2585 checks, 0 failures.

Source check against upstream `Paralysis.java`: `Char.damage` calls `Paralysis.processDamage(dmg)` post-resist/pre-shield; damage accumulates in a persistent positive `ParalysisResist` buff and paralysis breaks when `NormalIntRange(0, resist.damage) >= NormalIntRange(0, HP)`; the resist decays `damage -= ceil(damage/10)` per turn only while no paralysis is active and detaches at zero, and bundles its damage counter. The port previously rolled a per-hit inline `randi_range(0, amount)` in `Paralysis.on_damage_taken` with no accumulation — and `char.gd:318` already called a nonexistent `process_damage`, so the intended hook was dead while `on_damage_taken` double-served. Now `paralysis.gd` implements `process_damage` (attach-or-reuse `ParalysisResist`, accumulate, roll break vs current HP) and the inline `on_damage_taken` path was removed; new `src/actors/buffs/paralysis_resist.gd` handles decay/detach and serializes `damage` through the generic buff `_script_path` restore (no save-contract change). - Tags: chasms, heaps, drop-routing, source-fidelity, tests
- Routed `Level.drop_item` onto CHASM terrain to the floor below (SPD parity plan immediate-queue item 4). Source check against current upstream `Level.drop()`: when a new heap would be created on a cell with `map[cell] == Terrain.CHASM` (or `pit[cell]` — this port has no separate pit array; CHASM terrain is the equivalent), upstream calls `Dungeon.dropToChasm(item)` and discards the heap instead of placing it. The port previously appended a heap on any cell, so items dropped, thrown, or mob-dropped over a chasm floated on the chasm tile. `Level.drop_item` now routes serializable items on CHASM cells through the existing `GameManager.drop_to_chasm` queue (delivered by `LoadingScene._deliver_fallen_items` on arrival, past-last-depth items lost) and returns a `dropped_to_chasm`-flagged dictionary without appending to `heaps`; heap rendering iterates `level.heaps`, so no sprite appears. All drop sites (hero drop, chasm-fall `drop_random_item`, mob/thief/mimic/statue loot, disarming/gateway traps) share this single routing point. Added `test_chasm_drop_item.gd` (7 checks, registered): chasm drop queues for next depth with no heap, normal floor still heaps, last-depth chasm drop is lost. Local `git diff --check` clean; full headless suite passed (2201 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented divergences: upstream's falling-item visual/SFX on discard is omitted, and an existing heap already on a chasm cell (impossible now via drop_item) is not re-routed.

- Tags: visuals, pacing, sprites, movement, source-fidelity, tests
- Checked current upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon (`00-Evan/shattered-pixel-dungeon` master `7b8b845`) for visual/gameplay pacing: `CharSprite.DEFAULT_MOVE_INTERVAL` is `0.1f`, `HeroSprite.RUN_FRAMERATE` is 20 fps, attack/zap is 15 fps over the four attack frames, operate is 8 fps, movement is `sprite.move(pos, step)` plus `spend(delay / speed())`, and visible mob pacing in the port was already on the same short 0.1s interval. The port had drifted slower in several visual paths (`0.12s` snapshot/remote movement, `0.15s` keyboard movement/autowalk pacing, and `0.28s` touch movement), so `CharSprite.DEFAULT_MOVE_INTERVAL` now centralizes the 0.1s source interval and hero/mob snapshot, remote, keyboard, touch, and autowalk visual movement all use it. Also grounded character art by applying a 2px downward body offset on top of the existing 2px perspective raise, matching the reported "down 2-ish pixels" feel while leaving the shadow baseline intact. Updated `test_char_sprite_animations.gd` to lock the grounded sprite/body relationship. Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full registered headless suite passed (2164 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: traps, disarming, source-fidelity, tests
- Tightened `DisarmingTrap` against current upstream `DisarmingTrap.java` (SPD parity plan Phase 2 trap fidelity; backlog audit:S10). Source check: upstream only disarms when `Dungeon.hero.pos == trap.pos`, the hero is not flying, and `hero.belongings.weapon` exists and is not cursed; the weapon is unequipped and dropped on a random respawn cell at PathFinder distance 10-20 from the trap, not adjacent. The port already had the belongings-slot and `drop_item(drop_pos, weapon)` argument-order fixes, but still disarmed cursed weapons, flying heroes, and threw the weapon to a neighbouring cell. `disarming_trap.gd` now gates to Hero/non-flying/uncursed weapon, chooses a random passable drop cell 10-20 cells away, keeps the belongings unequip path, and destroys the port's `animated_statue` mob when it triggers the trap. Expanded `test_disarming_trap.gd` for distant-drop bounds plus cursed/flying no-disarm regressions. Remaining divergences: loose heap teleport, visited/mapped reveal, particles/SFX, and exact `randomRespawnCell` exclusions are still simplified.

- Tags: wands, warding, sentry, source-fidelity, tests, backlog-reconcile
- Corrected the WandOfWarding sentry damage/cap numbers to upstream and retired a stale backlog item (backlog audit:S14). The [P1][audit:S14] entry claimed "WandOfWarding is fully inert: on_zap only tracks positions … no Sentry actor is spawned" — that is no longer true: the wand already spawns a real `WardSentry` mob (`wand.gd` `WandOfWarding.on_zap`) that zaps hostiles each turn and is covered by `test_wand_of_warding.gd`. Verified against current upstream `WandOfWarding.java` (fetched from `00-Evan/shattered-pixel-dungeon` master): the full upstream model is a per-ward TIER system (tiers 1-6, energy cost per tier, `2 + level` max wards per `Wand.Charger`, `totalZaps = 2*tier-1` for tiers 1-3 and HP-drain-per-zap for tiers 4-6) — too large for a narrow slice and not ported. Within the port's existing simplified single-tier "sentry" model I fixed the two directly-portable numeric divergences: (1) **Damage roll** — upstream `Ward.damageRoll()` is `heroDamageIntRange(2 + wandLevel, 8 + 4*wandLevel)`, but the port returned `[2 + lvl, 5 + 2*lvl]`, under-tuning the max (e.g. lvl 3: max 11 vs upstream 20). `get_damage()` now returns `[2 + lvl, 8 + 4*lvl]`. (2) **Concurrent-ward cap** — upstream allows `2 + level` wards; the port's `MAX_SENTRIES + level` used `MAX_SENTRIES = 3` (i.e. `3 + level`). Lowered `MAX_SENTRIES` to `2` so the live cap is `2 + level`. Extended `test_wand_of_warding.gd` with `_test_damage_range_matches_upstream` (asserts min/max at lvl 0/1/3/5) and `_test_concurrent_ward_cap` (places `cap+2` wards, asserts only `2 + level` survive and tracked positions stay in sync). Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full registered headless suite passed (2164 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1) — including the 8 new damage-range checks and the `2 + level` cap check (`Concurrent wards capped at 2 + level (3), got 3`). (Note: the workspace TOOLS.md claim that Godot is not installed locally is stale — `godot` 4.7.1 is on PATH and the suite runs headless.) Documented divergences still open (out of this narrow slice): no per-ward tier progression, energy-cost-per-tier placement, `totalZaps` expiry, or tier-4-6 HP-drain death; the sentry's per-zap roll uses uniform `randi_range` rather than SPD's `NormalIntRange`; and there is no zap SFX/visual.

- Tags: mobs, necromancer, summon-timing, source-fidelity, tests
- Wired the Necromancer's `first_summon` flag into summon charge-up timing (backlog audit:S05). Source check against current upstream `Necromancer.java` (fetched from `00-Evan/shattered-pixel-dungeon` master): when the necromancer begins raising a skeleton its custom `Hunting` state runs `summoning = true; sprite.zap(summoningPos); spend( firstSummon ? TICK : 2*TICK )`, so the FIRST skeleton charges up in one tick while every re-summon afterward takes two; `summonMinion()` then sets `summoning = firstSummon = false`. The port tracked, set, and serialized `first_summon` but **never read it** — both the first and later charge-ups called a plain `spend_turn()` (1 tick), so the promised faster first summon never happened. Fix: in `necromancer.gd` `_act_hunting()`, the summon-start branch now calls `spend_turn(1.0 if first_summon else 2.0)` (the port's `spend_turn(n)` forwards `n` as the TICK-count to `TurnManager.spend_energy`, `cooldown += turns / speed`), the no-valid-position fall-through keeps a normal `spend_turn()`, and the completion branch keeps its normal turn — so total time to raise is 1+1 ticks first vs 2+1 later, preserving upstream's "first is faster" relationship exactly at the charge-start. Added `tests/cases/test_necromancer_summon.gd` (registered in `run_tests.gd`): registers the necromancer with `TurnManager`, runs one hunting act from a clean cooldown, and asserts the first charge-up costs 1.0 tick, a re-summon (after `first_summon` flips off) costs 2.0 ticks, the re-summon is strictly slower, and the summoning state is entered. Godot import clean; full registered headless suite passed (2148 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented divergences (unchanged, out of this narrow slice): the port begins summoning at any range rather than upstream's within-4-tiles gate, and there is no sprite-zap charge-up visual.

- Tags: wands, lightning, chain-lightning, source-fidelity, tests
- Rewrote `WandOfLightning` to match upstream `WandOfLightning.java` (backlog audit:S14 chain-lightning). Verified verbatim against current `master`. Five divergences fixed in one slice: (1) **Damage range** — port had `[3+lvl, 10+3*lvl]`; upstream is `min = 5+lvl`, `max = 10+5*lvl`, so the wand was under-tuned and scaled worse. Now `get_damage()` returns `[5+lvl, 10+5*lvl]`. (2) **Damage falloff model** — port applied a per-arc GEOMETRIC falloff (`damage_multi *= 0.7` each hop, so 1.0/0.7/0.49/…); upstream instead computes ONE shared multiplier `0.4 + 0.6/affected.size()` and applies it EQUALLY to every affected char (crowd of 1 → full, 2 → 0.7, 3 → 0.6, …). Reimplemented as the shared multiplier. (3) **Arc targeting** — port chained to the single nearest char up to `3+level` hops via an 81-cell `find_char_at` grid scan (`_find_nearest_char`, now deleted); upstream floods via `PathFinder.buildDistanceMap` from each caught char out to 1 tile (2 in water) over non-solid cells, recursively, catching *every* connected char with no hop cap. Ported as a wrap-safe BFS (`_cells_within` → `_arc`), respecting `is_passable` when the level exposes it and hero-adjacency (`n is Hero` only caught at BFS step distance ≤ 1). (4) **Water / self-damage** — upstream: a struck cell in water forces multiplier `1.0` (full damage to the whole chain) and extends arc reach to 2; the caster is damaged only when the chain actually reaches them (adjacent, or through water), at `damageRoll * multiplier * 0.5`. Port previously always self-hit a hero standing in water regardless of proximity, at a flat `0.5 * roll` with no crowd multiplier. Now water forces `1.0` + reach 2, and the caster takes half damage only when caught in `affected`. (5) **Ally filtering** — upstream removes same-alignment chained targets after the arc graph is built while preserving a same-alignment direct hit; the port now lets allies conduct the chain, then filters allied chained targets out of the damage set. Added `tests/cases/test_wand_of_lightning.gd` (20 checks): damage range at lvl 0/3, lone-target full damage, 3-mob shared-multiplier 60-each (proving no geometric spread), water→full-100 chain, distant-hero-untouched vs adjacent-hero-caught-at-half (35 while the mob takes 70), allied-mob non-damage/direct-hit/conduit behavior, and bare-terrain fizzle; registered in `run_tests.gd`. Note (out of scope, tracked): `roll_zap_damage()` still uses uniform `randi_range` where SPD's `DamageWand.damageRoll()` uses `Random.NormalIntRange` (bell curve) — a cross-wand concern (backlog audit:S14 NormalIntRange item), and the LightningCharge on-hit staff proc has no port equivalent yet. Local `git diff --check` clean, Godot import clean, full registered headless suite passed (2142 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: weakness, encumbrance, weapons, traps, source-fidelity, verification
- Deep re-verified the `cron/weakness-encumbrance` theme against current upstream `master` (no code change; confirms the earlier audit:S15 note that this theme is parity-correct). Verbatim source checks: (1) `Weakness.DURATION == 20f` — port `Weakness.BASE_DURATION == 20.0` matches; Weakness is a pure FlavourBuff with no STR/damage subtraction, and the port keeps its combat effect as the existing `Char.attack()` 0.67 outgoing multiplier. (2) `WeakeningTrap.activate()` runs `Buff.prolong(ch, Weakness, DURATION*3)` **unconditionally** after the boss/miniboss `DURATION/2` line, and `prolong`/`add_buff` both take the max, so bosses effectively receive 60 turns just like everyone else — the port's single `BASE_DURATION*3` (60) application is correct and its "3x dominates the boss floor" comment holds. (3) Encumbrance math: weapon `accuracy_factor` (`1/pow(1.5, deficit)`), `speed_factor` (`delay*pow(1.2, deficit)` only when deficit>0), and `STRReq = maxi(1, 8+tier*2+bonus - floor((sqrt(8*lvl+1)-1)/2))` all match upstream `Weapon`. (4) Excess-STR damage bonus: upstream adds `Hero.heroDamageIntRange(0, exStr)` which resolves to `Random.NormalIntRange` (triangular, ignoring the ThirteenLeafClover trinket branch), so the port's two-roll `(a+b)/2` average over `[0, exStr]` is the correct approximation — not a uniform-roll divergence. (5) Adjacent gas-trap seed amounts spot-checked: `ConfusionTrap`/`ToxicTrap` both seed `300 + 20*scalingDepth()`, matching the port exactly. Genuine remaining gaps (both out of scope for a narrow slice, logged to backlog): mob `HazardAssistTracker` is still omitted by WeakeningTrap/ConfusionTrap/ToxicTrap (a cross-cutting mob-assist system that would be inert without consumer logic), and the port's legacy `paralytic_trap.gd` (no upstream `ParalyticTrap` exists in current master) seeds a flat non-scaling `5.0` ParalyticGas that is far weaker than the sibling gas-trap pattern. Full registered headless suite passed (2109 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: traps, grim, halls, source-fidelity, tests
- Rebuilt `GrimTrap` on current upstream `GrimTrap.java` (SPD parity plan Phase 2 trap parity; backlog audit:S10). Source check: current SPD makes Grim Trap a visible Halls trap (`canBeHidden = false`) that first targets a character standing on the trap cell, otherwise finds the closest living character it can aim at within `max(6, viewDistance)+0.5`, treating invisible targets as max-range and using `trueDistance`/projectile aimability. Damage is the same current/max HP mix used by Psi Blast, `round(HT/2 + HP/2)`, capped to 90% HT for the hero; it does not apply Weakness and does not use a depth-scaled low-HP execution rule. The port now sets Grim Trap visible, uses an SPD-style true-distance closest-aimable fallback via `Ballistica.PROJECTILE`, applies the SPD damage/cap formula through `take_damage(_grim_damage(target), self)`, and removes the invented Weakness survivor debuff. Added and registered `test_grim_trap.gd` covering on-cell damage, hero 90% HT cap, no Weakness regression, closest aimable fallback, blocked/too-far target rejection, diagonal true-distance range, and invisible target max-range handling. - Tags: plants, sorrowmoss, toxic-imbue, buffs, blobs, source-fidelity, tests
- Rebuilt Sorrowmoss and added a functional `ToxicImbue` (SPD parity plan Phase 2, plant/blob routing — Sorrowmoss line). Source check against current upstream `Sorrowmoss.java` + `ToxicImbue.java`: Sorrowmoss applies `Buff.affect(ch, Poison).set(5 + round(2*scalingDepth/3))` to whoever steps on it, and a Warden hero additionally gets `Buff.affect(ch, ToxicImbue, ToxicImbue.DURATION*0.3)`; `ToxicImbue.act()` seeds 6 units of `ToxicGas` into every non-solid `NEIGHBOURS8` cell each turn (a solid neighbour's share folds into `centerVolume`, which starts at 6), seeds the accumulated centre volume on the owner's cell, and adds `Poison`/`ToxicGas` to the owner's immunities (stripping existing Poison on attach) so the owner walks unharmed inside their own cloud. The port previously used an invented `4 + depth*0.5` poison amount and gave the Warden nothing. Fix: `src/plants/sorrowmoss.gd` now exposes a static `poison_amount(depth) = 5 + roundi(2*depth/3)`, applies Poison via the existing max-duration `add_buff` merge (matching SPD `set()`), and branches Warden → new `ToxicImbue`. Added `src/actors/buffs/toxic_imbue.gd`: a 50-turn positive buff whose `on_turn()` seeds the redirected 6-per-cell ToxicGas footprint via `Level.add_blob`, `immunities()` returns `["Poison","toxic_gas","ToxicGas"]`, and `on_attach()` clears existing Poison. It rides the already-wired `Char._tick_buffs_once()` timeline (no hot-path changes). Added `test_sorrowmoss.gd` (15 checks): poison formula at depths 1/3/6/12, non-Warden 9-turn poison, Warden gets the 30%-duration imbue + Poison immunity + no self-poison, imbue seeds the base centre volume with all 8 open neighbours gassed, and a solid neighbour redirects its share to the owner's cell. Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (2109 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented divergences: `scalingDepth()` is plain `level.depth` (no challenge modifiers); the port's `Blob.seed()` uses max-per-cell rather than SPD's additive accumulation (pre-existing port-wide blob semantics); upstream's 5-turn post-gas immunity tail (`left <= -5` detach) is not modelled — the imbue and its immunity expire together at `time_left == 0`; and the `HazardAssistTracker` mob hint plus gas SFX/particles are omitted, consistent with the sibling plant/trap slices. The Fire/Frost imbue `proc()` hooks remain unwired (separate backlog item, audit:S06); this slice's `ToxicImbue` is instead a per-turn `on_turn()` gas emitter that needs no attack-path wiring.
- Tags: chasms, weak-floor, pit-room, landing, source-fidelity, tests
- Added the first SPD-style PitRoom landing hook for weak-floor falls (SPD parity plan Phase 2, chasm/pitfall landing). Source check against current upstream `Chasm.java`: `Chasm.heroFall(pos)` sets the interlevel `fallIntoPit` flag only when the source cell belongs to a `WeakFloorRoom`; the next floor then uses the pit-room landing context instead of a generic random landing. The port now preserves that source-context through `FloorTransitionCoordinator.handle_fall()` as `fall_into_pit` metadata and `LoadingScene._landing_anchor_for_transition()` prefers the center/platform cell of a generated `PitRoom` when that flag is set, falling back to the nearest usable pit-room interior cell and then the old random passable landing when no usable `PitRoom` exists. Ordinary chasm/pitfall-trap falls that do not originate in a `WeakFloorRoom` keep the previous generic landing behavior. Expanded `test_chasm_fall.gd` for weak-floor source detection and PitRoom platform anchoring. Local `godot --headless --path . -s res://tests/run_tests.gd --case res://tests/cases/test_chasm_fall.gd` ran the full registered suite in this harness and passed (2091 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Remaining divergences: `PitRoom.paint()` still uses the port's existing simplified pedestal/chasm-ring room rather than upstream's crystal-door + empty-well + skeleton-loot setup, fall metadata is not serialized beyond the immediate loading-scene transition, and pitfall heap drop-to-next-floor remains separate.

- Tags: buffs, earthroot, herbal-armor, warden, source-fidelity, tests
- Rebuilt Earthroot's Herbal Armor to match upstream `Earthroot.Armor` and fixed the Warden branch (SPD parity plan Phase 3-adjacent armor/buff content; backlog audit:S06). Source check against current upstream `Earthroot.java`: `activate()` gives a Warden hero `Barkskin.conditionallyAppend(hero, hero.lvl + 5, 5)` and every other character `Buff.affect(ch, Armor.class).level(ch.HT)`; the inner `Armor` buff has NO flat armor bonus and NO fixed timer — it exposes `blocking() = (scalingDepth + 5)/2`, `absorb(dmg)` reduces the hit by `block = min(dmg, blocking())` and drains that from the pool (detaching when the pool `<= block`), `level(v)` grows the pool to `max(pool, v)`, and `act()` detaches the moment the owner moves off its anchored cell. The port previously invented a flat `+5 modify_armor` AND a fixed 15-point post-hit `on_damage_taken` pool on a 20-turn timer — none of which is the SPD mechanic, and it never scaled to HT/depth, never detached on move, and stacked flat DR on top of absorption. `src/actors/buffs/herbal_armor.gd` now holds a single `pool` (sized to `ch.HT` on apply), implements the pre-hit `absorb_damage()` block/drain path used by the existing ShieldBuff loop in `Char.take_damage`, detaches via `on_move()` and on depletion, keeps `duration = -1` (no timer), merges by keeping the larger pool, and serializes `pool` while migrating the legacy `absorb_remaining` key so old saves still load. `src/plants/earthroot.gd` now branches Warden→`Barkskin.conditionally_append(hero, hero.lvl + 5, 5)` (matching the existing Firebloom/Icecap Warden pattern) and everyone else→`HerbalArmorBuff` with `apply_pool(ch.HT)`. Added `test_herbal_armor.gd` (18 checks): blocking formula, no-flat-armor regression, HT-sized pool, per-hit block + overflow-to-HP through `Char.take_damage`, small-hit capping, detach-on-move, detach-on-depletion, pool serialize round-trip + legacy migration, and the Earthroot plant sizing the pool to HT on the non-Warden path. Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (2064 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented divergences: `scalingDepth()` uses plain `GameManager.depth` (this port has no challenge modifiers), and the SPD anchored-cell `act()` self-check is realized through the buff-move hook rather than a per-turn position poll (same observable result — armor is lost on the first move).
- Tags: traps, edge-wrap, level-generation, source-fidelity, tests
- Closed the generic trap-footprint row-wrap gap in several 3x3/adjacent trap effects (SPD parity plan Phase 2, trap routing/level-generation hardening; backlog audit:S10). Source-fidelity rationale: SPD uses `PathFinder.NEIGHBOURS8/9` over true grid neighbours, so a trap at column 0 must never treat `pos - 1` as a west neighbour on the previous row. The port still had raw `pos + dir` loops with only `0 <= cell < LEN` bounds in `FlashingTrap`, `ExplosiveTrap`, `BlazingTrap`, `FlockTrap`, `GuardianTrap`, `SummoningTrap`, `DisarmingTrap`, and `RockfallTrap`, letting blindness, explosion damage, fire spread, spawn/drop candidates, and rockfall splash leak across map edges. Those loops now gate candidates through `Level.adjacent(pos, adj)` before applying effects or collecting candidates. Added `test_trap_edge_wrap.gd` covering FlashingTrap, BlazingTrap, and ExplosiveTrap at a left-edge cell: the true same-row neighbour is affected while the wrapped previous-row cell is not. Local `git diff --check` clean; Godot import clean; full headless suite passed (2046 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Broader remaining trap/level-gen follow-ups are unchanged: full Pitfall delayed-collapse/PitRoom semantics, alternate builders/door graph repair, and any trap-specific upstream effect approximations not touched here.

## 2026-07-21

- Tags: spells, wild-energy, wands, artifacts, source-fidelity, tests
- Rebuilt Spell of Wild Energy on current upstream `WildEnergy.java`, removing an exploitable invented effect (backlog audit:S17 P2). Source check against `WildEnergy.affectTarget()`: current upstream is a pure recharge spell — it plays lightning/chargeup SFX, calls `ScrollOfRecharging.charge(hero)` (visual), `hero.belongings.charge(1f)` (an immediate +1 charge to every wand), `ArtifactRecharge.chargeArtifacts(hero, 4f)`, `Buff.affect(hero, Recharging.class, 8f)`, and `Buff.affect(hero, ArtifactRecharge.class).extend(8)`; it deals **no damage** and **never upgrades/transmutes an item**. The backlog's "emits damaging sparks" note was stale — verified there is no `Actor.findChar`/`ch.damage`/`NormalIntRange`/Lightning-arc code in the method. The port's `_cast_wild_energy()` previously (a) recharged each wand by a single fractional `recharge()` tick and (b) rolled a **30% chance to `upgrade()` a random backpack item** — an invented free-upgrade exploit with no upstream basis. Fix: dropped the random-upgrade roll entirely; each wand now gets an immediate `gain_charge(1)` (matching `belongings.charge(1f)`), the hero gets an 8-turn `Recharging` buff (matching `Buff.affect(hero, Recharging.class, 8f)`; the port's `Wand.recharge()` already reads this buff's `recharge_rate()` 4× multiplier on later turns), and the equipped artifact gains +4 `charge` clamped to `charge_max` (matching `ArtifactRecharge.chargeArtifacts(hero, 4f)`). Iterates `Belongings.get_items()` so wands inside a magical holster/bag are reached, mirroring `recharge_wands`. Updated the factory description to the recharge-only wording. Added `test_wild_energy_spell.gd`: an immediate wand charge, the 8-turn Recharging buff attaches, 60 casts on a full-charge wand never raise its level (exploit regression), and an equipped artifact's charge rises by 4. Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (2040 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented divergences: the port has no `ArtifactRecharge` accelerated-recharge buff (only the immediate +4 charge is applied, and the port's opaque 0–100 artifact charge scale doesn't map 1:1 to SPD's per-artifact charge), no lightning/chargeup SFX or SpellSprite CHARGE visual, and this remains a self-cast spell rather than upstream's `TargetedSpell` ballistica bolt (which in current SPD is cosmetic — the bolt cell has no effect).

- Tags: rings, sharpshooting, missile-weapons, source-fidelity, tests
- Made Ring of Sharpshooting missile-only, matching current upstream (backlog audit:S16 P2). Source check against `RingOfSharpshooting.java` + `MissileWeapon.java`: the ring's `Aim` buff has NO combat hooks; `MissileWeapon.min()/max()` add `RingOfSharpshooting.levelDamageBonus(hero)` to `buffedLvl()` (raising the missile's effective level, which widens its damage range) and `durabilityPerUse()` multiplies by `1.2^bonus` — it never touches accuracy and never touches melee. The port's `SharpshootingBuff` had generic `modify_accuracy(acc)=acc+bonus*2` and `modify_damage(dmg)=dmg+bonus` hooks, which `Char.accuracy()`/`Char.damage_roll()` iterate over for EVERY attack, so the bonus leaked into all melee swings and inflated melee accuracy. Fix: dropped both hooks (buff is now a pure marker), added static `Ring.sharpshooting_level_bonus(ch)` (reads the equipped ring's `bonus()` via the `RingOfSharpshooting` buff, 0/null-safe), refactored `Weapon` into reusable `_damage_range_for_level(lvl)` + `_roll_from_range(range, owner)` helpers (no public signature changes), and overrode `MissileWeapon.damage_roll(owner)` to roll from `_damage_range_for_level(buffed_lvl() + bonus)` so the ring raises missile effective level rather than adding flat post-roll damage. Updated the ring description (no accuracy claim). Added `test_ring_of_sharpshooting.gd`: a +3 ring lifts a level-0 dart from [1,10] to [4,16], melee `damage_roll()` is byte-identical before/after equipping a +4 ring, `accuracy()` is unchanged by a +5 ring, and the static helper returns the bonus level (0 without ring, null-safe). Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (2034 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.x). Documented divergences (unchanged this slice): missile durability is still an integer `uses_left` decrement rather than SPD's fractional `durabilityPerUse *= 1.2^bonus` accumulation, and SpiritBow (a separate simplified stub that fires via `shoot()` and extends `Weapon` not `MissileWeapon` here) does not yet route through the sharpshooting bonus.

- Tags: wands, corruption, buffs, source-fidelity, tests
- Rebuilt `WandOfCorruption.on_zap()` on the real upstream `WandOfCorruption.java` power-vs-resistance model, replacing the invented flat HP-ratio threshold (`corrupt_power = (level+1)*10 + debuffs*15` vs `resist = hp_max*hp_ratio`). Source check against current upstream: `corruptingPower = 3 + buffedLvl()/3`; `enemyResist = base * (1 + 4*(HP/HT)^2)` (100% HP → 5x, 50% → 2x, 25% → 1.25x), with per-type base overrides (Mimic/Statue `1+depth`, Piranha/Bee `1+depth/2`, Wraith `(1+depth/4)/5`, Swarm min 4, else 1). Each existing debuff weakens resistance — major debuffs `*0.5`, minor/other-NEGATIVE `*0.75` (constants `MAJOR_DEBUFF_WEAKEN 0.5`/`MINOR_DEBUFF_WEAKEN 0.25`, full major/minor id sets ported). If `power > resist` → `corruptEnemy` (CorruptionBuff, or **Doom** for corruption-immune/boss targets, matching SPD's `isImmune(Corruption) → Buff.affect(Doom)`); else `debuffChance = power/resist` rolls a weighted MAJOR (`Amok 3/Slow 2/Hex 2/Paralysis 1`) or MINOR (`Weakness 2/Vulnerable 2/Cripple 1/Blindness 1/Terror 1`) debuff at duration `6 + level*3`, skipping already-present/immune buffs and escalating minor→major→corrupt when the pool is exhausted. Already-corrupted/doomed targets force the major-debuff branch (`power = resist - 0.001`), as upstream. This removes the old hard boss early-return (bosses now resist via the formula and get Doom when overpowered) and the invented Terror+Weakness partial effect. `test_wand_of_corruption.gd` rewritten: injured-target full corruption, ally hunts mobs, low-HP boss → Doom (not ally/Corruption), full-HP enemy resists and instead gets a random debuff, and stacked major debuffs let a full-HP enemy be corrupted. `git diff --check` clean; full headless suite passed (2025 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Divergences: no Ascension-challenge resist term (challenge not ported, base stays 1); DwarfKing boss-badge disqualifier and wand SFX/`wandProc` on-hit effects not wired; `_base_resist` keys off `mob_id` strings rather than upstream `instanceof`.

- Tags: potions, experience, xp, source-fidelity, tests
- Restored Potion of Experience's full-level XP grant (backlog audit:S15 P3). Fable verified the `cron/weakness-encumbrance` theme first: the Weakness debuff and STR-encumbrance systems are already parity-correct against live current upstream — `Weakness` is a 20-turn FlavourBuff whose only combat effect is the `Char.attack()` `dmg *= 0.67f` multiplier (`Hero.STR()` does NOT subtract for Weakness), and the port's weapon/armor encumbrance already matches upstream exactly (`STRReq = (8 + tier*2) - floor((sqrt(8*lvl+1)-1)/2)`; accuracy/evasion `/pow(1.5, enc)`; weapon-delay `*pow(1.2, enc)`; armor-speed `/pow(1.2, enc)`; excess-STR damage bonus; surprise gated on meeting STR req), with Ring of Might baked into `str_val` and no AdrenalineSurge/Strongman content yet — so `str_val == Hero.STR()`. No fix was warranted there. The genuine narrow divergence found and fixed: `PotionExperience.drink()` granted only `xp_to_next - xp` (just the remainder to the next level, discarding partial progress), whereas upstream `PotionOfExperience.apply()` calls `hero.earnExp(hero.maxExp())` where `maxExp(lvl) == 5 + lvl*5 == ConstantsData.xp_for_level(lvl)` — a full current-level's worth of XP added on top of existing progress so the surplus carries into the next level. `drink()` now grants `ConstantsData.xp_for_level(hero.hero_level)`. Added `test_potion_of_experience.gd` (full-level grant preserves partial progress + overflow; zero-progress grants exactly one level with no overflow; a grant at MAX level triggers the existing surge-of-power Bless without overflow). Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (2019 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented divergence: the drink message is a port string rather than SPD's exact copy, and level-up SFX `levelup` is still unregistered (pre-existing, benign warning).

- Tags: traps, geyser, caves, city, halls, knockback, water, level-generation, source-fidelity, tests
- Implemented the missing upstream `GeyserTrap` and wired it into the Caves, City, and Halls weighted trap pools (SPD parity plan Phase 2, builder/trap weighting; backlog audit:S10 - this was the last omitted implemented-region trap alongside `Distortion`). Source check against current upstream `GeyserTrap.java`: Geyser is a teal diamond trap whose `activate()` (1) floods a radius-2 footprint with water via `buildDistanceMap` over non-solid cells - `dist < 2` always, `dist == 2` at `Random.Int(3) > 0` (about 2/3) - clearing fire; (2) for each of the 8 neighbour characters removes `Burning` (if alive) and knocks them back 2 tiles along an extended `Ballistica` trajectory via `WandOfBlastWave.throwChar(ch, traj, 2, true, true, source)`; (3) for the character on the trap cell picks a knockback direction (`centerKnockBackDirection` if set, else the hero avoids `Dungeon.level.avoid` cells, else random) and applies the same douse+knockback; and (4) scalds fiery enemies (fire elementals) for `NormalIntRange(5 + scalingDepth(), 10 + scalingDepth()*2)` - full at the centre, `x0.67` for pushed neighbours. The port now adds `src/levels/traps/geyser_trap.gd` (one-shot, normally hidden until revealed/triggered, teal): `_flood_water()` converts floor-like terrain (EMPTY/GRASS/HIGH_GRASS/FURROWED_GRASS/EMBERS/EMPTY_SP) to `WATER` over a Chebyshev radius-2 footprint (inner always, ring at 2/3), `_erupt_char()` strips `Burning`, knocks the char 2 tiles (neighbours away from the trap via a signed offset, the centre char via a settable `center_knock_back_direction` or first open direction) using the shared `Char.move_to()` passability/occupancy guards, and scalds `Elemental` (the port's only fiery char, matched by `mob_id == "elemental"` or a duck-typed `is_fiery()`) with the depth-scaled triangular `NormalIntRange` and `0.67` neighbour multiplier. Regional trap tables add Geyser last in source order after Gateway: Caves 31->32 slots, City 34->35, Halls 35->36 (Halls now omits only `Distortion`). Added `test_geyser_trap.gd` (water flood + Burning douse, neighbour + centre knockback direction/distance, fire-elemental scald, one-shot consumption + serialization) and updated `test_region_trap_tables.gd` / `test_halls_traps.gd` for the new slot counts. Local `git diff --check` clean; `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (1897 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented divergences (kept out of this narrow slice, consistent with prior trap slices): no `throwChar` wall-collision slam damage (knockback just stops at the first blocking cell), no hero-specific `Level.avoid` hazard-avoidance for the centre push (this port has no `avoid` map), water spread uses a Chebyshev footprint over floor-like terrain rather than `buildDistanceMap` over all non-solid cells, terrain fire-blob clearing and the splash/gas audiovisual effects are not modelled, and `HazardAssistTracker` mob hinting is absent (same omission as sibling traps).

- Tags: traps, pitfall, chasm, boss-levels, source-fidelity, tests
- Gave `PitfallTrap` the upstream "nowhere to drop" guard (SPD parity plan Phase 2, chasm/pitfall parity; backlog audit:S10 follow-up). Source check against current upstream `PitfallTrap.activate()`: it aborts with the `no_pit` warning when `Dungeon.bossLevel() || Dungeon.depth > 25 || Dungeon.branch != 0`, so a pitfall never functions on a boss floor or past the last droppable depth. The port previously always dropped the triggerer: on a boss level a hero would fall straight past the encounter (cheese), and at `MAX_DEPTH` the fall path relocated + applied landing damage instead of doing nothing. `pitfall_trap.gd._do_effect()` now checks a new `_pit_is_sealed()` helper (`GameManager.is_boss_depth()` or `depth >= ConstantsData.MAX_DEPTH`; branch checks omitted — this port has no level side-branches) and, when sealed, prints the upstream string ("the ground is too solid for a pitfall trap to work here.") and drops nothing while the trap is still consumed as a one-shot. Droppable-floor behavior (hero emits `hero_fell` → `"fall"` transition; non-levitating mob dies) is unchanged. Added `test_pitfall_trap_seal.gd`: proves a boss-depth pitfall neither kills a mob nor falls a hero (but is still consumed), a final-depth pitfall is likewise inert, and a normal floor still drops mobs (attributed to the pitfall) and falls heroes. Local `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (1822 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Remaining pitfall divergences (documented, not addressed here): SPD's delayed 3x3 collapse, heap-drop, and PitRoom landing semantics.

## 2026-07-20

- Tags: hud, ui, toolbar, buff-icons, source-fidelity, tests
- Moved another HUD chrome slice onto real SPD atlas assets (SPD parity plan Phase 4, learnability/UX parity). The HUD status overlay now uses the `status_pane.png` hero portrait socket and the 24x32 `avatars.png` class portrait frame instead of the generic placeholder, toolbar action buttons now use icon-only SPD atlas regions with tooltips while preserving mobile quickslot paging, and `BuffIcon` uses known regions from `buffs.png` for common buff/debuff ids before falling back to the old procedural marker. Added/updated HUD tests for atlas-backed status portraits, non-empty toolbar icon regions, icon-only compact behavior, and existing mobile safe-area fit.

Expanded `test_char_sprite_animations.gd` with attack-frame checks for those four sheets.

- Tags: traps, blobs, caves, confusion-gas, source-fidelity, tests

Source check against current upstream `ConfusionTrap.java`, `ConfusionGas.java`, and `CavesLevel.java`: Caves includes Confusion as the first rare trap after Guardian; the trap seeds `ConfusionGas` at `300 + 20 * scalingDepth`, and the gas prolongs `Vertigo` on its evolve tick. - Tags: mobs, invisibility, ai, source-fidelity, tests
- Fixed a chase-parity bug where drinking a Potion of Invisibility applied the `Invisibility` buff and incremented `hero.invisible`, but hostile mob detection ignored that counter and continued treating the hero as visible. `_find_visible_heroes()` now filters out invisible heroes, and `_act_hunting()` only attacks or refreshes `target_pos` when the target is actually detectable; an invisible target is pursued only to the last-known cell and then cleared. Added `test_mob_invisibility_targeting.gd` to lock visible-vs-invisible hero detection and the adjacent invisible target regression.

- Tags: plants, firebloom, icecap, blobs, warden, source-fidelity, tests
- Corrected Firebloom and Icecap to match upstream plant behavior, fixing a source-fidelity error introduced when both were dual-wired (immediate AoE **and** a lasting blob) during the earlier plant/trap→blob routing pass (SPD parity plan Phase 2; backlog audit:S19/S20). SOURCE CHECK against current upstream `Firebloom.java` and `Icecap.java` (`00-Evan/shattered-pixel-dungeon` master): the two plants are **opposites**. `Firebloom.activate()` only calls `GameScene.add(Blob.seed(pos, 2, Fire.class))` — the Fire blob (not the plant) applies Burning and burns flammable terrain on its own tick — and grants a Warden hero `FireImbue` for `FireImbue.DURATION*0.3`. `Icecap.activate()` instead loops `PathFinder.NEIGHBOURS9`, calling the **legacy one-shot** `Freezing.affect(cell)` on each non-solid cell (an immediate frozen-solid buff with fire/heap cleanup — it does **not** seed a blob) and grants a Warden `FrostImbue` for `FrostImbue.DURATION*0.3`. The port previously had Firebloom apply an invented direct Burning + direct adjacent-grass ignition (duplicating what the seeded Fire blob already does), and had Icecap seed an invented lasting `FreezingBlob` gas cloud (upstream leaves none — Icecap is a one-shot freeze), and neither plant granted the Warden imbue interaction. Fix: `src/plants/firebloom.gd` now only seeds `FireBlob` at its cell (drops the direct Burning + grass-burn loop) and gives a Warden hero a short `FireImbue` (`BASE_DURATION*0.3` = 9 turns); `src/plants/icecap.gd` now applies its immediate freeze over the column-safe, non-solid 3x3 footprint only (drops the `FreezingBlob` seeding entirely) and gives a Warden hero a short `FrostImbue` while skipping the Warden in the freeze loop (SPD's FrostImbue grants frost immunity). Rewrote `test_plant_blob_seeding.gd` to the corrected model (Firebloom seeds Fire + no immediate burn/grass, blob burns the hero on the next tick, Warden gets FireImbue; Icecap freezes immediately + seeds no blob, Warden gets FrostImbue + is not self-frozen, solid cells are skipped) and trimmed the now-moot Firebloom half of `test_plant_edge_wrap.gd` (Firebloom no longer iterates neighbours, so it has no edge-wrap surface; the Icecap edge-wrap guard remains). Local `godot --headless --path . --import` clean; full headless suite passed (1744 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Documented minor divergences: FireImbue/FrostImbue immunity is still not implemented (backlog S06), so a non-Warden hero standing in Firebloom's fire still burns on tick as intended, and Icecap's water-cell freeze-duration tripling from SPD's legacy `Freezing.affect` is not yet ported (kept the flat `FREEZE_DURATION`).

Verified upstream `CorrosionTrap.java`: `activate()` seeds a single `CorrosiveGas` at the trap cell with volume `80 + 5 * scalingDepth()` and strength `1 + scalingDepth()/4` (the NEIGHBOURS9 loop only tags nearby mobs with a `HazardAssistTracker` AI hint — no extra gas), then the blob spreads by diffusion and applies the real escalating `Corrosion` debuff on its own tick. The port previously applied `Ooze` to the triggerer, `Ooze` to cardinal-adjacent mobs, and a flat `2 + depth` direct hit — none of which is the SPD corrosion mechanic. `src/levels/traps/corrosion_trap.gd` now seeds one `CorrosiveGas` at `pos` (`80 + 5 * depth` volume, strength `1 + depth/4`, source `"CorrosionTrap"`) via `Level.add_blob()` and drops the Ooze/one-shot path; the already-implemented `CorrosiveGas.affect_char()` handles the `Corrosion` application on the shared blob timeline. Added `test_corrosion_trap.gd`: proves the trap seeds a live `corrosive_gas` blob (not Ooze/no one-shot damage), sets depth-scaled volume/strength, deactivates as a one-shot, and corrodes a standing hero on the next blob tick.

Verified upstream `PitfallTrap.java`: valid pitfall traps append a delayed pit effect and, when the hero is caught, call `Chasm.heroFall(...)`; landing damage/cripple happens later in `Chasm.heroLand()`, while non-hero mobs caught by the pit fall out through `Chasm.mobFall(...)`.
