- Tags: runestones, traps, stone-of-disarming, source-fidelity, tests
- Fixed the Stone of Disarming to actually disarm traps instead of applying a mislabelled `Weakness` debuff to an adjacent character (SPD parity plan Phase 2, environment/traps; backlog audit:S17). SOURCE CHECK (upstream `StoneOfDisarming.java` via the SPD wiki + gdx mirror): the stone is thrown at a cell, shadow-casts an FOV of radius `DIST = 8`, collects every **active trap** in view, sorts them by distance to the target (ties random), caps the list at the **nine** nearest, and neutralises each with `t.reveal(); t.disarm();` (plus a steam puff). It does NOT touch characters — the port's `Weakness`-on-a-bystander was the wrong item entirely (the *trap that disarms the hero's weapon* is the separate `DisarmingTrap`, already correct per S10). Implemented: added `Trap.disarm(level)` to the base trap (`src/levels/traps/trap.gd`) mirroring SPD `Trap.disarm()` → `Level.disarmTrap()` — sets `active = false`, flips a `TRAP`/`SECRET_TRAP` tile to `INACTIVE_TRAP`, and drops the trap from `Level.traps` so it can never fire again. Rewrote `Stone._use_disarming()` (`src/items/stones/stone.gd`) to gather active traps within `DISARM_DIST = 8` of the hero, sort nearest-first, `reveal()` + `disarm()` up to `DISARM_MAX_TRAPS = 9`, and report the count; empty case crumbles the stone with a message. SOURCE-FIDELITY NOTE: runestones in this port have no thrown-cell target yet (backlog S17 P1), so the effect is centred on the hero and uses a Chebyshev-distance radius rather than SPD's shadow-cast FOV — a documented approximation, strictly more correct than the prior Weakness behaviour, to be tightened when the Stone targeting pipeline lands. Also refreshed the factory description ("Disables malicious traps in the area, disarming up to nine at once."). Added `test_stone_of_disarming.gd` (registered in `run_tests.gd`): in-range traps (incl. a hidden one, which is revealed first) go inactive, leave the trap table, and flip to `INACTIVE_TRAP`; an out-of-range trap is untouched; the hero gains no `Weakness`; and the nine-trap cap holds when twelve are in range. Local `git diff --check` clean; full headless suite passed (1625 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: bombs, blobs, smoke-screen, smoke-bomb, seeders, source-fidelity, tests
- Wired the `SmokeScreen` blob to its canonical source, the alchemical Smoke Bomb (SPD parity plan Phase 2, slice: blob seeders/routing; backlog audit:S20 follow-up). Verified upstream `SmokeBomb.java`: it overrides `explosionRange() -> 2`, calls `super.explode(cell)` (so it deals the standard bomb blast damage), then `PathFinder.buildDistanceMap(cell, !solid, 2)` and pours `Blob.seed(i, 40, SmokeScreen.class)` into every reachable cell of the radius-2 blast (25 cells in the open — SPD's `1000 = 40*25` budget). Mapped into this port's typed-`Bomb` architecture rather than a new class: added `BombType.SMOKE` (appended to the enum so existing `bomb_type` int serialization stays stable) and a `SMOKE_SEED_VOLUME = 40.0` constant; `Bomb.create("smoke_bomb")` builds a radius-2 SMOKE bomb with the port's normal-bomb damage (10–30) to mirror the retained `super.explode()` blast; `_apply_area_effect()`'s new SMOKE case seeds `SmokeScreen` at 40 vol/cell across `Blob.blast_cells(level, bomb_pos, 2)` (the same passable-filtered, edge-safe footprint), so `Level.add_blob` merges them into one blob and the existing damage/terrain path in `detonate()` is preserved untouched. Registered `smoke_bomb` in `Generator.BOMBS` and its `ItemSpriteSheet` index (85) so it is discoverable. SOURCE-FIDELITY NOTE: the smoke bomb intentionally still deals blast damage — SPD's `super.explode()` call makes it damaging, not a harmless stealth item. Added `test_smoke_bomb.gd`: proves `Bomb.create`/`Generator.create_item` build a radius-2 SMOKE bomb, that detonation seeds a single `smoke_screen` blob over the full 25-cell footprint at 40 volume/cell (center and edge), that the seeded smoke blocks LOS through `Level.update_fov()`'s smoke branch, and that the SMOKE subtype + radius survive a bomb serialize/deserialize round-trip. Registered in `run_tests.gd` beside `test_smoke_screen.gd`. Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1595 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: blobs, smoke-screen, los, fov, source-fidelity, tests
- Added the `SmokeScreen` blob and lit up the previously-dead LOS branch (SPD parity plan Phase 2, slice: missing blob classes; backlog audit:S20). `level.gd` and the scene visual coordinator already referenced `smoke_screen`, but no class existed, so the LOS-blocking branch was dead. Verified upstream behaviour against Shattered Pixel Dungeon `SmokeScreen.java`: it is a bare `Blob` subclass with NO `evolve()` override and NO character effects — it only renders (a SMOKE speck emitter) and blocks vision. The vision blocking itself lives in `Level.updateFieldOfView`, which for non-ally viewers marks every cell where the smoke's `cur[i] > 0` as LOS-blocking. This port now mirrors that split: new `src/actors/blobs/smoke_screen.gd` (`blob_id = "smoke_screen"`, no `affect_char()`, inherits the base volume-conserving diffusion + a slow decay so the screen lingers a few turns), and `Level.update_fov()`'s smoke branch was corrected to walk each smoke blob's whole `active_cells` footprint and block every cell — previously it only blocked the entry's representative `pos` (`active_cells[0]`), leaving the rest of a spread screen see-through. Added `test_smoke_screen.gd`: proves the blob applies no buffs to a character standing in it (LOS-only), that a smoke cell blocks LOS to the cell directly behind it while the smoke cell itself stays visible, that a non-first active cell of a multi-cell screen also blocks LOS (proving the whole-footprint scan, not just `pos`), and that the blob round-trips through the structured `Level.serialize()/deserialize()` path as a live `SmokeScreen` with density intact. Registered in `run_tests.gd` beside the blob suite. REMAINING GAP (documented, not invented): no seeder yet — SPD seeds SmokeScreen from the alchemical Smoke Bomb (radius-2 bomb, 40 vol/cell); this port has a typed `Bomb` item but no SMOKE variant, so that seeder is a follow-up slice. Local `git diff --check` clean; full headless suite passed (1580 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: blobs, wells, water-of-health, seeders, source-fidelity, tests
- Wired the `WaterOfHealth` well seeder and made the blob source-faithful (SPD parity plan Phase 2, slice: blob seeders/routing; backlog audit:S20). The port had a `WaterOfHealth` class and scene visuals but no seeder — wells painted a `WELL` tile with no healing behind it — and the blob applied a hard-coded per-tick partial heal (3 HP, three debuffs) that diverged from upstream. In Shattered Pixel Dungeon `WaterOfHealth` is a `WellWater` blob seeded on the well tile at generation; it holds a single cell without spreading or decaying, and `affectHero()` fires only for the hero, fully restoring HP, cleansing the standard `PotionOfHealing.cure()` ailment set, then calling `WellWater.use()` to empty the well (the `WELL` tile becomes a spent `EMPTY_WELL`). This port now mirrors that: `WaterOfHealth` sets `spread_rate = 0`/`decay_rate = 0` so the well persists untouched until used; `affect_char()` guards on `ch.is_hero`, triggers when the hero is hurt or carries a curable ailment, heals to `hp_max`, detaches the curable set (Poison/Cripple/Weakness/Bleeding/Blindness/Burning/Ooze/Paralysis/Slow/Vertigo/Chill/Charm — the ids that exist as buffs in this port), then `_consume(ch.pos)` clears only the used well cell and flips that `WELL → EMPTY_WELL`. A new static `WaterOfHealth.seed_well(level, cell)` routes through `Level.add_blob`, so the well joins the shared blob layer (ticked by `advance_blobs`, saved by the structured blob-persistence contract), and `MagicWellRoom.paint()`/`SecretWellRoom.paint()` call it right after laying their center `WELL` tile. Because the heal rides the blob's own tick/effect path, there is no hard-coded terrain interaction (the hero.gd `WELL` case stays a no-op). Added `test_water_of_health_well.gd`: proves `MagicWellRoom.paint()` seeds a `water_of_health` blob anchored on the well cell; that standing on the well heals the hero to full and cures poison through the blob tick and then flips the tile to `EMPTY_WELL` and drops/removes that cell from the blob layer; that using one well preserves other wells in the same shared blob; that a mob standing on the well neither heals nor drains it; and that an unused well round-trips through `Level.serialize()/deserialize()` as a live `WaterOfHealth` with its cell, density, and non-spreading/non-decaying tuning intact. Registered in `run_tests.gd` beside the blob suite. Local `git diff --check` clean; parent-review full headless suite passed (1569 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: blobs, confusion-gas, potions, seeders, source-fidelity, tests
- Wired the ConfusionGas seeder and made the blob's effect source-faithful (SPD parity plan Phase 2, slice: blob seeders/routing; backlog audit:S20). ConfusionGas had a class and scene visuals but no seeder, and its `affect_char` diverged from upstream — it applied `Amok` to mobs and `Blindness` to the hero. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon seeds ConfusionGas from exactly one player-reachable source, `PotionOfLevitation.shatter()` (`GameScene.add(Blob.seed(cell, 1000, ConfusionGas.class))`), and its `ConfusionGas.evolve()` calls `Buff.prolong(ch, Vertigo.class, 2)` on every char in the cloud — hero and mob alike — so the gas disorients via Vertigo rather than blinding/amok, and the effect fires on the blob's evolve tick, not at seed time. This port's thrown Potion of Levitation previously granted a brief `Levitation` buff on shatter; it now seeds a `ConfusionGas` blob at the collision cell instead (via the existing `_seed_shatter_blob` helper, amount 5.0 to match the port's density-scaled ToxicGas/ParalyticGas potions rather than SPD's raw 1000), matching the same shatter → blob → effect precedent as the other gas potions. `ConfusionGas.affect_char()` now applies `Vertigo` (the existing debuff already wired into movement via `Vertigo.randomize_direction`) to all chars, topping it up to at least 2 turns per tick through `Buff.postpone` — mirroring SPD's `prolong(..., 2)` (extend, never shorten). Drinking Levitation is unchanged (still the float buff). Added `test_confusion_gas_seeder.gd`: proves the levitation shatter seeds a `confusion_gas` blob, that no Vertigo lands until the blob ticks (deferred, not instant), and that the blob vertigoes hero and mob alike through its own effect path. Registered in `run_tests.gd` beside the blob suite. Local `git diff --check` clean; parent-review full headless suite passed (1549 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: blobs, save-load, persistence, source-fidelity, tests
- Made level blob persistence honour the `Blob.serialize()` contract instead of duplicating live Node references (SPD parity plan Phase 2, slice: blob-layer persistence; backlog audit:S20). Previously `Level.serialize()` did `blob_entry.duplicate(true)` on `{blob:<live Blob Node>, pos}` and `Level.deserialize()` re-appended the raw dict, so `Blob.serialize()`/`deserialize()` were never called on the level path and a Node reference (not JSON-serializable) leaked into the save dict — the save only survived at all because Godot's `store_var` can serialize a whole Node subclass, which is fragile and bypasses the per-blob data contract. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon persists each `Blob` as data and rebuilds the concrete subclass via its bundling factory; this port mirrors the existing plant/trap idiom instead: `Blob.serialize()` now stamps `_script_path = get_script().resource_path`, a new static `Blob.create_from_data(data)` factory loads that script → `new()` → `deserialize()` (falling back to base `Blob` when the path is missing/invalid), and each subclass's `_init()` restores its own spread/decay tuning while `deserialize()` replays the active-cell densities. `Level.serialize()` now writes `{pos, blob: blob.serialize()}` per entry and `Level.deserialize()` reconstructs a live, level-linked Node through the factory — no Node reference ever enters the save dict. Preserves blob type/id, per-cell active densities, center/cell position, and the `_blob_time` cursor, so Fire/Freezing/Toxic/Paralytic/Confusion/WaterOfHealth all round-trip. Added `test_blobs.gd::_test_level_blob_round_trip`, which seeds FireBlob + ToxicGas in a level, ticks to spread, round-trips through `Level.serialize()/deserialize()`, and proves both blobs come back as fresh instances (not the saved Node), reconstruct the correct concrete subclass script, keep their active-cell counts and every per-cell density, and remain functional (evolve on the next tick). Local `git diff --check` clean; parent-review full headless suite passed (1542 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: blobs, traps, plants, routing, source-fidelity, tests
- Routed the fire/frost traps and plants through lasting blobs on the shared timeline (SPD parity plan Phase 2, slice: blob seeders/routing). Previously `FireTrap`/`FrostTrap` applied a one-shot direct hit (fire trap: flat damage + Burning + manual ember spread; frost trap: flat cold damage + one-shot Frozen + water hardening + adjacent-mob freeze) and the `Firebloom`/`Icecap` plants applied only their immediate AoE, so neither left the lingering Fire/Freezing gas that upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon relies on — the blob layer these effects should ride was under-wired. Upstream `BurningTrap.activate()` seeds `Fire` over the passable `NEIGHBOURS9` (3x3) footprint, `FrostTrap.activate()` flood-fills `Freezing` out to distance 2, `Firebloom` seeds `Fire` at its cell, and `Icecap` seeds `Freezing` over `NEIGHBOURS9`; in every case the seeded blob (not the trigger) is what applies Burning/Frozen, converts flammable terrain to embers, and hardens water as characters stand in it. This port follows the existing `ParalyticTrap → ParalyticGas` precedent: `FireTrap` now seeds a `FireBlob` (amount 2.0) over its 3x3 footprint and `FrostTrap` seeds a `FreezingBlob` (amount 5.0, scaled down from SPD's 20 for our density model) over a radius-2 footprint, both dropping their one-shot direct hits — the `FireBlob`/`FreezingBlob` effect passes (already implemented) handle Burning/ember/Frozen/water on tick via `Level.advance_blobs`/`tick_blobs`, preserving the conserved-diffusion behaviour. Footprints are enumerated through a new `Blob.blast_cells(level, center, radius)` helper that walks cells by `(x, y)` (never `center + offset`) and filters non-passable cells, so the seed pattern can never wrap across a map edge — the same row-wrap class the plant/blob edge tests guard against. `Firebloom` and `Icecap` additively seed their lasting blob (`FireBlob` at the cell; `FreezingBlob` over the 3x3) while keeping their immediate AoE, guarded by `has_method("add_blob")` so the lightweight edge-wrap fake levels and the direct-AoE regression guards are untouched; the immediate plant AoE is a documented minor divergence retained for parity with the existing behaviour. Rewrote `test_fire_trap.gd` to the blob model (seeds Fire, no direct damage, tick applies Burning + embers), added `test_frost_trap.gd` (seeds Freezing, no direct damage, tick freezes + hardens water) and `test_plant_blob_seeding.gd` (Firebloom/Icecap seed their blob and still apply the immediate effect). Local `git diff --check` clean, full headless suite passed (1523 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); existing Icecap/Firebloom edge-wrap and Halls frost-trap coverage still green.

- Tags: blobs, diffusion, volume-conservation, source-fidelity, tests
- Replaced the copy-outward blob spread model with SPD-style volume-conserving diffusion (SPD parity plan Phase 1, slice 4). The old `Blob._spread()` pushed `density * spread_rate * 0.25` into each cardinal neighbour with `max()`, minting fresh density at every frontier cell without removing it from the source — total blob volume grew every step, so a seeded gas plateaued/expanded instead of dissipating. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon's `Blob.evolve()` instead sets each open cell to the AVERAGE of itself and its open cardinal neighbours (`sum/count`) and subtracts a constant decay drain (`-1` in integer units), which conserves total volume across the field (averaging can never push a cell above its neighbourhood max, so the peak is non-increasing) and makes the total trend strictly downward until cells fall below threshold and prune out. This port now implements that in `Blob._evolve()`: it scans only the frontier (active cells plus the open cells they can flow into), computes every new value from a read-only snapshot before writing back (no mid-pass self-averaging), and sets `value = (sum/count) - decay_rate`, zeroing/pruning cells that fall to/below `min_density`. `spread_rate` is now a gate rather than a per-blob rate — SPD diffuses all gases uniformly — so `spread_rate == 0` blobs (webs, wells, timeline counters) route through a `_decay_in_place()` path that holds their shape and only decays, preserving existing non-spreading behaviour. `tick()` now applies character/terrain effects on the pre-diffusion field (so a char in freshly-seeded dense gas is hit at full strength) then evolves; row-wrap prevention (`_cardinal_neighbors`), wall blocking (`_blocks_spread`), effect overrides (fire terrain burn, freezing water/thaw, toxic/paralytic/confusion buffs), serialization, and `Level.advance_blobs()` timeline cadence are all unchanged. Extended `test_blobs.gd` with volume-non-explosion (12 steps, total never exceeds the seeded amount), decay-reduces-volume (strict per-step decrease), radial-symmetry/outward-monotonic shape, first-step conservation, and paralytic-gas coverage, alongside the retained spread/row-wrap/decay-prune/effect/serialize/timeline cases. Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1515 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: turn-system, scheduler, buffs, timeline, source-fidelity, tests
- Moved timed buffs onto the shared game timeline (SPD parity plan Phase 1, slice 2). Previously every `Char` ticked its buffs once per its own `act()` via `process_buffs()`, so buff duration burned down by the owner's action count, not by game-time: a Hasted actor (which acts twice as often) expired its own debuffs twice as fast in game-time and periodic effects (poison/burning/bleed) ran too often, while a Slowed actor's effects lingered too long. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon models buffs as `Actor`s on the shared `Actor.now` timeline, so a buff acts on a fixed game-time cadence regardless of its owner's speed. This port keeps a relative per-actor cooldown, so `TurnManager` now maintains a monotonic game clock (`_now`, advanced by the min cooldown consumed each step and exposed via `now()`, reset on `clear_actors`, and persisted in the schedule dict). `Char` gained `act_buffs()`, which each actor's turn (`Hero.execute_action`, `Mob.act`, and the special mobs/NPCs) now calls instead of `process_buffs()`: it advances buffs by the real game-time elapsed on the shared clock since that character last acted, ticking whole game-turns and carrying the sub-turn remainder (a Hasted actor's 0.5-turn actions accumulate to one tick every two actions). `process_buffs(elapsed_turns := 1.0)` remains the low-level primitive so its historical "one tick per call" contract (relied on by `test_fire_trap`/`test_combat_buffs`) is unchanged, and behavior at normal speed is identical (one tick per action). Existing buff serialization/source tracking is preserved, and `Char.serialize_char()` now persists the per-character timeline marker plus fractional pending time so reloads do not lose the first post-load elapsed slice. Added `test_buff_timeline.gd` (fractional-carry math, marker serialization, and an end-to-end scheduler run proving a Hasted actor takes ~2x the actions to expire a 20-turn buff, both a normal and a Hasted actor's buff expire at the same point on the shared timeline, and after 20 Hasted actions the buff is only ~half spent rather than expired by action count). Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1465 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: turn-system, scheduler, save-load, persistence, source-fidelity, tests
- Persisted the `TurnManager` scheduling timeline across save/load (SPD parity plan Phase 1, slice 1). Previously a save stored hero/level/mob state but not the scheduler, so on reload every actor re-registered with a zero cooldown: a just-acted, Slowed, or Hasted actor lost its place on the timeline and could act immediately again. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon persists each `Actor`'s timeline position (`Actor.time`) plus the global clock (`Actor.now`); this port uses a relative per-actor cooldown, so `TurnManager.serialize_schedule()` now saves each actor's cooldown/speed keyed by stable `actor_id` alongside the turn/round counters and current input actor. `SaveManager` writes this under `turn_manager`; on load it stages the schedule and the loading-scene transition re-links it by `actor_id` after all actors re-activate (`apply_pending_schedule()`), so cooldowns and counters resume where the save left off. Staging is single-use so a later level descent's clear+re-register never re-stamps stale cooldowns. Added `test_turn_manager_schedule_persistence.gd` (round-trips through `var_to_bytes`, proves just-acted/slowed/hasted cooldowns and counters survive a simulated reload, unsaved actors keep defaults, and relative ordering is preserved). Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1453 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: blobs, timeline, source-fidelity, tests
- Moved live blobs/gases from the hero-round hook onto the shared game timeline (SPD parity plan Phase 1, slice 3). Previously `Level.tick_blobs()` ran one blob step per `round_completed` (all registered heroes act once), so a gas cloud's spread/decay was tied to hero-action cadence rather than game-time: a single Hasted hero ticked blobs ~2x too fast, a Slowed hero ~2x too slow, and a co-op party ticked blobs once per party round regardless of how much game-time elapsed. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon models each `Blob` as an `Actor` evolving on the shared `Actor.now` clock, one step per game-time TICK independent of any actor's speed. This port keeps blobs out of the scheduler and instead adds `Level.advance_blobs(now_time)`, which catches blobs up to `TurnManager.now()` by running exactly one `_tick_blobs_once()` step per elapsed `TurnManagerNode.TICK` (with a `MAX_BLOB_CATCHUP_STEPS` safety cap). It tracks a per-level `_blob_time` cursor, persists it in the level save alongside the scheduler's `now`, snaps the cursor forward without ticking when the clock resets beneath it (level transitions reset `now()` to 0), and keeps the cursor current while no blobs exist so a later seed does not trigger a burst. `GameScene` now forwards `TurnManager.turn_processed` into `SceneFeedbackCoordinator.on_turn_processed()` so blobs advance after every actor turn and do not batch behind party rounds; `round_completed` remains responsible for round-based bombs. `tick_blobs()` is retained as a single-step compatibility wrapper for the existing manual-tick coverage. `test_blobs.gd` was extended for partial-tick gating, multi-tick catch-up, final-decay refresh, and blob-time serialization at the `Level` API level. Added `test_blob_timeline.gd`, which drives a real `TurnManager` timeline with Hasted/Slowed/normal/two-hero clock actors and proves the governing invariant (blob steps == `floor(now())`) across all speeds, that a Hasted actor under-ticks and a Slowed actor over-ticks relative to its action count, that equal game-time yields equal blob steps regardless of action rate, and that the reset-snap and save/load cursor persistence run no catch-up burst. Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1491 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). Follow-up (slice 4): spreading still uses the current copy-outward density model and needs SPD-style volume-conserving blob diffusion.

- Tags: rings, force, unarmed-combat, source-fidelity, tests
- Restored Ring of Force's unarmed damage path. The Force buff already carried the SPD-style STR/tier/level damage curve, but `Hero.damage_roll()` only used equipped weapon rolls and otherwise fell back to base bare-hand class damage, so unarmed Force behaved like weak unarmed damage plus the armed flat bonus. Unarmed heroes with Force now roll through `ForceBuff.force_damage_roll(str_val)` and skip reapplying that same Force buff as a flat armed modifier, equipped melee still receives the flat Force bonus, and thrown missiles skip the Force bonus to match SPD's non-missile armed bonus rule. Added `test_ring_of_force.gd`. Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1435 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: rings, speed, combat-pacing, source-fidelity, tests
- Split Ring of Furor and Ring of Haste into their SPD action domains. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon exposes Furor as an attack-speed multiplier (`1.09051^bonus`) and Haste as a movement-speed multiplier (`1.175^bonus`); the port had both flowing through `Char.get_speed()`, so Furor sped movement and Haste also sped attacks/non-movement actions because `TurnManager.spend_energy()` divides all hero action costs by speed. `FurorBuff` now uses a `modify_attack_delay()` hook, `HasteBuff` uses the current SPD movement multiplier only, and `Hero` pre-scales non-movement action costs so movement-only speed no longer leaks into melee, zaps, search, wait, or item actions. Extended `test_speed_modifiers.gd` to cover Haste movement-only behavior, Furor attack-only behavior, and combined scheduler cooldowns.

- Tags: buffs, weakness, source-fidelity, tests
- Corrected the Weakness audit item instead of landing a non-source STR penalty. Upstream current Shattered Pixel Dungeon keeps `Weakness` as a flavour debuff, applies its combat effect in `Char.attack()` as `dmg *= 0.67f`, and does not subtract Weakness inside `Hero.STR()`; the port already had the outgoing-damage multiplier, so the proposed `STR_PENALTY`/equipment-encumbrance change would have stacked a divergence. Updated `Weakness` to use SPD's 20-turn duration and removed the stale strength-penalty comments. Extended `test_combat_buffs.gd` to lock the 20-turn duration and 67% outgoing-damage behavior.

- Tags: rogue, search, secrets, source-fidelity, tests
- Restored Rogue's SPD-style wider intentional search reach. Upstream Shattered Pixel Dungeon gives Rogue a base search distance of 2 tiles while other classes search 1 tile before Wide Search/Foresight modifiers; the port always searched only adjacent cells, so Rogues did not actually detect traps/secret doors more easily despite the class perk text. `Hero._do_search()` now passes a class-specific radius into `Door.search()`, and `Door.search()` scans a bounded square radius without edge wrapping while preserving the default adjacent behavior for non-Rogues. Added `test_hero_search_radius.gd` for Warrior adjacent-only and Rogue two-tile secret door/trap reveal. Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1411 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

Spinner hunting now retargets under Amok, updates/uses `target_pos` like base `Mob._act_hunting()`, gives up when it reaches the last-known cell without sight, and still shoots webs/attacks adjacent visible targets as before. Narrow compact mode now uses single-letter `W`/`S` labels with full tooltips, while roomier compact and desktop labels are unchanged. - Tags: mobile, touch, camera, zoom, controls, tests
- Restored mobile pinch zoom after adding one-finger look-around. Pinch tracking previously lived only in `GameCamera._unhandled_input()`, while the new touch router handled look-around in `GameScene._input()`, so mobile/browser event routing could let drag work while starving the camera's zoom path. `GameScene` now forwards gameplay touch presses, releases, and drags directly to `GameCamera`'s touch handlers, consumes two-finger gestures before they become look-pan or tap-to-move input, and leaves HUD/modal touches isolated. Single-finger look drags also keep camera touch tracking current so a later second finger starts pinch from the live position. Extended touch-routing and camera zoom regressions to cover scene-routed pinch in/out, no one-finger pan fallback, and no movement submission after pinching. Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1372 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

The buff now samples only true in-bounds 8-neighbours and falls back to the intended destination if none are passable. Added `test_vertigo_edge_wrap.gd`. Extended `test_speed_modifiers.gd` with a scheduler regression for speed-up and slow-down changes after registration. Local full headless suite passed (1354 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

The next normal gameplay tap recenters the camera back to hero-follow before acting. Also added missing pinch-in coverage so mobile zoom-out remains tested alongside zoom-in. - Tags: gladiator, subclass, combat, source-fidelity, tests

Current source already routes landed melee attacks through `Char.attack()` -> `on_damage_dealt` and `GladiatorCombo.modify_damage()`, so the test now locks the intended simplified behavior: subclass application attaches the tracker, successive melee hits build combo, the finisher threshold boosts damage, the finisher starts a new chain, and combo state serializes. Marked the backlog item done with a follow-up noting that this remains an automatic simplified finisher rather than SPD's explicit combo-move picker. Local `git diff --check`, Godot import, and full headless suite passed (1273 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

## 2026-07-18

- Tags: mobs, respawn, vision, source-fidelity, tests
- Tightened regular-floor respawn placement so replacement mobs respect each hero's effective sight radius, not just the base `ConstantsData.VIEW_DISTANCE`. Offscreen mob respawn is intentional SPD-style balance, but the previous guard could allow a spawn at distance 9-10 for a Huntress or other extended-sight hero because `_is_valid_respawn_cell()` rejected only visible cells and cells within the base radius. Respawn placement now asks `hero.get_view_distance()` when available, preserving normal offscreen respawns while preventing visible/extended-vision pop-in. Extended `test_mob_spawn_positions.gd` with a Huntress sight regression and marked the stale backlog respawn item done.

- Tags: mobile, zoom, web-export, touch, tests
- Restored mobile web pinch zoom after the deploy shell patch had re-enabled browser-owned gestures. The GitHub Pages workflow was rewriting Godot's exported viewport from `user-scalable=no` to `user-scalable=yes` and setting `touch-action: auto`, so mobile browsers could consume two-finger pinches as page gestures before `GameCamera` received the touch stream. The web shell now keeps page zoom disabled and canvas touch handling claimed by Godot (`touch-action: none`), and `GameCamera._claim_web_canvas_touch_gestures()` now replaces the viewport content with one consistent no-page-zoom policy instead of appending potentially contradictory flags at runtime. Added a workflow regression to `test_game_camera_mobile_zoom.gd`.

- Tags: docs, mobile, combat-input, combat-pacing, follow-up
- Documented the remaining playtest follow-ups from the mobile attack/pacing fix in `backlog.md` and `active-context.md`: real-device verification for tap attacks/damage numbers, windowed/device tuning against SPD feel for visible mob pacing and possible action animation gating, and a separate online-only duplicate-damage-feedback investigation path if co-op still reproduces stacked numbers.

- Tags: mobile, combat-input, turn-pacing, source-fidelity, tests
- Fixed mobile tap attacks double-submitting through synthesized mouse events and restored readable visible-mob turn pacing. The touch path already suppressed generated mouse events in `_input`, but `_unhandled_input` could still receive a delayed left-click after `_handle_touch_tap()` had submitted the real attack, making a mobile tap appear to hit twice and stack feedback at the same cell. `GameScene._unhandled_input()` now applies the same synthesized-touch guard before dungeon click routing, and the suppression window is 1s to cover slower mobile/browser click synthesis. Input handled-marking is routed through a null-safe helper so bare headless scene tests stay clean under Godot 4.5. `TurnManager.MOB_ACTION_DELAY` is back to `0.1s`, matching SPD's short movement interval so visible mob moves/attacks do not all snap through instantly after the hero acts. Added a touch-routing regression for the tap-then-synthetic-click path.

- Tags: mobile, zoom, camera, combat-feedback, damage-numbers, tests
- Restored mobile settings zoom and de-duplicated scene combat feedback wiring. The settings window previously wrote `zoom_level` as an undeclared dynamic property and set `Camera2D.zoom` directly; `GameCamera`'s smoothing loop still held its old private `_target_zoom`, so the next camera update could pull the view back and make zoom-in appear broken. `GameManager` now owns and persists `zoom_level`, `GameCamera` initializes from that saved value, `WndSettings` applies zoom through `GameCamera.set_zoom_level()` so both immediate zoom and the smoothing target stay in sync, and the title settings Save button now calls the real display-settings persistence path instead of an old no-op `GameManager.save_settings()` check. Also made `GameScene._connect_signals()` idempotent for all EventBus hookups, preventing repeated setup paths from connecting damage handlers more than once and stacking multiple damage numbers on a single hit. Added regressions to `test_game_camera_mobile_zoom.gd` and `test_event_bus.gd`. Local Godot import and full headless suite passed (1246 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: multiplayer, network, run-config, host-authority, source-fidelity, tests
- Fixed a host/client run-config desync in online co-op. `NetworkManager._client_receive_online_run_start(config)` re-ran `_sanitize_run_config(config)` on the client, and that helper rebuilds `party_classes`/`player_infos` from the receiver's own `lobby_players` snapshot (only falling back to the passed config when local lobby is empty). The host already sanitizes the config in `start_online_run` before broadcasting it (and `_server_register_player` resends the same `active_run_config` to late joiners), so re-sanitizing on the client discarded the host's authoritative class-to-slot mapping in favor of the client's local lobby state. Any divergence between host and client lobby snapshots at run start — ordering, an in-flight class change, or a peer whose registration hadn't fully propagated — produced a different class-to-slot mapping per client and desynced the run. `_client_receive_online_run_start` now adopts the host payload verbatim via `config.duplicate(true)` (matching the deep-copy pattern of the sibling `_client_receive_*` handlers) and no longer calls `_sanitize_run_config` on the receive path; the host stays the sole sanitizer, preserving host authority. Added `test_network_run_config_authority.gd` (registered in the runner): with a client whose local `lobby_players` deliberately diverges from the host, the emitted `online_run_start_requested` config must match the host's `party_classes`, `chosen_class`, `run_seed`, and per-slot `player_infos`, and be an isolated deep copy. The test was confirmed to fail against the old re-sanitize behavior (3 failures) and pass with the fix. Full local headless suite green (1143 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); `git diff --check` clean.

- Tags: wands, corruption, warding, mobs, allies, ai, source-fidelity, tests
- Gave Wand of Corruption and Wand of Warding real effects backed by a shared Mob ally mechanism. Both wands were cosmetic: `WandOfCorruption.on_zap` set a bogus `alignment = "ally"` string (Mob has no `alignment` field, so the write was a silent no-op) and pinned a **permanent Amok** buff, which makes the "corrupted" mob attack the nearest character — frequently the hero — the opposite of an ally; the purpose-built `CorruptionBuff` class existed but had zero callers and its `on_attach` called a nonexistent `set_alignment`, while its `on_detach` re-entered `take_damage` (recursive death). `WandOfWarding.on_zap` only appended to a `_sentry_positions` array and printed a message — no sentry actor ever existed, so nothing zapped anything. Fix adds a gated ally mode to base `Mob` (`is_ally`/`ally_hero`), whose whole surface is behind `if is_ally` so non-ally mobs are untouched: `act()` routes allies to a new `_act_ally()` that mirrors the proven `SummonedElemental` loop (attack nearest visible hostile mob via `_find_nearest_enemy_mob()`, else follow the hero, else wander — never targeting the hero); `take_damage` returns 0 for hero-sourced friendly fire; `is_ally` is serialized and `ally_hero` re-linked from `GameManager.hero` on load. `CorruptionBuff` was rewritten to flip the target through that mechanism (`is_ally=true`, `xp_value=0`, clears Amok/Terror/Dread, no recursive detach damage), and `WandOfCorruption` now attaches `CorruptionBuff` on a successful roll and refuses bosses and already-allied targets (SPD parity). Warding gained a real `WardSentry` actor (`src/actors/mobs/special/ward_sentry.gd`): a rooted ally that each turn zaps the nearest hostile mob within `3+level` range for the wand's bolt damage, scales HP with wand level, grants no XP, is immune to the hero, and de-spawns the oldest sentry when the wand's `MAX_SENTRIES+level` cap is hit; it renders from the existing `wards.png` sheet via new `mob_sprite` entries. Removed the now-duplicate `ally_hero` declarations that `SummonedElemental`/`Bee`/`RoseGhost` inherited from Mob and marked those three `is_ally = true` so allies never target each other. Added `test_wand_of_corruption.gd` (corruption makes a real ally, grants no XP, drops Amok reliance, ally hunts hostile mobs and shrugs off hero damage, bosses immune) and `test_wand_of_warding.gd` (zap conjures exactly one live `WardSentry` on the level, tracks its position, and the sentry zaps a nearby hostile mob for damage on its turn), both registered in the runner. Full local headless suite green (1151 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups OPEN: enemy mobs don't actively target allies (they still focus the hero, matching existing SummonedElemental behavior); partial (resisted) corruption still applies Terror+Weakness rather than SPD's scaled amok/debuff; sentries persist on-level rather than expiring when the hero leaves the depth; hero melee/movement doesn't yet swap places with allied mobs.

## 2026-07-17

- Tags: mobile, hud, web, controls, viewport, tests
- Aligned mobile web content scaling with the HUD's visible-viewport layout source. HUD already preferred `window.visualViewport` so the toolbar/status layout could avoid browser chrome and safe-area clipping, but `GameManager._get_mobile_web_viewport_size()` still sized Godot's mobile content scale from `window.innerWidth/innerHeight`. On mobile browsers those can diverge, leaving the HUD laying out against one rectangle while the canvas/content scale uses another, which makes the already-sensitive portrait/landscape toolbar geometry flaky. `GameManager` now prefers `visualViewport.width/height` and falls back to `innerWidth/innerHeight`, matching HUD behavior while leaving non-Web and desktop fallback behavior unchanged. - Tags: mobile, hud, toolbar, quickslots, web, controls, tests
- Restored phone access to all six quickslots without widening the compact toolbar. Fable's review found that compact mobile hid quickslots 3-6 (`Toolbar._apply_button_labels`) and, unlike desktop, mobile has no keyboard fallback for `1`-`6`, so assigned items could become unreachable. `Toolbar` now keeps two quickslots visible at a time and adds a compact pager button that cycles 1-2, 3-4, and 5-6, preserving the 375px portrait width budget while making every slot tappable. Also switched web HUD viewport probing to prefer `window.visualViewport` over `innerWidth/innerHeight`, and registered web `visualViewport.resize`/`orientationchange` callbacks so toolbar/status layout refreshes immediately when mobile browser chrome or rotation changes the actually visible viewport. - Tags: scenes, turn-loop, mob-refresh, current-scene, source-fidelity, tests, audit-S28
- Fixed the SceneManager/`current_scene` desync that silently killed per-action mob refreshes (S28 P1). `SceneManager._do_transition` adds every screen via `root.add_child()` and never called `set_current_scene`/`change_scene_to_*`, so Godot's `SceneTree.current_scene` stayed pinned to the boot `MainScene` for the whole run. `TurnManager._get_game_scene_cached` read `tree.current_scene` and cached it as `_cached_game_scene`, so the `has_method("on_mob_action")` guard in `_process_mobs_async` was always false and the loop's only caller of `GameScene.on_mob_action` never ran — visible mob moves/attacks during the mob phase got no per-action FOV/sprite refresh (they only "snapped" on the next full turn refresh). Two-part fix: (1) `SceneManager._finalize_transition` now calls `tree.set_current_scene(new_scene)`, guarded on `new_scene.get_parent() == tree.root` (which `_do_transition`'s deferred `root.add_child` guarantees by the time the deferred finalize runs); the tree is resolved via `get_tree()` when SceneManager is in-tree, else `Engine.get_main_loop() as SceneTree`, so it also works in bare-instance/headless contexts where autoloads aren't mounted and `get_tree()` is null. Confirmed against Godot 4.5 that `set_current_scene` applies synchronously for a node already parented to root and does NOT free the previous current_scene. (2) `TurnManager._get_game_scene_cached` now prefers `SceneManager.current_scene` (set synchronously inside `_do_transition`, so it's correct a frame before `tree.current_scene` is mirrored) and falls back to the tree; and TurnManager resets `_cached_game_scene` on `SceneManager.scene_changed` via a new `_on_scene_changed` handler. The signal hookup is deferred from `_ready` (`_connect_scene_manager.call_deferred()`) because TurnManager registers before SceneManager in the autoload order, so the `SceneManager` global is null during TurnManager's own `_ready`. Net: the engine's `current_scene` and `SceneManager.current_scene` now agree after every transition, and the turn loop resolves the live GameScene (self-healing across floor descents, since old scenes are freed → `is_instance_valid` false → re-resolve). Added `test_scene_transition_current_scene.gd` (+ `_stub.gd` GameScene stand-in exposing `on_mob_action`), registered in the runner: `_finalize_transition` mirrors a probe scene into `tree.current_scene`; TurnManager resolves the SceneManager-tracked scene and confirms it exposes `on_mob_action`; and `_on_scene_changed` clears the cached scene. Full local headless suite green (1093 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups still OPEN under S28: MainScene leak (first-transition `queue_free` skipped, P2), no transition re-entrancy guard (P2), `scene_changed` still lacks non-TurnManager consumers e.g. per-screen audio (P2, now partial), no full `go_to` free/meta test since add_child/free run deferred and the synchronous runner can't step frames (P2, now partial), `go_to` null-guard (P3), and the meta-vs-direct-prop parameter split (P3).

- Tags: hud, minimap, fov, source-fidelity, tests, audit-S35
- Made the minimap own its `level_changed` refresh instead of racing HUD-side population against the minimap's own clear handler. `Minimap._on_level_changed()` now repopulates from `GameManager.current_level`, focused hero, current visible/visited arrays, mobs, and party positions when level state exists; it only clears to unexplored when there is no active level. `HUD._on_level_changed()` now updates HUD labels/status only, removing the duplicate minimap writer. This preserves SPD-style live floor awareness by keeping the minimap populated immediately after floor transitions regardless of signal ordering. Added `test_minimap_refresh.gd` and registered it in the headless runner. Local Godot import passed; full headless suite green (1089 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1). `gdparse` is not installed locally.

- Tags: weapons, enchantments, curses, procs, source-fidelity, tests, audit-S13
- Wired curse weapon enchantments end to end (S13 P2). Two halves of the same feature were dead: (1) `WeaponEnchantment.proc()` `match enchant_id` listed only the 13 non-curse ids, so the fully-implemented `_annoying_proc`/`_sacrificial_proc`/`_displacing_proc` bodies were never reached — any curse enchant just fell through to `return damage`; and (2) `WeaponEnchantment.random_curse()` had zero callers repo-wide, so no code path ever produced a weapon carrying a curse enchantment. Even a cursed loot weapon (`Weapon.random()`, `effect_roll < 0.3`) only set `cursed = true` and left `enchantment == null`, unlike SPD where a cursed roll also does `enchant(Enchantment.randomCurse())`. Fix is minimal and source-faithful: added the three ids that have proc bodies (`annoying`, `sacrificial`, `displacing`) to the `proc()` match so they dispatch, leaving the curse ids without implementations (dazzling/explosive/wayward/polarized/friendly) to keep falling through as no-ops; and added `enchant(WeaponEnchantment.random_curse())` to `Weapon.random()`'s cursed branch. Non-curse generation and probabilities are untouched — the good-enchant branch still fires at `effect_roll >= 0.85` via `random()`, and the cursed threshold stays `< 0.3`. Added `test_curse_weapon_enchantments.gd` (registered in the runner): `random_curse()` over 40 rolls only ever returns `is_curse` ids in `CURSE_IDS`; `create()` flags the three implemented curse ids; a cursed weapon from `Weapon.random()` now carries a curse enchantment that is itself `is_curse`; `_sacrificial_proc` dispatches (returns 1.3× damage and deals self-damage to a stub attacker via `take_damage`); `_displacing_proc` dispatches (teleports a stub defender to the stub level's `random_passable_cell`); `_annoying_proc` still emits `EventBus.enchantment_proc('annoying')`; and a non-curse proc (`shocking`) still routes and adds its bonus damage. Full local headless suite green (1086 checks, 0 failures, Godot); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups still OPEN under S13: the remaining five curse enchants have no proc implementations (their `create()`/color/`is_curse` metadata exists but `proc()` is a no-op for them), and the separately-tracked simplified non-curse enchant behaviors vs SPD (`_shocking_proc` chain-lightning, `_kinetic_proc` overkill storage, `_grim_proc` curve) remain open.

- Tags: armor, glyphs, buffs, haste, rooted, source-fidelity, tests, audit-S13
- Gave FlowGlyph and EntanglementGlyph real effects (S13 P2). Both glyphs hand-built a bare `Buff.new()` and stamped a `buff_id` string on it — Flow set `buff_id="Haste"`, Entanglement set `buff_id="Rooted"` — instead of instantiating the real buff classes the way the sibling `SwiftnessGlyph` already does (`Haste.new()`). Because the base `Buff.modify_speed()` returns speed unchanged, Flow's water-proc "haste" was completely inert: the wearer got a status entry that `has_buff("Haste")` could find but that never actually sped them up. Entanglement's fake "Rooted" was subtler — the hero's `_do_move` root gate keys on `has_buff("Rooted")`, so movement *was* blocked, but the buff was a NEUTRAL non-debuff with the wrong typing/description and none of the real `Rooted` class's behavior. Fix mirrors `SwiftnessGlyph` exactly: `FlowGlyph` now does `var haste: Haste = Haste.new(); haste.set_duration(2.0 + level)` and `EntanglementGlyph` does `var root: Rooted = Rooted.new(); root.set_duration(2.0 + level)` — the same `Rooted` class already used by the gripping trap and stormvine plant, so the debuff is genuine, findable by class, and duration still scales with armor level. No behavior removed: Flow still only procs on WATER terrain, Entanglement still counts adjacent grass tiles and grants `(2+level)*grass_count` shielding via `add_shielding`. Added `test_flow_entanglement_glyphs.gd` (registered in the runner): with a dummy char/level, the Flow proc on water attaches a real `Haste` (asserting `buff is Haste` and `modify_speed(1.0) == 3.0`, which an inert `Buff` fails), the Entanglement proc attaches a real `Rooted` whose `is_debuff` is true, both durations equal `2 + armor.level`, and adjacent grass still adds shielding — 9 checks. Full local headless suite green (955 checks, 0 failures, Godot); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups still OPEN under S13: no curse armor glyphs exist, and `has_curse_glyph()` is always false because `ArmorGlyph` has no `is_curse` property.

- Tags: armor, glyphs, loot, generator, source-fidelity, tests, audit-S13
- Made random loot armor able to spawn with glyphs (S13 P1). `Armor.random()` — called by `Generator` when spawning armor loot (`generator.gd:492`) — rolled a `+0/+1/+2` level, then a single `effect_roll`: `<0.3` cursed, `>=0.85` "good glyph". Both effect branches were stubs (`cursed = true` with a "curse glyph would go here" comment, and a bare `pass` on the good branch), so **every** piece of loot armor came out glyph-less; the only way to get a glyphed armor in the whole game was Scroll-of-Enchantment `inscribe()`. The 12 fully-implemented common glyphs (Obfuscation/Swiftness/Viscosity/Stone/Repulsion/Affection/Anti-Magic/Thorns/Potential/Brimstone/Flow/Entanglement) and `ArmorGlyph.random()` already existed and were used by `upgrade_armor(inscribe_glyph=true)` — they were simply never reached from loot generation. Fix is one line: the good branch now calls `inscribe(ArmorGlyph.random())`, exactly mirroring the already-correct `Weapon.random()` → `enchant(WeaponEnchantment.random())` sibling path (`weapon.gd:279-282`). Roll thresholds, the `+0/+1/+2` level distribution, and the curse/glyph mutual exclusivity are unchanged; the cursed branch stays `cursed = true` with **no** curse glyph because no curse-glyph set exists in the port yet (that's the separate `[P3][audit:S13]` "no curse armor glyphs" item, along with the always-false `has_curse_glyph()` and the inert Flow/Entanglement raw buffs — all left OPEN). Also refreshed the now-stale docstring/comments that described the missing "full glyph system." Added `test_armor_random_glyphs.gd` (registered in the runner): over 400 seeded `random()` rolls, some armor gains a valid non-empty-id `ArmorGlyph` and some is cursed (proving both branches are live); across another 400 rolls no cursed armor ever carries a good glyph (branches stay exclusive); `degrade()` preserves an inscribed glyph and only lowers level/str req; and an inscribed glyph round-trips through `serialize()` → `deserialize()` with its id intact. Full local headless suite green (944 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups still OPEN under S13: curse armor glyphs don't exist, `has_curse_glyph()` is always false (no `is_curse` on `ArmorGlyph`), and FlowGlyph/EntanglementGlyph still apply inert hand-built `Buff.new()` instances instead of real `Haste`/`Rooted`.

- Tags: huntress, spirit-bow, weapons, serialization, save-load, tests, audit-S13
- Pinned the Spirit Bow save/load fix with a regression test (S13 P1). The bug itself — `SpiritBow.deserialize()` being a no-op `pass` that discarded the whole saved payload, so the Huntress's bow lost its upgrade level / augment / enchantment / curse+id flags on every load (belongings restores an equipped item via `Generator.create_item('spirit_bow')` + per-item `deserialize()`) — was already corrected in commit c45362c (WS0 quick-wins, PR #2) by replacing the override with `super.deserialize(data)`. `SpiritBow` carries no state of its own beyond the shared `Weapon`/`Item` base (its damage scales off live hero level, not a stored field), so chaining to super is the complete and correct fix; no further code change was needed this slice. Added `test_spirit_bow_serialization.gd` to lock it (registered in the runner): the core `serialize → Generator.create_item('spirit_bow') → deserialize` round-trip preserves upgrade level, `level_known`, and the resulting damage range; augment (`DAMAGE`) and enchantment (id `blazing`) survive; curse/`cursed_known`/`identified` flags survive; the equipped `spirit_bow` belongings slot round-trips level+augment through the real `Belongings.serialize/deserialize` path; and a Huntress starting-kit save/load keeps an upgraded (+5) bow. Verified the test genuinely guards the regression — 12 failures when `deserialize()` is temporarily reverted to `pass`, 0 after. Full local headless suite green (933 checks, 0 failures, Godot); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups still OPEN (separate from this data-loss item): the broader Spirit Bow feature parity — `shoot()` is a simplified virtual-projectile stub with no real `SpiritArrow` entity, and SPD's spirit-hunter aim/followup mechanics aren't ported.

- Tags: geometry, ballistica, pathfinder, shadow-caster, fov, tests, bugfix, audit-S22
- Backfilled the geometry core with focused headless tests and fixed a real row-edge-wrap bug it exposed (S22). Added three test cases (registered in `run_tests.gd`): **`test_ballistica.gd`** — stop-flag semantics on a hand-verified 10-wide grid (STOP_SOLID halts in the cell *before* a wall; STOP_CHARS/STOP_TARGET halt *on* the cell; MAGIC_BOLT sails through the aimed cell to the next obstacle/edge; WONT_STOP degrades collision to the last cell), diagonal `x==y` walk, and Chebyshev `distance`; **`test_pathfinder.gd`** — corner/edge neighbor sets with no column wrap, straight-path length, unreachable across a full wall column (`find_path`/`find_step`), diagonal corner-cut rejection, and the BFS `build_distance_map` flood (source 0, cardinal step 1.0, walled cells INF); **`test_shadow_caster.gd`** — origin + all 8 neighbors lit, circular radius cutoff (cardinal cell at radius visible, one past it not), and wall occlusion (wall cell visible, cell directly behind occluded, off-axis clear). The Ballistica tests surfaced a genuine bug: `cast()` traced the ray by incrementing a **flat** index and re-derived `x = cell % width` in `_inside_map`, so a horizontal (or near-horizontal) ray reaching the east/west border wrapped its index onto the adjacent row (x=width-1 → +1 → x=0, y+1) and kept tracing there instead of terminating at the map edge. Reachable in gameplay via `wand.gd:148`, where an unidentified wand fires MAGIC_BOLT through `_make_open_passable()` — an all-open grid with no border walls to STOP_SOLID against — so a bolt aimed at a map edge would wrap onto the next row. Fixed by tracking true `cur_x`/`cur_y` alongside the flat `cell` through the DDA (mirroring SPD's `Ballistica.build()`, which iterates 2D coords) and bounding the loop on those coordinates; removed the now-dead `_inside_map` helper. Regression pinned by a `test_ballistica.gd` assertion that every cell of a horizontal WONT_STOP ray stays on its origin row. Full local headless suite green (917 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups still OPEN under S22: the entire hand-written `Pathfinder` A* remains dead code (runtime pathing uses Godot `AStar2D`), so these tests lock the *contract* of currently-unwired helpers; `build_distance_map` still uses non-uniform 1.0/√2 costs vs SPD's uniform-cost integer BFS; `Ballistica.IGNORE_SOFT_SOLID` stays inert until `avoid[]` lands; and the cosmetic `shadow_caster.gd` integer-division annotation was left untouched.

- Tags: npcs, quests, blacksmith, items, loot, source-fidelity, audit-S21
- Made the Troll Blacksmith quest completable (S21 P1). `blacksmith.gd` required 15 `dark_gold_ore` and called `Belongings.count_item`/`remove_item_quantity`/`remove_item_by_id` on that id, but repo-wide grep found `dark_gold_ore` **only** in `blacksmith.gd` — no generator branch, loot table, or terrain produced it, so the hero could never deliver and both the quest and its reforge reward were dead. The `_spawn_blacksmith` comment ("Dark gold ore drops from bats naturally — handled by bat loot tables", `quest_handler.gd:126`) described a source that didn't exist. Fix in two small pieces: (1) `Generator` now knows the item — a new `_make_stackable_misc_item()` helper builds a stackable, pre-identified, non-unique MISC material (mirroring `_make_misc_item` but stackable so pieces pool and merge), and `create_item("dark_gold_ore")` dispatches to it (`generator.gd`); (2) Caves bats carry it — `Bat.loot_table = [{"item_id": "dark_gold_ore", "chance": 0.5}]` (`bat.gd`), which flows through the existing `Mob._drop_loot()` → `Generator.create_item` → `Level.drop_item` pipeline with zero pipeline changes. Because the ore is stackable, `Belongings.add_item` merges every bat drop into one backpack stack, and the blacksmith's existing count/consume calls Just Work. Added `test_blacksmith_ore_quest.gd`: ore is a real stackable pre-identified MISC item; 15 separate pickups pool to `count_item == 15` in a single merged slot and `remove_item_quantity(15)` clears it; the bat loot table lists ore with a positive chance; a forced-chance `_drop_loot()` places exactly one ore heap at the bat's cell through the real mob pipeline; and the blacksmith's `_count_ore`/`_take_ore` count then fully consume the required 15 (via lightweight hero/level stubs, no UI). Registered in the runner. Full local headless suite green (878 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-ups OPEN: this port sources ore from bat drops rather than SPD's Pickaxe + Caves mining terrain, and the 0.5 per-kill chance / 15-piece gate are gameplay-tuned, not SPD-exact; the broader Blacksmith parity gap (Pickaxe/mining, CRYSTAL/GNOLL quest variants + boss, favor system, harden/upgrade/smith reward options — currently a single reforge) remains the separate S21 P2 item.

- Made same-tier weapons mechanically distinct (S13 P1). Previously every weapon in a tier shared one profile: `delay_factor` defaulted to 1.0 and was never set in any factory branch, and damage came purely from `tier`/`level` in `Weapon.get_damage_range()`, so a Dagger and a Cudgel differed only in name and (for spear/glaive) reach. Added two shared `Weapon` fields — `damage_multiplier` (scales the whole tier/level damage range on top of the augment multiplier) and `str_req_bonus` (added to the tier-derived STR requirement) — and wired them into `get_damage_range()` and `get_str_requirement()`, alongside the already-plumbed-but-unused `delay_factor` that flows through `speed_factor()`. New saves serialize/deserialize the profile fields, while legacy saves without those fields keep the current factory archetype instead of flattening old heavy/fast weapons back to neutral. `MeleeWeapon.create` now assigns a source-fidelity archetype per weapon: **heavy** (cudgel, hand_axe, mace, battle_axe, flail, war_hammer, greataxe) = slower DLY + higher damage + `+1` STR; **light/fast** (gloves, dagger, dirk, sai, rapier, scimitar, assassins_blade) = lower DLY + lower damage + `−1`/`0` STR; **defensive** shields (round_shield, greatshield) = slower + `+1` STR; reach polearms (spear, glaive) keep the neutral profile; and the six "standard" weapons (worn_shortsword, shortsword, sword, longsword, greatsword, runic_blade) stay at the exact pre-change baseline (multiplier 1.0, delay 1.0, bonus 0) so nothing regressed for them. `MissileWeapon.create` got a light matching pass (throwing_club/throwing_stone heavier, shuriken faster) since the fields live on the shared base. Added `test_weapon_differentiation.gd`: worn_shortsword baseline unchanged (1-10 dmg, STR 10), ≥4 distinct delays / ≥4 distinct max-damage / ≥2 distinct STR reqs across the five tier-1 melee weapons, heavy>standard>light ordering through the combat-facing `get_damage_range`/`speed_factor`/`get_str_requirement` APIs, factory-cached `str_requirement` matching the live value, a serialize→deserialize round-trip of the new fields, legacy-load preservation of a heavy factory profile, and a missile club-vs-dart diff — registered in the runner. Full local headless suite green (862 checks, 0 failures, Godot 4.7.1); `git diff --check` clean. Follow-up still OPEN: per-weapon ACC factors and unique abilities (rapier lunge, sai combo, dagger surprise-damage bonus) are not yet ported — only DLY/damage/STR differentiate.

- Tags: bags, inventory, pickup-routing, magical-holster, source-fidelity, audit-S18
- Made bags actually work in play (S18), closing the two remaining bag items. **Pickup routing**: `Belongings.add_item()` now looks for a held bag whose `can_hold` accepts the incoming item (`_bag_that_accepts()`) and, if found, stores the item inside that bag — still firing `on_pickup` — before it would ever consume a top-level backpack slot (`belongings.gd`); a bag never nests inside another bag. A full bag falls back to the normal backpack path, and stackable pickups merge into an existing bag stack even when the bag is at slot capacity — achieved by splitting `Bag.can_hold`/`add_to_bag` into a category test (`accepts_category`) and a separate capacity/stacking test so a merge never needs a free slot (`bag.gd`). **Lookups stay whole**: added `Belongings._all_backpack_items()` (backpack plus every bag's contents) and routed `get_items`, `find_item`, `find_item_by_id`, `count_item`, `has_item`, `remove_item`, `remove_item_by_id`, and `remove_item_quantity` through it, with removals resolving to the correct container via `_remove_item_object()`. This matters for correctness: seeds route into the Velvet Pouch, and the wandmaker seed quest (`count_item`/`remove_item_quantity`) plus wand recharge/XP scans (`get_items`) would otherwise no longer see them. **Magical Holster**: a new serialized `accepts_missiles` flag makes `accepts_category()` admit any `MissileWeapon` alongside the `WAND` category while still rejecting melee weapons that share the WEAPON category (`bag.gd`). Added `test_bag_pickup_routing.gd` — routing into the pouch, in-bag stacking, full-bag fallback to the backpack, find/count/remove reaching bag contents (partial + final removal), and holster wand/missile acceptance with melee rejection and flag survival across serialize/deserialize — registered in the runner. Full local headless suite green (836 checks, 0 failures, Godot). Follow-ups: no inventory UI yet to open a bag or move items between bag and backpack; acquiring a bag does not retroactively sweep already-held matching items into it (SPD does).

- Tags: bags, inventory, serialization, save-load, source-fidelity, audit-S18
- Closed the latent bag data-loss bug (S18): `Bag.serialize()` already wrote its `items` array, but `Bag.deserialize()` discarded it (a stale comment claimed "items are deserialized by the item loading system" — nothing did), so every bag would silently empty on save/load the moment bags went live. `Bag.deserialize()` now reads `data["items"]`, recreates each entry via `Generator.create_item(id)`, per-item `deserialize()`s it, and appends directly to `items` (bypassing `can_hold`/`add_to_bag` so a round-trip preserves exact contents even if capacity/category rules change) — `bag.gd`. Added `test_bag_serialization.gd` (round-trip preserves two mixed items with concrete scripts, generator-backed reload mirroring how `Belongings` rebuilds a backpack bag, empty-bag round-trip, and category preservation), registered in the runner. Full local headless suite green (809 checks, 0 failures, Godot). Follow-up still OPEN: bags are not yet wired into `Belongings` (no `bags` array, pickups don't auto-route via `can_hold`), so bag containers remain non-functional in play — this slice only closes the serialize/deserialize data-loss half; `magical_holster` missile-weapon `can_hold` branch also still open.

- Tags: rings, artifacts, damage, gold, source-fidelity, audit-S16
- Revived two dead S16 effects. **Cape of Thorns**: its `on_hero_damaged` charge/absorb/reflect logic existed (`artifact.gd`) but `Char.take_damage` never called it, so the artifact never charged, absorbed, or retaliated. Wired the hook surgically from the damage pipeline — after computing applied damage, `take_damage` reads `get("belongings").get_equipped_artifact()` (heroes only; base Chars have no `belongings` and skip it) and folds the hook's return value back into the applied damage (`char.gd`). Also added the "thorns" retaliation the description promised: when the shield is active, the absorbed amount is reflected onto a living `Char` attacker (`artifact.gd`). **Ring of Wealth**: `WealthBuff` existed but nothing read `bonus()`, so it was inert. Added `Gold.wealth_adjusted_quantity(hero)`, which scales a gold pile by the wielded ring's `1.2^bonus` (queries `RingOfWealth`→`WealthBuff.ring.bonus()`, same buff-lookup pattern wands use for Ring of Energy); `Gold.on_pickup` now collects the adjusted amount and forwards the collector hero to `GameManager.add_gold` (`gold.gd`). Added `test_cape_of_thorns.gd` (charges from hits, no reduction un-activated, 50% absorb + attacker reflection when active) and `test_ring_of_wealth.gd` (no-ring/null passthrough, 1.2× and 1.2³ scaling, unequip clears, real pickup path updates gold/stat totals), registered both. Full local headless suite green (796 checks, Godot 4.7.1). Follow-ups: wealth's rare-drop / mob-loot boost is still unimplemented (`Mob._drop_loot()` has no hero context — gold-pile multiplier only); the Furor/Haste attack-vs-move split remains open.

- Tags: hero, mage, weapons, wands, source-fidelity, audit-S02
- Replaced the Mage's placeholder worn shortsword start with a real `MagesStaff` slice. `Generator.create_item("mages_staff")` now creates the Mage's unique, no-bones, zero-value staff with SPD's reduced tier-1 melee max damage, default `ZAP`, and an identified/uncursed Wand of Magic Missile imbued at the staff's +1 max-charge baseline. The hero wand-zap path now accepts an equipped non-wand item that exposes `zap()`, so the held staff can cast through the same action pipeline as backpack/equipped wands. Added `test_mages_staff.gd` covering generator construction, source-fidelity properties, equipped-staff zap charge spending, staff/belongings serialization, and the Mage starting kit. Full local headless suite green (779 checks, Godot 4.7.1). Follow-ups: staff upgrade/imbue UI/procs are still incomplete, and the staff max-charge bonus is static in this slice rather than a dynamic wrapper around future wand level recalculation.

- Tags: backlog, reconciliation, docs, audit-S01, audit-S06, audit-S14, audit-S16, audit-S18, audit-S20, audit-S24, audit-S26
- Reconciled stale audit tags in `docs/memory/backlog.md` against current `src/`+`tests/` (docs-only; no code touched). Verified each item in source and by its committed/uncommitted headless test before flipping the tag. Marked **[DONE]**: S01 atomic save write + `.bak` rotation (`save_manager._write_atomic_var`, `test_save_manager.gd`) and save migration path (`SAVE_VERSION=2`, `_migrate_save`/`_migrate_v1_to_v2`); S06 Fury single-application (`test_fury_damage`), `get_speed()` folding `modify_speed` (`test_speed_modifiers`), Doom as permanent +67% damage-taken amp (`test_combat_buffs`), Frozen one-potion loop dedent (`test_frozen`), Barkskin/ArcaneArmor interval-gated decay (`test_interval_armor_buffs`), Barrier `get_shielding`/`absorb_damage` unification (`test_barrier_shielding`); S14 wand per-turn recharge (`test_wand_recharge`), WandOfFrost `was_chilled` gate (`test_wand_of_frost`), wand use-to-id refill via `on_hero_gain_exp` (`test_wand_use_identification`), WandOfDisintegration routed through `take_damage` (`test_wand_of_disintegration`); S16 ring/artifact passive rebuild on load via `resolve_post_load` and Ring of Might non-persistent buff + clean-base serialize (`test_ring_of_might_state`); S18 ordinary LOCKED_DOOR unlocks with iron keys (`test_door_key_gating`); S20 `Level.tick_blobs()` driven per round + Fetid Rat `add_blob` arg fix (`test_blobs`); S24/S26 depth transitions consolidated onto `GameManager.descend()/ascend()` restoring the `MAX_DEPTH` cap, plus S26 `quest_flags` now persisted. Left **OPEN** (partial/unverified): S01 per-floor-transition autosave (only app-close/pause + continue autosave are wired; a fresh descend/ascend still doesn't autosave); S13 random-loot armor glyphs (`Armor.random()` still `pass`-stubs both glyph branches — only Scroll-of-Enchantment inscribes); S16 Furor/Haste split (hook is live and the two are no longer identical, but Furor still applies to movement speed rather than attack speed). All other open audit items left untouched.

- Tags: traps, disarming, source-fidelity, audit-S10
- Made Disarming Trap actually disarm the hero. `DisarmingTrap._do_effect` read the equipped weapon from `triggerer.get_weapon()` / `triggerer.weapon`, but `Hero` exposes neither — equipped weapons live on `Belongings` (`belongings.get_equipped_weapon()`). The trap therefore always hit the `weapon == null` branch and printed "You have nothing to disarm," doing nothing, despite now being live in the Halls trap pool (depth 21-24, added this session). Fix: read the weapon from `triggerer.belongings.get_equipped_weapon()` first (falling back to the old `get_weapon`/`weapon` paths for non-hero triggerers), and unequip via `belongings.unequip("weapon")` instead of the non-existent `triggerer.unequip_weapon()`, so the weapon is knocked to a nearby passable cell as a heap (SPD behavior). The earlier arg-swap P1 (`drop_item(drop_pos, weapon)`) was already fixed. Added `tests/cases/test_disarming_trap.gd` (weapon unequipped + dropped as a nearby heap; unarmed hero drops nothing) and registered it in the runner; verified it fails against the pre-fix detection (2 failures) and passes after. Full local headless suite green (754 checks, Godot 4.7.1). Remaining S10 open: cursing-trap equipment path still dead, pitfall trap doesn't descend a depth, several trap classes still unpooled outside Halls.

- Tags: plants, aoe, source-fidelity, audit-S19
- Fixed grid edge-wrap in `icecap.gd` and `firebloom.gd`: `DIRS_8` neighbours are now column-safe, so a wall-edge freeze/ignite can no longer affect a cell wrapped across the map edge. Added `test_plant_edge_wrap.gd`.

- Tags: mobile, hud, safe-area, controls
- Tightened mobile HUD safe-area layout so the game log anchors above the safe-area-adjusted toolbar instead of using a separate bottom calculation, and mobile landscape layout is detected when both dimensions fit the mobile web bounds. Extended `test_mobile_hud_input.gd` to cover portrait/landscape log clearance and bottom safe-area toolbar placement.

- Tags: wands, identification, source-fidelity, audit-S14
- Fixed wand use-based identification permanently stalling after 5 zaps (audit S14 P1). `Wand` ported SPD's two-pool scheme (`_uses_left_to_id = 10`, `_available_uses_to_id = 5`) but never ported the regeneration: `_use_for_identification()` early-returns once `_available_uses_to_id <= 0`, and nothing ever refilled it, so every wand froze at 5/10 uses and could NEVER be identified by use — a defining SPD mechanic silently dead. SPD refills the pool in `Wand.onHeroGainExp(levelPercent, hero)` (`availableUsesToID = min(USES_TO_ID/2, availableUsesToID + levelPercent*USES_TO_ID/2)`), called for every carried item from `Hero.earnExp` with `levelPercent = exp/maxExp()` computed before consuming XP. Ported faithfully: added `Wand.on_hero_gain_exp(level_percent)` (guards identified wands, caps at the half-pool), `Belongings.notify_hero_gain_exp()` iterating `get_items()` by `has_method`, and a call in `Hero.earn_xp()` using `amount/xp_to_next` before mutating XP. Added `test_wand_use_identification.gd` (up-front pool stalls without XP, uses-left frozen at half, XP refills to the cap without overfilling, identified wands stop regenerating, and an end-to-end hero-XP-interleaved zap loop that IDs the wand — the end-to-end case fails without the wiring). Full local headless suite green (744 checks, Godot 4.7.1). Remaining open S14 P1s: Corruption produces an enemy not an ally, Warding spawns no sentry.

- Tags: targeting, controls, source-fidelity, audit-S23
- Guarded targeting resolution against out-of-bounds cells before range/visibility checks or item callbacks run. This keeps SPD-style targeting/cell-selection from resolving an invalid cell after mobile/browser coordinate drift or stale input, and leaves targeting active so the player can pick again. Extended `test_targeting_input.gd` to cover invalid-cell rejection and a valid-cell callback path. Local headless suite green (732 checks, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: mobile, hud, toolbar, controls, web, playtesting
- Hardened mobile toolbar tap activation against nested/scaled Control coordinate drift. `Toolbar.activate_button_at_screen_position()` now resolves touches in toolbar-local space as well as global space, so HUD-forwarded mobile taps still hit the intended action buttons when browser/backing-canvas scaling or nested panel containers shift rect spaces. Extended `test_mobile_hud_input.gd` to assert a real HUD touch on the bottom toolbar emits a toolbar action. Local headless suite green (399 checks, Godot 4.7.1); no real-device/iPhone confirmation yet.

- Tags: buffs, plants, scrolls, stones, source-fidelity, audit-S15, audit-S17, audit-S19
- Replaced the remaining renamed-Paralysis sleep/freeze substitutes in Scroll of Lullaby, Stone of Deepened Sleep, Dreamfoil, and Icecap with the dedicated `SleepBuff` / `Frozen` effects. Sleep now uses the wake-on-damage contract instead of generic paralysis, and Icecap now gets Frozen's shared freeze semantics rather than an unrelated paralysis timer. Added `test_sleep_freeze_effects.gd` to cover sleep wakeup and Icecap Frozen attachment. Local headless suite green (397 checks, Godot 4.7.1).

- Tags: mobile, hud, toolbar, web, playtesting
- Fixed scaled mobile HUD touch activation after the backing-canvas layout fix. `HUD` now accepts high-DPI backing-canvas touch coordinates for hit-testing, converts them back into HUD layout coordinates before activating toolbar controls, and applies the same conversion to party-row buttons. Extended `test_mobile_hud_input.gd` with scaled toolbar hit-testing and party-button activation coverage. Local headless suite green (384 checks, Godot 4.7.1); no real-device/iPhone touch confirmation yet.

- Tags: potions, blobs, gases, source-fidelity, audit-S15, audit-S20
- Re-pointed offensive potion shatter effects at the live blob layer. Toxic Gas, Paralytic Gas, and Liquid Flame now seed `ToxicGas`, `ParalyticGas`, and `FireBlob` through `Level.add_blob()` instead of applying one-shot 3x3 buffs, and Potion of Frost now seeds a new `FreezingBlob` that freezes characters and water terrain. Updated blob and thrown-potion shatter tests to assert blob seeding, deferred tick effects, and freezing behavior. Follow-up: blob ticking still runs per hero round rather than true actor time, and freezing/fire cross-blob cancellation is not implemented yet.

- Tags: items, stack-splitting, source-fidelity, audit-S12, audit-S15, audit-S17
- Fixed stack splitting so split-off consumables keep their concrete behavior instead of becoming inert base items. Base `Item.duplicate_item()` now reconstructs clones through `Generator.create_item(item_id)` before copying shared state, so factory-backed stackables like seeds, bombs, stones, spells, food, missile weapons, torches, and dewdrops keep their scripts and subtype fields. Potion and scroll overrides now route through `Potion.create(item_id)` / `Scroll.create(item_id)` so split stacks preserve their actual `drink()`/`read_scroll()` implementations instead of downgrading to base `Potion`/`Scroll`. Added `test_item_stack_splitting.gd` to cover the affected families.

- Tags: mobile, hud, web, playtesting
- Fixed mobile web HUD layouts that used the visible browser viewport but still rendered into a larger Godot backing canvas. `HUD` now scales its `CanvasLayer` by backing-canvas size over CSS-visible layout size, so the status strip and toolbar keep their intended on-screen size when mobile browsers expose a large canvas behind a phone-sized viewport. Extended `test_mobile_hud_input.gd` with a 393x852 visible viewport / 1179x1704 backing-canvas regression that asserts the scaled toolbar remains full-width. Local headless suite green (311 checks, Godot 4.7.1); GitHub CI and Pages deploy green for commit `4fa7201`. Browser smoke: live page and `index.js`/`index.pck`/`index.wasm` returned 200, and Chromium mobile-width screenshot loaded the name prompt; no real-device/iPhone in-run touch confirmation yet.

- Tags: potions, throwing, source-fidelity, audit-S15
- Wired thrown potions into the existing shatter path. `Hero._do_throw_item()` now detects `Potion`, resolves the projectile collision cell, calls `Potion.shatter(collision_pos, level)`, identifies the potion, records potion usage, and consumes one stack item instead of falling through to generic ranged-miss logic. Added `test_thrown_potion_shatter.gd` covering Toxic Gas shatter effects, stack consumption, identification, and last-item removal. Follow-up: offensive potion shatter effects still use current one-shot 3x3 buff/terrain behavior; the next fidelity pass should re-point Toxic Gas/Paralytic Gas/Liquid Flame/Frost at real blobs.

- Tags: doors, keys, source-fidelity, audit-S11
- Fixed ordinary locked doors consuming the wrong key type. `Door.open()` now asks for `"iron"` keys instead of `"golden"` keys, activating the existing depth-scoped iron-key logic in `Hero.has_key()`/`use_key()` and preserving golden keys for golden chests. Added `test_door_key_gating.gd` to cover golden-key rejection and iron-key consumption. Follow-up: key placement for locked special rooms is still thin and should be audited against SPD's guaranteed same-floor iron-key placement.

- Tags: chasms, level-transitions, source-fidelity, audit-S11
- Replaced chasm instant-death with a first-pass SPD-style fall flow. Heroes with `Levitation` or flight now cross chasms safely; grounded heroes emit a fall transition, descend without requiring stairs, land on a passable cell of the next floor, and then take `NormalIntRange(HT/6, HT/3)` fall damage plus Cripple instead of being killed in place. `Chasm` now owns the shared crossing and fall-damage logic, `LoadingScene` understands `"fall"` arrivals, and `test_chasm_fall.gd` covers the damage formula, levitation immunity, and event handoff. Remaining follow-ups: PitfallTrap still needs to reuse the fall path, boss-arena chasm rings need encounter-specific handling, and true PitRoom landing support is not implemented yet.

- Tags: mobs, level-generation, source-fidelity, audit-S07
- Added the missing regular-floor scheduled mob respawner. `RegularLevel` now stores the generated floor's target mob population and activates a level-owned `Respawner` actor on the active floor, matching SPD's scheduler shape with a 50-tick respawn cooldown. The respawner adds depth-appropriate replacement mobs only while the living count is below that target, avoids occupied/visible/hero-adjacent cells, registers spawned mobs with the turn manager, and persists the respawn target with cached/saved level state.
