- Continued multiplayer authority cleanup for item/spell subwindows: `Scroll of Transmutation` now opens a targeted transmutation window for remote owners, `WndTransmute` now routes confirmed transmutes back through a host-owned `transmute_item` action, `Alchemize` now opens a targeted alchemy window for remote owners, and `WndAlchemy` now routes brewing through a host-owned `alchemy_brew` action. `GameScene` now normalizes those item-ref payloads, resolves both actions on the host, and sends the resulting success/failure feedback back to the owning player instead of letting those systems mutate locally on clients.
- Extended the same selector-window multiplayer pattern to more remaining item/spell flows: `WndItemSelect` now supports host-owned online selections, `Scroll of Identify` and `Scroll of Upgrade` now open targeted remote selectors instead of only host-local windows, and `Recycle` / `Curse Infusion` now do the same. `GameScene` now resolves `identify_item`, `upgrade_item`, `recycle_item`, and `curse_infusion_item` on the host and sends the resulting feedback back to the owning player instead of leaving those actions on old single-player callbacks.
- Simplified the main menu structure in `TitleScene`: `Host Co-op` / `Join Co-op` are now collapsed into one top-level `Co-op` entry, and the top action row now puts `New Game` and `Continue` on the same line with a wider `New Game` button and a narrower `Continue` button when a save exists, while `New Game` expands to full width if no save is present.
- Continued multiplayer selector-window cleanup for stones: `Stone of Enchantment` now uses the same targeted remote selector flow as other host-owned item picks, and `Stone of Augmentation` now supports a full two-step remote flow with targeted item selection followed by a targeted augment-choice window. `WndAugmentSelect` now supports host-owned online selections, and `GameScene` now resolves the resulting stone enchantment and augmentation actions on the host instead of leaving those flows local-only.
- Continued multiplayer NPC authority hardening by adding targeted `npc_message` delivery: NPC base class now routes dialogue and feedback to the interacting hero’s owning peer when the host is handling a remote interaction, `GameScene` now logs those targeted UI events on the client, and the core shop/ghost/imp/wandmaker/blacksmith interaction and reward/reforge messages now follow the player who triggered them instead of only appearing in the host log.
- Removed the redundant top-level Rankings button from TitleScene, so rankings are now accessed only through the Player Profile area instead of appearing in two separate title-menu locations.
- Folded co-op into the `New Game` flow as a default-off toggle under the main start row, removed the separate `Co-op` and `About` title-menu entries, and made `New Game` open the co-op host/join panel only when that toggle is enabled.
- Continued multiplayer direct-effect hardening by routing a larger set of scroll and runestone result messages through owner-targeted UI delivery, so remote players now see success/failure feedback for effects like remove curse, magic mapping, teleportation, recharging, intuition, blink, clairvoyance, sleep/disarm, flock, and shock on their own client instead of those results only appearing in the host log.
- Moved co-op entry out of the title screen and into `HeroSelectScene`: `TitleScene` no longer has a co-op toggle or About entry, `New Game` always opens hero select, and hero select now exposes `Host` / `Join` controls above the normal `Back` / `Start` row.
- Added a small inline join panel to `HeroSelectScene` for host IP + port entry, and wired hero-select online session start/connect signals to transition straight into the co-op lobby after hosting or joining.
- Refined `HeroSelectScene` layout to give co-op its own framed subsection inside the left column, with a titled host/join area and the inline join panel nested inside it. This reduces the visual jump from single-player hero select to the hosted lobby and gives the co-op controls clearer separation from the normal start/back actions.
- Reworked the multiplayer entry flow again: `TitleScene` now owns a dedicated `Multiplayer` submenu under `New Game`, with `Open Lobby` as the primary action and `Join Lobby` using lobby IP + port. This keeps hosting as a backend detail while leaving hero select focused on class/loadout instead of network session setup.
- Removed the temporary host/join controls and inline join panel from `HeroSelectScene`. Hero select is back to its normal single-player layout unless it is entered from an already-active online session, where it only shows the online party/loadout state.
- Expanded `PlayerProfile` into a real profile identity shell: it now persists a selected profile icon plus unlocked profile icons, defaults to the Warrior icon, and unlocks additional class icons from badge progression milestones over time instead of treating profile identity as only a plain text name.
- Reworked the title-screen Player Profile panel to use a proper profile header instead of an always-editable text box. The player name is now shown as a large display label with a small edit button that reuses the profile-name prompt, and a new profile-icon panel sits beside it with its own small edit control for cycling currently unlocked icons.
- Moved online class choice into `LobbyScene` itself. The co-op lobby now has a top-row class selector matching the new-game hero buttons, class choice is synchronized through `NetworkManager` lobby state, right-side lobby splash previews now reflect each connected player's chosen class, and host start now launches the online run directly from the lobby using those synchronized class selections.
- Added a first-pass controller scheme for the title screen, hero select, and co-op lobby. D-pad navigation and face-button accept/cancel now work on those menu surfaces, and the lobby also supports quick class cycling plus a controller shortcut for opening lobby settings.
- Simplified multiplayer entry again so pressing `Multiplayer` on the title screen now immediately creates the local player's own hosted lobby instead of opening a separate host/join setup panel. Joining another session is now handled from inside `LobbyScene` with a bottom-row `Join Lobby` action and an inline join overlay for host IP + port.
- Extended synchronized lobby identity to include the player's selected profile icon as well as class choice. `NetworkManager` now carries profile icon IDs in lobby state, class selection persists across hosted/joined lobbies through saved local lobby preferences, and the co-op lobby player list now shows actual profile icons beside each player's row instead of only plain text.
- Closed another multiplayer setup gap by removing the extra online hero-select step from the main host flow. The lobby now owns the core pre-run multiplayer state directly: host opens lobby, players pick classes there, and host starts the run from there.
- Reworked the co-op lobby action layout into a real 2x2 grid: `Settings` top-left, `Join` top-right, `Back` bottom-left, and one shared bottom-right action button for ready/start flow. Hosts now use that single bottom-right button as `Ready Up` first and then `Start Run` once they are marked ready, with the start state greying out until the rest of the party is ready.
- Split lobby settings into `General` and host-only `Co-op` tabs. Normal player settings stay under `General`, while host session settings like port and player cap now live under the dedicated `Co-op` tab instead of being mixed into the same panel for everyone.
- Mirrored Shattered-style class unlock progression so Warrior is the only guaranteed starting class again. `PlayerProfile` now persists unlocked hero classes and tracks Mage/Rogue/Huntress/Duelist unlock progress, `Hero` now emits the missing surprise-attack and thrown-weapon progress events, solo hero select now previews locked classes but disables `Start` and shows each unlock requirement, and the co-op lobby now clamps the local class selector to classes the player has actually unlocked.
- Expanded the Player Profile summary so the new progression is visible: it now reports unlocked classes, lists the currently available class names, shows icon progress, and surfaces the remaining class unlock requirements directly in the profile panel instead of leaving the new class-gating system hidden behind the hero-select screen.
- Tightened the Player Profile layout again so progression data no longer pushes the page below the visible frame. The profile summary is now a fixed-height snapshot with compact class/icon progress, one current unlock target, and condensed run stats instead of a long multiline report.
- Simplified the Player Profile summary back into a cleaner stats card and moved progression detail into the Achievements popup. The profile panel now focuses on visually compact account/run stats, while `WndBadges` now has separate `Achievements` and `Unlocks` tabs so class/icon unlock requirements are visible without overflowing the profile or requiring horizontal scrolling.
- Replaced the profile icon edit cycle with a proper popup picker. The new `WndProfileIconPicker` shows all profile icons in a grid, keeps locked ones visible but greyed out, marks the current icon, and only allows choosing icons the player has actually unlocked.
- Expanded the profile icon system beyond hero portraits. The picker is now taller so the current icon set fits without scrolling, title/profile previews now use circular portrait crops with a tighter zoom, enemy trophy icons (`rat`, `gnoll`, `crab`, `skeleton`, `goo`) were added, and `PlayerProfile` now unlocks those icons from lifetime kill thresholds while the lobby and Achievements `Unlocks` tab both understand and display the new icon set.
- Fixed the new circular profile icon rendering path and simplified the picker presentation. Circular icon previews now render their textures correctly again, the icon picker tiles are less text-heavy, and locked icons now read through a small corner badge instead of extra explanatory copy inside each tile.
- Reworked profile icon texture extraction to use real cropped textures instead of atlas textures before applying the circular portrait treatment. This should restore visible art in the profile header, picker, and lobby list, and the picker’s lock badge is now an actual drawn corner lock anchored at the lower-right of the icon frame instead of a centered text placeholder.
- Tuned the circular crop framing for hero portrait icons specifically: class portraits are now framed slightly lower-left and a touch less zoomed-in than the default enemy-trophy crop, while the lock badge remains anchored at the lower-right corner of the icon area.
- Updated the co-op lobby player list to use the real selected profile icons from each synchronized lobby slot through the same circular profile-icon renderer and crop rules used by the profile UI and icon picker, instead of drawing them as tiny square textures.
- Ran a broader menu-shell polish pass to unify submenu layout and design language. `TitleScene` profile/settings/multiplayer panels now use one shared submenu size, consistent internal padding, and aligned bottom action rows; the square frame around the profile icon header was removed in favor of a cleaner centered circular portrait; the co-op lobby player list dropped its old square icon box and now uses bare circular profile icons; lobby buttons and modal actions were restyled to match the rest of the menu chrome; and `RankingsScene` was resized and relaid out to the same overall shell dimensions and bottom-button pattern as the other submenu pages.
- Fixed the remaining hero-select layout drift by moving `HeroSelectScene`'s `Back` / `Start` row into the shared bottom action zone instead of leaving it at the old mid-panel coordinates, and removed the obsolete online-only Y-offset that was still making that submenu feel misaligned compared to the rest of the menu family.
- Tightened the co-op lobby stack so its 2x2 action grid sits higher and with more bottom breathing room, bringing the multiplayer submenu closer to the same bottom-button band and padding rhythm used by the other menu pages.
- Removed the extra explanatory lobby text block under the co-op action grid so the multiplayer submenu reads cleaner and stays closer to the same minimal action-first layout as the other submenu pages.
- Matched `HeroSelectScene`'s left-column padding to the co-op lobby by moving its title, class row, description, stats, party/loadout rows, notices, and action row onto the same 24px inset system. This removes the last obvious offset mismatch between the `New Game` and multiplayer submenu layouts.
- Corrected `HeroSelectScene`'s left-column content width so the right-side inset inside the left pane now matches the left-side inset, instead of leaving an overly narrow inner column with visibly uneven padding against the center split.
- Rebuilt `HeroSelectScene`'s left pane around the same constrained `VBoxContainer` stack pattern used by the co-op lobby, instead of continuing to position each label/row manually. The hero-select title, class label, class row, description, stats, optional party rows, notices, and bottom action row now flow through the same structural layout model as the multiplayer left pane, so the two screens should finally format alike instead of only sharing approximate spacing values.
- Tightened that hero-select alignment further by matching the lobby's actual effective content widths and button sizing rhythm instead of only its inset origin. `HeroSelectScene` now uses the same narrow stacked content column and 145px action-button widths as the co-op lobby, which should remove the remaining visual mismatch in the left-pane formatting.
- Fixed the Playwright web-smoke regressions found in the live build: `StatusPane` now uses `update_minimum_size()` instead of calling the `minimum_size_changed` signal like a method, exported audio loading now uses explicit SFX/music manifests instead of runtime `res://` directory scans, the compile smoke now loads every `src/**/*.gd` script, and the deploy workflow now runs a mobile Playwright smoke that fails on critical script/audio console regressions.
- Restored room-door generation for tunneled regular-level neighbor links. `StandardPainter` now places a door at each valid tunnel mouth, preserving secret doors for secret rooms, locked doors for vault/armory rooms, and crystal doors for crystal vaults; the tunnel pair tracker now uses stable string keys instead of an overflowing numeric hash.
- Restored the boss-floor skeleton-key loop closer to SPD: boss mobs now drop a canonical, depth-tagged `skeleton_key`, and descending from boss depths consumes a current-depth skeleton key before transitioning. The check is co-op-aware and can consume the key from any active party hero while rejecting wrong-depth keys.
- Fixed mobile modal close controls by making `WndBase`'s title-bar `X` consume direct screen-touch down/up events and close on the first touch release. Removed the duplicate content-level `Close` buttons from inventory and item detail windows so each popup has a single close affordance again.
- Widened compact mobile toolbar fallback hit targets by 8px without changing desktop behavior, making safe-area/scaled near-edge taps less likely to miss or fall through to world input. Covered by `test_mobile_hud_input.gd`.
- Restored the unblessed Ankh lost-inventory item contract (adapted from current SPD): revival no longer destroys the backpack. Unique items, bags, and `kept_though_lost_invent` items stay with the hero; everything else (including held-bag contents) drops as recoverable heaps at the hero's position, and the consumed ankh is never dropped. Ankh is now `bones = true` like upstream. `Item.serialize()` now persists the kept flag as `kept_lost` (upstream `Item.KEPT_LOST`) with a SAVE_VERSION 2->3 stamp-only migration. Covered by `test_ankh_revival.gd`. Remaining divergences: upstream's LostInventory buff + LostBackpack retrieval heap, WndResurrect's two-item keep choice, respawn-away-from-death placement, and upstream revival HP values (unblessed full HP; blessed HT/4 + Invulnerability vs this port's HT/4 unblessed / HT/2 blessed).
- Restored the upstream `Dungeon.dropToChasm` fallen-item pipeline for pitfalls: `DelayedPit` collapse now removes item heaps inside the open 3x3 footprint and queues their serialized items on `GameManager.pending_dropped_items` for depth+1 (lost past `MAX_DEPTH`, as upstream); `LoadingScene` delivers pending items on arrival at a random passable cell with potions shattering on impact (host-side only online), and run state now persists the pending map with a stamp-only SAVE_VERSION 3->4 migration. Covered by `test_pitfall_heap_drop.gd`. Remaining divergences: seeds/runestones are dropped as heaps instead of auto-planting on landing, and `Level.drop_item` onto CHASM terrain does not yet route thrown/placed items to the floor below.
- Brought `PitRoom` contents to upstream `PitRoom.java` parity: the room is now a sealed special room with a CRYSTAL door entrance (single connection, upstream min/max 6-9 sizing), an empty well against the wall opposite the entrance, and a centre skeleton heap holding the main loot (1/3 ring, 1/3 artifact, 1/3 weapon/weapon/missile/armor/armor), 1-2 minor prizes (potion/scroll/food/gold), and a depth-bound `crystal_key` that opens the door from inside. The old non-upstream chasm-ring/pedestal layout is removed. Added `Room.can_place_trap/can_place_grass/can_place_water` generation hooks (upstream `Room.canPlace*`) consulted by `RegularLevel._place_traps` and `StandardPainter` grass/water feeling patches; PitRoom bans all three. Covered by `test_pit_room_contents.gd`. Remaining divergences: no `setHauntedIfCursed` wraith haunting on the skeleton heap, and upstream merges all centre drops into one heap while this port stacks separate heaps on the same cell.
- Implemented the Rogue's Protective Shadows talent to upstream `Talent.ProtectiveShadowsTracker` behavior: gaining Invisibility now attaches a hidden tracker buff that builds a Barrier every 2/1 turns up to 3/5 shielding at 1/2 points, detaching when visibility returns or the talent is absent. Prerequisite in the same slice: `Barrier` decay now matches upstream's fractional `min(1, shielding/20)` per-turn loss with `partial_lost_shield` reset on `set_shield`/`inc_shield` (was a flat 1/turn, which made small talent barriers impossible to sustain); the partial value persists through buff serialization with a defaulting read, no SAVE_VERSION change since buff dicts are schemaless. Added `Buff.show_in_ui` (upstream `BuffIndicator.NONE`) and filtered hidden buffs from the status pane and mobile HUD buff rows. Talent metadata updated (real description, max 2 points as upstream). Tracker self-dedupes on attach because `_deserialize_buffs` appends without merging while a restored Invisibility spawns a fresh tracker. Covered by `test_protective_shadows.gd`. Remaining divergence: upstream `HoldFast.buffDecayFactor` (Warden) is not applied to barrier decay since HoldFast is not implemented.
- Implemented the Huntress's Nature's Aid talent to upstream `Plant.trigger()` behavior: any plant triggering on a currently visible cell now grants `Barkskin.conditionally_append(hero, 2, 1 + 2*points)` — barkskin level 2 decaying every 3/5 turns at 1/2 points — regardless of who triggered the plant (heroes, mobs, or instant seed activation all route through `Plant.activate`). Co-op adaptation: upstream checks the single `Dungeon.hero`'s FOV; this port grants it to every party hero holding the talent when the plant cell is in the shared visible set. Talent description in `talent_data.gd` replaced with the real effect text. Covered by `test_natures_aid.gd`. Remaining divergence: upstream places NATURES_AID in tier 1 (this port's 4-talent class layout keeps it in tier 2 alongside Followup Strike), and the port's shared visibility array means any party member's sight qualifies rather than per-hero FOV.
- Closed the stale S01 floor-transition autosave backlog item after a source check: every in-run stair/fall transition already routes through `FloorTransitionCoordinator.transition_to_loading` with `is_continue: true` + `transition_type`, which arms `LoadingScene._autosave_after_generation`, so descend/ascend/fall all autosave post-generation (upstream `Dungeon.saveAll` on level switch); only a brand-new game's first floor skips it, as upstream. Added `test_transition_autosave.gd` locking in the meta contract (transition metas arm autosave, new-game meta does not) plus the `autosave_if_active()` guards (no active run → no write, level/run depth mismatch → no write, consistent active run → save file written), with save-file backup/restore around the test.
- Brought Cave Spinner AI to upstream `Spinner.java` state-machine parity: `_act_hunting` is now just the web-shot pre-step (plus Amok retarget) delegating chase/attack/flee/lost-target handling to `super._act_hunting()`; poisoning moved fully into `on_attack_hit` (50% chance, poison duration `randi_range(7, 8)` matching upstream `Random.IntRange(7,8)`, web cooldown reset to 0, state -> FLEEING only when the poison lands); and a new `_act_fleeing` override returns to HUNTING once the visible target is no longer poisoned unless Terror/Dread force the flee, while still allowing web shots mid-flee. Covered by `test_spinner_ai.gd` (4 new cases: flee->hunt on poison expiry, keep fleeing while poisoned, Terror overrides the return, poison hit sets FLEEING + resets cooldown). Remaining divergences: web placement uses the port's simplified single-cell prediction with a 6-tile range cap instead of upstream's Ballistica 3-cell spread, and the ascension-challenge poison-duration modifier is not implemented (no ascension mode).
- Replaced the retired `mage_energizing_upgrade` groundwork slot (talent no longer exists in current upstream) with the real Mage tier-2 Shield Battery talent from upstream `Wand.onSelect`'s self-target branch: with the talent, zapping a wand (or the Mage's Staff, via its imbued wand) at the Mage's own cell converts all current charges into a Barrier of `round(HT * 0.04 * charges)`, x1.5 at 2 points, drains the wand to 0 charges, and skips the normal zap; a 0-charge self-zap fizzles with a warning and no shield. Implemented as a `_do_zap_wand` intercept + `_try_shield_battery` helper in `hero.gd` (small hand-edits, fragile file), with a `deserialize` migration remapping saved `mage_energizing_upgrade` points to `mage_shield_battery` clamped to the new 2-point cap (schemaless talent dict, no SAVE_VERSION change). Covered by `test_shield_battery.gd` (6 cases: conversion math, x1.5, class/talent gating, fizzle, non-self zap untouched, save migration). Remaining divergences: no upstream MagicImmune block (buff not implemented), no charge-up sound/SpellSprite flash, and the port's action framework still spends the turn via the generic zap delay rather than upstream's explicit `spendAndNext(TICK)`.
- Replaced all 42 factory-spawnable mob placeholder descriptions with the real upstream SPD text from `actors.properties` (rat through Yog-Dzewa, including `elemental$fireelemental`, `dwarfking`, `npcs.mirrorimage`, `npcs.sheep`, and `statue` for the animated statue), stripped upstream `_highlight_` markup, kept `\n` paragraph breaks, and gave `yog_fist` its missing description (base upstream `yogfist` text). Examine-mode `WndInfoMob` now shows real flavor/mechanics text instead of one-line placeholders. Covered by `test_mob_descriptions.gd` (length + no-markup check on every factory id, upstream-phrase spot checks, yog_fist). Remaining divergences: no per-fist-type Yog fist descriptions, no shaman mask `spell_desc` suffixes, and port-specific mobs (golden statue, rose ghost, summoned elemental, ward sentry) keep adapted text.
- Brought examine-cell object collection to current upstream `GameScene.examineCell()`/`getObjectsAtCell()` parity: `CellExaminer.list_cell_objects` now collects everything of interest on a known cell in upstream order (visible char, heap items, plant, visible+active trap); one object opens its info window directly, several open the new `WndExamineChoice` chooser (upstream `WndOptions`, "Choose Examine" / "There are multiple things of interest here…"), and an empty cell falls back to the terrain info window. Since the port stores one heap entry per item instead of upstream's single item stack, each item on the cell gets its own chooser entry named via `get_display_name()` — this is the heap multi-item listing from the examine backlog item. `describe_cell` is kept as first-object-priority classification on top of the same collector (existing tests unchanged), and the old "There is more than one item on this spot." suffix is gone. Covered by `test_examine_multi.gd` (12 cases: order, per-item heap entries, FOV char skip, hidden trap skip, chooser button wiring). Remaining divergences: chooser has no INFO icon art, heap entries name the item rather than upstream container titles (chest/tomb/skeleton heaps don't exist in the port), and pressing chooser buttons is verified by wiring only in headless (close animation needs the scene tree).
- Implemented the Berserker's Endless Rage talent (first real subclass T3) to upstream `Berserk.damage()`/`currentShieldBoost()` intent: `BerserkerRage.max_rage()` now returns `100 * (1 + 0.1667*points)` (cap 117%/133%/150%, upstream `1f + 0.1667f*points`), `on_damage_taken` fills to the raised cap, and rage held above 100% multiplies outgoing damage by `rage/100` in `modify_damage` (up to x1.5) — the port's adaptation of upstream overfill, where power > 1 multiplies the berserk shield and halves recovery; this port's rage system is death-prevention + missing-HP fury rather than upstream's shield-berserk state machine, so the overfill payoff lands on the fury damage instead. Death prevention still triggers at the base 100% threshold regardless of talent points. Talent metadata switched from `_make_inert` to real with upstream-style description. Covered by `test_endless_rage.gd` (8 cases: base cap, +1/+3 caps, overfill multiplier, no bonus at/below 100%, death prevention, serialize round trip); `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` updated to whitelist implemented subclass talents. Remaining divergences: no upstream Berserk state machine (NORMAL/BERSERK/RECOVERING, manual berserk action, shield-based survival), and no tier-3 hero-level gating anywhere in the talent UI — T3 talents become purchasable as soon as the subclass is chosen (upstream gates tier 3 behind level 13).
- Implemented the Gladiator's Cleave talent (first live Gladiator T3) to upstream `Combo.hit()` intent: a combo hit that kills its target now extends the combo's survival window instead of leaving the usual 1-turn reset — upstream sets `comboTime = 15f + 15f*pointsInTalent(CLEAVE)` versus a base of 5, so the port scales its 1-turn base window by the same 3x-per-step ratio (kill = 3 turns untalented, 6/9/12 with Cleave +1/+2/+3). `GladiatorCombo` gains a `combo_window` field (serialized, defaulting old saves to 1), kill detection via the `on_damage_dealt` target's `is_alive`, and upstream-style non-refresh on ordinary hits (a normal hit inherits the remaining window, mirroring `comboTime = max(comboTime, 5f)` decay rather than resetting to full). Window drops back to 1 turn when the combo lapses. Covered by `test_gladiator_cleave.gd` (12 checks: base window, untalented kill, +1/+3 scaling, idle-turn survival/expiry, non-refresh, post-drop reset, serialize round trip); `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` now uses `gladiator_combo_mastery` as its inert probe and asserts Cleave is upgradable. Remaining divergences: the port has no combo action moves (clobber/slam/parry/fury) — combo is an auto-finisher damage multiplier — so Cleave only preserves combo between kills rather than interacting with finisher choice, and upstream's full-HP-corruption trigger has no port equivalent.
- Implemented the Gladiator's Enhanced Combo talent (all three Gladiator T3s now live) as a port adaptation of upstream `Talent.ENHANCED_COMBO`: upstream empowers manual combo moves used at higher counts (Clobber gains +1 knockback and 3-turn Vertigo at count 7+, Parry empowers at 9+, and +3 grants a leap to targets within `1+count/3` plus empowered Slam/Crush/Fury at 3+). The port's combo is an auto-finisher with no manual move picker, so each point instead raises `GladiatorCombo.finisher_threshold()` from 3 to 5/7/9 hits, which finally makes the existing 1.5x-at-3 → 3.0x-at-10 multiplier curve reachable (untalented finishers always fired at exactly 3 hits ≈ x1.5). Untalented behavior is unchanged. Covered by `test_enhanced_combo.gd` (13 checks: threshold scaling, no-finish below threshold, raised-threshold finisher multiplier, x3.0 cap at 10 hits, untalented parity); `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` inert probe moved to `monk_flurry_mastery` and now asserts Enhanced Combo is upgradable. Remaining divergences: no manual combo moves (clobber/slam/parry/crush/fury), so no per-move empowered effects, knockback, vertigo, or leap; those need the combo-move picker system first.
- Implemented the Assassin's Bounty Hunter talent to upstream `Mob.lootChance()` behavior: a hero killing from Preparation with the talent multiplies each loot-table drop chance by `1 + 0.02 * 2^(prepLevel-1) * points` (2/4/8/16% per preparation level, x1-x3 by points). `AssassinPreparation.bounty_hunter_bonus()` computes the bonus, `Mob._drop_loot` now takes the killer from `_on_death(_source)` and applies `_loot_chance_multiplier`, and timing matches upstream because the hero dispels Invisibility (which detaches Preparation) only after `attack()` returns, so Preparation is still attached during the death loot roll. Covered by `test_bounty_hunter.gd` (10 checks: per-level bonus, point scaling, no-talent zero, killer multiplier, unprepared/mob/null killers). Remaining divergences: upstream attaches a one-attack `BountyHunterTracker` FlavourBuff while the port checks the still-attached Preparation directly (equivalent for melee kills; a prepared-attack DoT kill after invisibility ends would not get the bonus), and there is no RingOfWealth/ShardOfOblivion drop-bonus stacking since those systems aren't implemented.
- Implemented the Freerunner's Projectile Momentum talent (first live Freerunner T3) to upstream `Hero.attackSkill()` ranged-branch behavior: `accuracy *= 1f + pointsInTalent(PROJECTILE_MOMENTUM)/2f` while freerunning. The port has no manual freerun action, so "freerunning" is momentum held at the cap (`FreerunnerMomentum.is_freerunning()`, momentum >= 10), matching how the port already grants the max-momentum speed/evasion payoff. `ranged_accuracy_multiplier()` returns `1 + 0.5*points` (x1.5/x2.0/x2.5) at max momentum, and `Hero._resolve_ranged_attack` multiplies it into `acc_multi` for `MissileWeapon`/`SpiritBow` attacks before `Char.hit`. Notably the ranged path never calls buff `on_damage_dealt`, so thrown attacks already do not reset momentum (upstream-consistent — only melee breaks it); momentum still decays -3 per non-moving turn, so a full stack funds roughly three boosted throws, a reasonable stand-in for upstream's `2*stacks` freerun timer. `freerunner_momentum_mastery` placeholder replaced by the real talent; `freerunner_kinetic_flow` stays inert. Covered by `test_projectile_momentum.gd` (12 checks: registry, freerunning threshold, x1.5/x2.0/x2.5 scaling, no bonus untalented/below max, serialize round trip); `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` now asserts Projectile Momentum upgradable + Kinetic Flow inert. Remaining divergences: no manual freerun state machine (activation, 2*stacks timer, 10+4*stacks cooldown), and Evasive Armor / Speedy Stealth are unimplemented (Speedy Stealth's stealth stacking and x2 invisible speed need the freerun/invisibility interplay).
- Implemented the Freerunner's Speedy Stealth talent to upstream `Momentum.java` behavior, adapted to the port's passive momentum system: +1 makes invisible turns build +2 momentum (upstream `act()` gains +2 stacks while invisible and not freerunning) instead of decaying; +2 preserves momentum entirely while invisible at the cap (upstream freezes the freerun timer while invisible; the port has no separate timer, so invisibility blocks decay outright); +3 grants x2 movement speed while invisible independent of momentum (upstream `speedMultiplier()` second branch — note upstream freerun is also x2 while the port's max-momentum speed bonus stays x1.5, an existing adaptation). Also replaced the placeholder `freerunner_kinetic_flow` inert slot with the real upstream trio: inert `freerunner_evasive_armor` (needs excess-armor-strength evasion wiring in the hero defense path) + live Projectile Momentum + live Speedy Stealth. Covered by `test_speedy_stealth.gd` (11 checks: registry, +2 invisible gain and cap, untalented decay, +1 decay at max, +2 max preservation and visible re-decay, +3 speed gating). Remaining Freerunner divergence: no manual freerun state machine, and Evasive Armor is inert.
- Implemented the Warlock's Necromancer's Minions talent (all three Warlock T3s now live) to upstream `Mob.die()` parity: killing a soul-marked non-wraith mob now rolls `randf() < 0.4*points/3` (13%/27%/40%) to raise a wraith at the corpse cell, corrupted to fight for the hero. The stale "needs a Wraith mob + Corruption buff" blocker note was wrong — both already existed in the port (`wraith.gd`, `corruption_buff.gd`); only the death-hook wiring was missing. `Mob._necromancers_minions_on_kill` runs in `_on_death` next to the Soul Eater trigger, spawns via MobFactory + `level.add_mob` + TurnManager registration, applies `CorruptionBuff` (ally flip, zero XP), and falls back to an adjacent free passable cell when another character shares the corpse cell (`_find_minion_spawn_cell`/`_minion_cell_blocked` check all chars, since `find_char_at` would return the dying mob itself). Port adaptations: no depth-based wraith stat scaling (upstream `adjustStats(depth)` — port wraiths have fixed stats) and a log line instead of the shadow-particle/cursed-sound FOV effects. Covered by `test_necromancers_minions.gd` (13 checks: spawn/corruption/ally/no-XP/level registration, chance boundaries at 1 point, zero-points, unmarked, non-Warlock, no wraith chaining, blocked-cell neighbor fallback).
- Brought random level-drop generation to current upstream `Generator.Category`/`RegularLevel.createItems` parity: `Generator.CATEGORY_WEIGHTS` now matches upstream's two 35-item decks averaged and doubled (GOLD 20, POTION 16, SCROLL 16, WEAPON 7 with upstream's separate MISSILE(1.5) folded in via the existing 50/50 melee/missile split, ARMOR 3, WAND 2, SEED 2, STONE 2, RING 1, ARTIFACT 1 — total 70), removing FOOD and the port-only MISC from the random table; every regular floor now places one guaranteed `Generator.random_food()` heap after the random drops (upstream `Level.initForDepth` `addItemToSpawn(Generator.random(FOOD))`, non-boss floors only — RegularLevel is never a boss arena); and `item_count()` now rolls 3/4/5 items at 60/30/10 (`3 + Random.chances({6,3,1})`) with +2 on LARGE feelings instead of the old flat 3/+1. The stale S12 backlog claim that upstream "never yields gold" was corrected after a source check (upstream GOLD is 10/35 ≈ 28.6%; the old port weight was actually LOW at ~21%). Covered by `test_generator_category_weights.gd` (table shape, exact weights, 200-roll no-food + gold-presence sampling, item-count distribution ranks and LARGE bounds). Remaining divergences: no guaranteed strength-potion/scroll-of-upgrade region quotas (upstream `posNeeded()`/`souNeeded()` LimitedDrops counters — new backlog item), no trinket/alchemy categories, and upstream's per-item deck decrementing (probs deplete then reset) is not modeled — the port re-rolls a flat table every drop.
- Implemented guaranteed strength-potion / scroll-of-upgrade region quotas to upstream `Dungeon.posNeeded()`/`souNeeded()` + `LimitedDrops` parity: `GameManager.limited_drops` now persists per-run counters (`strength_potions`, `upgrade_scrolls`), `pos_needed()`/`sou_needed()` copy the upstream formulas exactly (2 PoS per 5-floor set dropped one per floor pair with a 50% early roll; 3 SoU per set at scrolls-left/floors-left odds), and `RegularLevel._build()` spawns the guaranteed heaps after the food drop via a new `_drop_guaranteed_item()` helper (upstream `Level.create()` `addItemToSpawn` — source check corrected the stale backlog claim that this lived in `initForDepth`). Counters reset in `new_game()`, round-trip through `serialize_run_state()`/`apply_run_state()`, and SAVE_VERSION bumped 4→5 with a stamp-only migration (missing key = zero counts; pre-v5 mid-run saves may get up to a full region quota over the remaining floors of their current region — accepted, matches a fresh region start). Port adaptation: the NO_SCROLLS challenge half-quota branch is skipped (no challenge system). Covered by `test_limited_drops.gd` (deterministic quota-met/quota-open cases at depths 1-9, probabilistic depth-1 bounds, counter increment, run-state round trip, pre-v5 default).
- Replaced the shared talent point pool with upstream per-tier point buckets (Hero.talentPointsAvailable/talentPointsSpent parity): each tier now earns one point per hero level inside its own band (tier 1: levels 2-6 = 5 pts, tier 2: 7-12 = 6 pts, tier 3: 13-20 = 8 pts, tier 4 gated off since armor abilities aren't ported), minus points spent in that tier, with tier 3 additionally requiring a subclass. `Hero.talent_points_available` (stored, +1 per level-up) is gone; `talent_points_spent(tier)` / `talent_points_available_for(tier)` / `total_talent_points_available()` derive availability from `hero_level` + `talent_levels`, `TalentData.TIER_LEVEL_THRESHOLDS` gained upstream's closing 31 bound, and `WndTalents` unlocked tier headers now show per-tier remaining points. Save contract: the hero key is no longer written and is ignored on read; SAVE_VERSION bumped to 6 with a stamp-only migration (pre-bucket saves that overspent a tier clamp to 0 available, hoarded points map onto whatever their tiers actually earned — upstream-correct). Covered by `test_talent_point_buckets.gd` (18 checks: band earn/caps, per-tier spend isolation, subclass gate, empty-bucket refusal despite other-tier points, overspend clamp, total sum); `test_talent_tier_gating.gd`/`test_talent_inert_gating.gd` updated off the stored pool.
- Closed the audit:S03 headless combat-test gap with `test_char_combat_core.gd` (16 checks): `Char.hit()` 1e6 evasion/accuracy bounds with evasion precedence, Vulnerable ×1.33 proven post-armor via a fixed-`dr_roll` defender (10 dmg, DR 5 → 6, not the pre-armor 8), Doom ×1.67 stacking on Vulnerable's truncated post-armor value (10 → 13 → 22), ShieldBuff-before-flat-shielding-before-HP ordering including `take_damage`'s HP-lost return value and depleted-Barrier self-detach, and `dr_roll` bounded to 0..armor with a ~armor/2 triangular mean. While verifying the neighboring audit item, confirmed `Char.resist()` has zero providers in the port (no `resistances()` implementations), so skipping resist for Char-sourced attack damage is a no-op today; that backlog item was downgraded to P3 with a note to route attack damage through `resist()` once class-keyed resistance sources are ported.
- Closed the audit:S29 badge-registry test gap with `test_badges_registry.gd`: the P1 "badge total undercounts by 3" turned out already fixed in `c45362c` (`strength_15`/`first_death`/`death_by_goo` are in `_ALL_BADGE_IDS`, 26 entries), so this slice locks it in with a regression test instead — the test scans `badges.gd` source for every literal `unlock("...")` call site, expands the `enemies_slain_%d`/`gold_collected_%d` threshold templates and `BOSS_BADGE_MAP` values, and asserts the reachable-id set exactly matches `_ALL_BADGE_IDS` (both directions: nothing unlockable is uncounted, nothing counted is phantom), plus no duplicate entries, `get_total_badge_count()` consistency, and a registered name/description per badge (description `""`-fallback is the reliable unregistered-id signal; name fallback `capitalize()` is indistinguishable for ids like `first_victory`). Backlog: S29 undercount marked DONE (stale), S29 no-tests item PARTIAL (catalog serialize round-trips + appearance determinism still open).
- Implemented Warrior Provoked Anger (upstream Talent.PROVOKED_ANGER, T1). Source-verified against current master: upstream attaches a 5-turn `Talent.ProvokedAngerTracker` in `ShieldBuff.processDamage` when a shield buff's shielding hits 0 from absorbing damage, and `Talent.onAttackProc` adds `1 + 2*points` bonus damage (3/5) then detaches the tracker. Port: new `provoked_anger_tracker.gd`; `char.gd` ShieldBuff absorb loop now records pre-absorb shielding and attaches the tracker when a shield buff breaks on a hero with the talent (safe post-`queue_free` read since destruction is deferred); `hero.gd` `attack_proc` applies the flat bonus before enchantment procs, matching upstream ordering (`Talent.onAttackProc` before `wep.proc`). Port adaptation: the legacy flat `shielding` field does not trigger the talent — only ShieldBuff instances, same as upstream. Registered `warrior_provoked_anger` as a real T1 talent (2 points) in `talent_data.gd`. `tests/cases/test_provoked_anger.gd` (9 checks: break attaches, dent doesn't, no-talent doesn't, +1/+2 bonus & consume, no-tracker no-bonus, 5-turn duration) registered in `run_tests.gd`. Local `git diff --check` clean, gdtoolkit parse clean on all touched files (incl. fragile `char.gd`/`hero.gd`), Godot import clean, full headless suite passed (3961+9 checks, 0 failures, local Godot).
- Implemented Warrior Liquid Willpower (upstream Talent.LIQUID_WILLPOWER, T2). Source-verified against current master: `Talent.onPotionUsed` grants `Barrier` shield `round(factor * HT * (0.030 + 0.035*points))` (6.5%/10% of max HP), fired from `Potion.drink` and the thrown-shatter path when not anonymous. Port: new `Hero.on_potion_used()` hooked in `Potion.execute` (drink) and the hero potion-throw branch; Barrier uses upstream max-not-stack setShield semantics via `set_shield(maxi(existing, new))`. Port adaptations: `talentFactor` is always 1 (no exotic potions with 0.5 factor) and there is no anonymous-potion mechanism, so every real potion use triggers. Covered by `test_liquid_willpower.gd` (7 checks: 1/2-point shield values, untalented no-op, larger-barrier kept, smaller-barrier raised not stacked, drink path end-to-end). Warrior upstream gaps remaining: Veteran's Intuition (T1), Iron Stomach, Improvised Projectiles (T2).
- Implemented Warrior Iron Stomach (upstream Talent.IRON_STOMACH, T2). Source-verified against current master: `Talent.onFoodEaten` attaches a `WarriorFoodImmunity` FlavourBuff lasting `hero.cooldown()` (the eating time), `Food.eatingTime()` cuts eating from 3 turns to 1 with the talent, and `Hero.damage` divides incoming damage by 4 at +1 and zeroes it at +2 while the buff is active (applied before tenacity/shielding). Port: new `warrior_food_immunity.gd` (1-turn positive buff), attach hook in `Hero.on_food_eaten`, reduction at the top of `Hero.take_damage` before `super` (so shields aren't consumed by negated damage, matching upstream ordering). Port adaptation: eating in this port always takes 1 flat turn (`use_item` spends `_get_non_movement_action_delay()` — there is no 3-turn eat time), so the eat-faster half is implicitly already true and only the damage-resistance half needed wiring; buff duration is the 1-turn eating window. Covered by `test_iron_stomach.gd` (6 checks: eat attaches, no-talent no-op, 8→2 quartered at +1, negated at +2, full damage without the buff, 1-turn duration) registered in `run_tests.gd`. Full headless suite green locally (3976 checks, 0 failures; required a `--import` first to refresh the global class cache for the new class_name). Warrior upstream gaps remaining: Veteran's Intuition (T1), Improvised Projectiles (T2); note the port also lacks upstream's 3-turn base eating time for all classes (potential future parity slice affecting all meal talents).
- Implemented Warrior Veteran's Intuition (upstream Talent.VETERANS_INTUITION, T1). Source-verified against current master: `Talent.onItemEquipped` identifies armor instantly on equip at +2, `Talent.onTalentUpgraded` retroactively identifies worn armor when reaching +2, and `itemIDSpeedFactor` gives 2x/instant armor and 1.75x/2.5x melee-weapon gradual ID speed. Port adaptations: this port has no gradual usage-based ID system, so +1 becomes a 50% identify-on-equip chance for armor (consistent with the port's chance-based intuition family), and the melee-weapon ID-speed half has no analogue and is not ported. Wiring: identify roll in `Armor.on_equip` (before the curse reveal), retroactive path in `Hero.upgrade_talent`, registered as a real T1 talent (2 points) in `talent_data.gd`. Covered by `test_veterans_intuition.gd` (registry, no-talent no-op, +2 instant, +1 statistical 60-trial band, upgrade-to-+2 retroactive identify) registered in `run_tests.gd`. Full headless suite green locally (3986 checks, 0 failures). Warrior upstream gaps remaining: Improvised Projectiles (T2); the 3-turn base eating time parity slice is still open.
- Implemented Rogue Silent Steps (T2) to upstream parity: a hero with the talent can no longer wake sleeping mobs from distance >= 4 - points (+1: safe from 3+ tiles, +2: safe from any non-adjacent tile), wired as `Mob._silent_steps_blocks` zeroing the sleeping-state detection chance in `_act_sleeping` (upstream Mob.act sleeping block sets detection to infinity); wandering detection is untouched, matching upstream. Also added inert `rogue_wide_search` and `rogue_rogues_foresight` groundwork slots so the Rogue T2 roster matches upstream ordering (`test_silent_steps.gd`).
- Implemented Rogue Light Cloak (upstream Talent.LIGHT_CLOAK, T3) — Rogue talent roster now fully live. Source parity: upstream `CloakOfShadows.cloakRecharge.act()` multiplies charge gain by `0.75 * points / 3` when the cloak is not equipped, so a backpack cloak charges at 25%/50%/75% of equipped speed. Port: new `Hero._light_cloak_recharge()` runs in the hero turn hook right after the equipped artifact's `on_turn`, scanning `belongings.backpack` for a non-cursed `cloak_of_shadows` and calling `_recharge(charge_rate * 0.25 * points)`; equipped cloaks keep the existing `on_turn` path so there is no double charge. Port adaptations: the port's flat `charge_rate` model replaces upstream's missing-charge-based `turnsToCharge` curve (same 25/50/75% ratio applied to the port's rate), and upstream's LockedFloor/RingOfEnergy modifiers don't exist in the port. Covered by `test_light_cloak.gd` (13 checks: registry, no-talent no-charge, +1/+2/+3 first-whole-charge turn counts, charge cap, cursed exclusion, equipped-cloak non-interaction). `test_enhanced_rings.gd` registry probe updated from inert to implemented.
- Closed the audit:S02 class passive/perk copy drift. `Hero._apply_class_buffs()` lost its dead Warrior/Rogue `match` block whose comments promised faster Warrior regen and a Rogue stealth spawn buff — current upstream `HeroClass.initHero()` attaches no class-specific spawn passives, so the function now only adds the universal Regeneration + Hunger buffs. Rewrote the false hero-select copy in `hero_class_data.gd` to match upstream + actual port behavior: Warrior description/perks now cover the broken seal's half-HP shield burst and armor-to-armor transfer (previously claimed extra HP, faster regen, and an unimplemented eat-at-full-HP perk), Rogue copy now covers cloak-of-shadows invisibility and greater-distance trap/secret detection (previously vague stealth/"surprise attack more often" claims), Mage perks now describe the Mage's Staff itself (previously "spell book" and class-wide "wands recharge faster"), and the old-vanilla "identifies X through use" bullets plus the unimplemented Huntress grass-sense bullet were replaced with true perks (Huntress +2 sight and infinite spirit-bow arrows are real port behavior). Copy-only plus dead-code removal; no behavior change, existing suite green (4284 checks).
- Fixed the Huntress talent roster to upstream `Talent.initClassTalents` ordering and implemented Invigorating Meal (upstream Talent.onFoodEaten): Followup Strike and Nature's Aid moved T2 -> T1 (no save migration needed — tier buckets recompute from TalentData tiers, same as the Rogue T1 move), and the upstream T2 row was added: real `huntress_invigorating_meal` (eating attaches Haste for `0.67 + points` turns — effectively 1/2 hastened turns at x3 port Haste speed; port adaptation: same-buff merge keeps the longer remaining duration instead of upstream `Buff.prolong`'s flat reset) plus inert groundwork slots `huntress_liquid_nature` (potion splash grass + roots), `huntress_rejuvenating_steps` (walking sprouts furrowed grass on cooldown), `huntress_heightened_senses` (through-wall enemy sense), and `huntress_durable_projectiles` (thrown-weapon durability, can likely reuse the Warden Durable Tips counter). Covered by `test_invigorating_meal.gd` (registry + inert slots + tier moves + 1/2-point Haste duration + no-talent/wrong-class gating + longer-buff merge). Full headless suite green locally (4300 checks, 0 failures).
- Started the screenshot-driven SPD mobile HUD composition pass: compact toolbar order now reads wait/search -> quickslots/page -> bag, with map/menu moved out of the bottom row into a top-right version/menu cluster; game log and toolbar panel backing are transparent so message text/buttons float over the black dungeon negative space like upstream. `HUD.handle_screen_tap` now treats the top-right cluster as HUD input with deterministic left/right half fallback hit regions for headless/unlaid-out containers. Covered by expanded `test_mobile_hud_input.gd`; full headless suite green locally (4304 checks, 0 failures).
- Closed the last examine/info UX parity gaps (upstream WndInfoTrap/WndInfoPlant + examineCell WndOptions INFO icon). New `WndInfoTrap` shows the trap's procedural `TrapSprite` glyph beside the description (upstream shows the trap tile via WndTitledMessage) and prepends the exact upstream `windows.wndinfotrap.inactive` line with a greyed glyph for triggered traps; new `WndInfoPlant` shows the plant's `PlantSprite` (seed-icon art) beside its description. `CellExaminer.examine_object` now routes plant/trap kinds to the styled windows instead of plain `WndInfoCell` text. `WndExamineChoice` now carries the upstream Icons.INFO title icon (verified `Icons.java` uvRectBySize(16,32,14,14) in the shared interfaces/icons.png atlas), added post-`_ready` so headless construction stays safe. `test_examine_info_windows.gd` (11 checks). [ENGINE]-adjacent: window layout/scaling of the embedded Node2D glyphs needs an in-game look.
- In-engine verification of the restored door system (audit:S08 P1 reconcile): new `test_generated_level_doors.gd` runs full `LevelFactory.create_for_depth` generation (depths 1/2/4/6, seeded) and asserts doors are painted and vault/armory/crystal special rooms are sealed by their gated door terrain; `_place_tunnel_door` now always places gated door terrains (SECRET/LOCKED/CRYSTAL_DOOR), bypassing the cosmetic doorframe/adjacent-door guards that could previously skip a gate entirely. The 2026-07-19 `1e74016` tunnel-mouth door fix plus the string pair-key rewrite had already closed the S08 P1/overflow/dead-code entries — backlog reconciled to DONE. Verification also surfaced two new S08 bugs (filed in backlog): tunnels can breach gated-room walls with extra non-door openings, and secret rooms can generate isolated/out-of-bounds/edge-flush (4/200 seeds); the test therefore only asserts sealing for connected secret rooms until those are fixed.
- Fixed gated special-room wall breaches from tunnel carving (audit:S08 P2, seed-1178 find): `StandardPainter._gated_border_cells()` collects the border ring of every gated room (secret/vault/armory/crystal, i.e. `_tunnel_door_terrain != DOOR`), `_carve_tunnels` passes that set minus the two door endpoints into `Builder.build_tunnel`, and `_carve_h_line`/`_carve_v_line` skip protected cells — an L-leg crossing a gated wall can no longer open an extra EMPTY hole (which `_apply_feeling` was then dressing as grass/water). Prevention (not post-repair) was chosen so door placement and feeling passes stay untouched. Covered by `test_tunnel_gated_breach.gd` (border-set collection, protected-carve unit check, 6-seed diagonal-offset vault paint scenario; verified the test fails 4 checks with protection disabled) and `test_generated_level_doors.gd` now asserts full border integrity for every in-bounds gated room in generated depths 1/2/4/6. Remaining S08: isolated/out-of-bounds/edge-flush secret-room placement (separate backlog item) still open; out-of-bounds rooms are skipped by the border-integrity assertion.
- Extended forced-movement chasm parity (audit:S09 follow-up) to the Repulsion glyph and Elastic enchant: new shared `KnockBack.throw_char` (`src/levels/features/knockback.gd`) mirrors upstream `Char.throwChar` traversal — stops at map edge/solid terrain/other chars, but chasm (AVOID) cells are enterable by forced movement with flying chars sailing over and grounded chars falling in via `Chasm.force_fall` (mobs die, heroes descend through `hero_fell`); no wall-slam damage (Blast Wave-specific, its bespoke path unchanged). Both sites also gained upstream's 2-cell push distance (upstream `throwChar(…, 2)` for RepulsionGlyph and Elastic; port previously pushed 1 cell). Covered by `test_forced_knockback_chasm.gd` (13 checks incl. an `_elastic_proc` end-to-end chasm kill) registered in `run_tests.gd`. Full headless suite green locally (4621 checks, 0 failures; `--import` needed once for the new class_name). Remaining S09 forced-movement gaps: Monk knockback + Gladiator combo (Clobber) not yet routed through the helper, and walk-in chasm entry still lacks the upstream confirm-prompt (chasm remains impassable to voluntary movement).
- Unified the duplicated quest depth gate (audit:S21): `QuestHandler.is_quest_depth` and `spawn_quest_npc` carried parallel depth-range + availability tables that could silently diverge. Both now share one `QUEST_WINDOWS` const table (ghost 2-4/5, wandmaker 7-9/10, blacksmith 12-14/15, imp 17-19/20 — upstream `Random.Int(guaranteed_by - depth) == 0` odds preserved) consumed by `_roll_quest_for_depth`, which `spawn_quest_npc` calls internally; `is_quest_depth` was deleted and `loading_scene.gd` now just calls `spawn_quest_npc` and checks for null. Covered by `test_quest_spawn_gate.gd` (window table shape, guaranteed last-depth spawns per NPC class, off-window depths, active/complete quests blocking respawn, ~1/3 depth-2 sampling, no double spawn per run).
- Implemented the Duelist weapon-ability charge pool foundation (upstream `MeleeWeapon.Charger`): new `WeaponCharger` buff attaches on Duelist init (upstream HeroClass.initHero) and via a load-repair in `Hero.deserialize` for pre-existing Duelist saves; starts at 2 charges, cap `min(8, 2+(lvl-1)/3)` (Champion `min(10, 4+(lvl-1)/3)`), passive gain `1/(60-1.5*(cap-charges))` per turn (x1.5 Champion) gated by the same locked-boss-floor rule as Regeneration, `gain_charge()` clamps at cap, charges/partial serialize additively (no SAVE_VERSION bump — buff serialization is dynamic), hidden from the buff bar and `revive_persists` like upstream. `duelist_weapon_recharging` flipped inert->real in the same slice (upstream numbers: +1/+2 add 1/15 / 1/10 charge per turn while a Recharging buff is active; max points corrected 3->2 to upstream; port adaptation: only the Recharging buff satisfies the gate since the port has no over-time ArtifactRecharge buff, and Brawler's Stance slowdown is skipped — Ring of Force stance not ported). No abilities consume charges yet — the per-weapon ability table (and aggressive_barrier / Champion / Monk talents that hook it) are the follow-up slices. Covered by `test_weapon_charger.gd` (23 checks).
- Replaced the invented alternating `ChampionDualWield` passive with upstream Champion dual-wield semantics (source-verified against current master KindOfWeapon/Belongings/MeleeWeapon): the Champion has no passive buff — the second weapon lives stowed in `Belongings.second_wep` and attacks always use the primary weapon. `champion_dual_wield.gd` is now a legacy save-migration shim: on attach it moves the buff-stored off-hand weapon into `second_wep` (backpack if occupied) and self-detaches, so old saves lose nothing; `SubclassAbilities._apply_champion` no longer attaches it. Equip path shipped: WndItem shows an "Equip 2nd" button for Champions holding an unequipped melee weapon (port adaptation of upstream KindOfWeapon.execute's primary/secondary WndOptions prompt), the `equip_item` hero action honors `slot:"second_wep"` (game_scene, minimal fragile-file edit), unequip detects the off-hand slot, WndInventory shows a Champion-only Off-hand equipment slot (upstream InventoryPane), and `Belongings.get_items()` now includes `second_wep` so scroll effects/uncurse-style sweeps see it. Covered by `test_champion_dual_wield_migration.gd` (no-buff-on-subclass, migration to empty slot, migration to backpack when occupied). Remaining for full Champion parity: the instant swap ActionIndicator, ability use from the off-hand weapon (upstream `Belongings.abilityWeapon`), then twin_upgrades/combined_lethality flip live.
- Champion off-hand ability path (upstream `Belongings.abilityWeapon`/`attackingWeapon()` + `KindOfWeapon.isEquipped` covering `secondWep`): `Belongings.ability_weapon` is a new transient (never serialized) override slot set by `MeleeWeapon.before_ability_used` and cleared by the new `Hero._do_weapon_ability` wrapper once the ability resolves (upstream afterAbilityUsed), and `get_equipped_weapon()` now returns it when set — so damage roll, accuracy factor, attack delay, enchant proc, and surprise-attack checks all route through the off-hand weapon during its ability strike, exactly the sites upstream reads via `attackingWeapon()`. The `_do_weapon_ability` gate now accepts `second_wep` alongside the primary (backpack weapons still refused); the existing WndItem ability button already appears for the off-hand slot since WndInventory opens equipment slots with equipped=true, so no UI change was needed. Regular attacks still always use the primary weapon (upstream Champion semantics). Covered by `test_weapon_ability_offhand.gd` (override routing incl. a +50 off-hand out-rolling the primary's whole damage range, off-hand cleave end-to-end with charge spend + cleared override, backpack refusal). Remaining for full Champion parity: instant swap ActionIndicator, then combined_lethality flips live.
- Champion Combined Lethality live (upstream Talent.COMBINED_LETHALITY, source-verified against current master MeleeWeapon.afterAbilityUsed + Char.attack): new `CombinedLethalityAbilityTracker` (hidden ~cooldown-length buff holding a runtime weapon ref; upstream does not bundle the field either) is armed/re-armed in the `Hero._do_weapon_ability` after-hook — the port's afterAbilityUsed point, gated on `ability_weapon` still being set so refused abilities do nothing — and a different weapon's non-attack ability consumes it (upstream detach branch). The `Char.attack` post-damage block executes a surviving non-boss/non-miniboss hostile at or below `0.4*points/3` of max HP (13/27/40%) when the hero's attacking weapon (incl. the off-hand via `ability_weapon` during ability strikes) differs from the tracker's weapon, and consumes the tracker either way; `get_talent_level` is invoked via `call()` since `Char` has no static binding for it. Talent un-inerted — zero inert talents remain in `talent_data.gd`; `test_talent_inert_gating.gd` now asserts combined_lethality is upgradable. Covered by `test_combined_lethality.gd` (arm/re-arm, differing-hit execute + consume, threshold + boss immunity gates, off-hand ability chain re-arm). Full headless suite green locally (5549 checks, 0 failures). Remaining Champion parity: the instant swap ActionIndicator.
- Scorpio SPD parity + Acidic rare alt (last remaining RARE_ALTS variant). Scorpio rewritten to upstream Scorpio.java: HP 110, attackSkill 36, defenseSkill 24, damage 30-40, drRoll 0-16, EXP 14/maxLvl 27, DEMONIC; ranged-only spike attack (upstream canAttack excludes adjacent — the scorpio retreats while hunting via the getCloser->getFurther override) with a 1/2 Cripple(10) proc; the invented 2-turn sting cooldown, 2-6 range band, poison proc, and 0.9x speed were removed. Loot ported to upstream createLoot: 50% random potion rerolled to never be healing or strength (table-driven loot replaced by a `_drop_loot`/`create_loot` override pair like ArmoredBrute). Port adaptation: the Ballistica PROJECTILE shot gate maps to the port's `can_see` LOS check, consistent with the other ranged mobs. New Acidic (upstream Acidic.java) joins `MobFactory.RARE_ALTS` as the 1/50 scorpio swap: Ooze(20) on every hit plus ooze splash onto adjacent melee attackers via defense_proc, ACIDIC property, guaranteed potion-of-experience drop. Sprites: scorpio.png frame width fixed 18->17 (upstream ScorpioSprite film is 17x17; 18 was stale), acidic = sheet row 1 (upstream frame offset 15), and real ScorpioSprite/AcidicSprite frame maps added (scorpio previously rendered static frame 0). Covered by `test_acidic_scorpio.gd` (rare swap, table absence, stats, never-melees/ranged-hit behavior, cripple/ooze procs incl. the ranged-attacker no-splash gate, loot pool filtering, serialization round-trip) + acidic added to `test_mob_descriptions.gd`. Full headless suite green locally (6841 checks, 0 failures; one `--import` for the new class_name). The upstream rare-alt roster for ported base mobs is now complete — remaining alts (GnollExile, HermitCrab, SpectralNecromancer, ChaosElemental) need their unported base mobs first.
